# CLAUDE.md

This file provides guidance for Claude Code when working with the Pythia codebase. CLAUDE.md must be kept as a comprehensive, up-to-date description of the Pythia system. **Update this file at the end of every session where Claude Code changes the repo** — include any new architectural decisions, environment variables, failure modes, workflow changes, or component behavior that future sessions need to know.

## Project overview

Pythia is an end-to-end AI forecasting system for humanitarian crises. It scans countries for emerging hazards, produces triage signals, generates research briefs, runs LLM ensembles for probabilistic forecasts (SPDs), scores and calibrates them, and serves outputs via a FastAPI API and Next.js dashboard.

## Invariants

Hard-won rules, each learned from a specific production failure. **Read this section before
changing the subsystem it names.** Every rule here is also stated inline in the post-mortem that
produced it — in `## Known failure modes` below, or in
[`docs/archive/claude_md_failure_modes.md`](docs/archive/claude_md_failure_modes.md) for older
entries. When you add a failure-mode entry with a `**Rule:**` line, add the rule here too.

**Batch pipeline and LLM cost**
- Any provider batch must contain exactly one model — group by `(provider, model_id)`, never by provider alone.
- Any key used to replay stored LLM output across process boundaries must be scoped to the execution that created it.
- A batch-pipeline stage must never depend on batch-submission SUCCESS to find its own identity — the sync fallback is only a fallback if the stage can still start.
- A batch that yields nothing must record the provider's terminal state; "collected" with zero results is indistinguishable from success in every downstream view.
- "Lost discount" must be measured against what this pipeline actually batches — a provider is not a unit of batchability, a call family is.
- `POLLER_MAX_CHAIN × POLLER_TICK_MINUTES` must always exceed `PYTHIA_BATCH_MAX_WAIT_H` — never hardcode the cap, and never raise the wait ceiling without re-deriving it.
- Every workflow input that shapes a stage's cost or scope must be carried in the batch-state artifact — the poller, not a human, launches stages 2..N.
- In `pythia_pipeline_stage.yml`, never write a bare truthiness test on a boolean input — compare against `true` and `'true'` (or `!= true && != 'true'`), because the poller, not the UI, launches most stages.
- A provider accepting a batch at creation proves nothing — its asynchronous validator is the acceptance, so a submit must watch the batch leave `validating` and re-upload on a file-access rejection, never persist a batch it has not seen validated.
- A prompt cache can only be read once one call carrying the prefix has finished — when a family runs synchronously, the first call per (model, prefix) must finish before the rest start, or the hit rate is zero by construction.

**Verification and telemetry**
- A cache flag that is on in the workflow proves nothing — only a non-zero `cache_read_input_tokens` does.
- A connector is reconciled by the rows carrying THIS run's write stamp within its OWN source filter, never by a table delta and never per shared table: an idempotent upsert moves no count (NMME merged 2,408 rows into a table that stayed at 4,816 and was flagged), and three sources on one table share one delta (VIEWS, conflictforecast and ACLED CAST all "agreed" against 546 rows on `conflict_forecasts`). `facts_resolved`/`facts_deltas` carry `updated_at`, stamped on every MERGE, because `created_at` never moves for a matched row; every per-country store returns the rows it wrote and the report carries it as `counts.rows_written` beside the country count.
- Freshness is measured from the newest value that has HAPPENED: a future value has a negative age and "fresh" is the wrong word for it, so it is counted (`n_future`) and named, never allowed to set the verdict.
- An empty table is a claim about a writer, and the check that reads it must know the writer: the bundle keeps a registry of the tables this workflow writes (FAIL when empty) and the tables another workflow carries in the same file (reported, with the writer's own reason where it can be re-derived), because "scores is empty" after a Resolver Update is not this run's fault and `calibration_weights` is legitimately empty below twenty resolved questions.
- A table nothing has ever written is dropped when empty (`pythia.db.schema.DEAD_TABLES`, and `meta_runs` in the resolver schema) — a table created on every startup and written by none teaches the empty-table check to be ignored.
- A diagnostic bundle must lead with what failed; enumerations that are useful but long belong in a second file, because a hundred lines of quiet pairs above a batch collapse is how a batch collapse goes unread.
- A collector added to the debug bundle must never fail the phase: a failure writes a stub naming the error, because an absent file and a file with nothing to say are indistinguishable and want different responses.
- A provider batch object is the only account of a validation failure there is, and it expires upstream long before anyone opens an artifact — capture it at collect time, and record a fetch failure's status and body rather than dropping the entry.
- Staleness is reported per EDITION, not only as a latest: two editions four months apart in one table is months that never landed, and a single "latest" field hides it.
- Credentials are fingerprinted, never blanked: `<redacted:sha256:xxxxxxxx>` still answers whether this run's key is the one the last good run used, and a constant mask does not.
- `PRAGMA table_info` yields `(cid, name, ...)` — the NAME is column 1. Reading column 0 gives the ordinal, and every column lookup then silently answers "absent".
- A feature flag that changes REQUESTS must be verified by response telemetry, not by the flag being set.
- Any diagnostic that exists only in a job log does not exist — logs expire, artifacts are the record.
- A diagnostic bundle must be regenerable outside CI and must degrade rather than fail: a missing database, missing CI variables or missing phase logs each cost their own section and are recorded as problems, never raised.
- A bundle assembled for a chat window is scanned for every secret in the build environment before it is written, and a hit FAILS the build — capture-time redaction is the first line, never the guarantee. The leak report names the fingerprint, never the value.
- When a size ceiling binds, code is dropped before evidence and every drop and truncation is recorded in the manifest with the original size — a bundle that silently loses half its content is worse than one that says it had to.
- A connector's response ENVELOPE is evidence the connector itself discards: capture every top-level field, the payload's columns, and the maximum of every date-like column, so a stalled vintage can be told from a spent quota without a bespoke probe.
- A cell that was assessed and produced no row must carry a REASON CODE at the moment the decision is made — deriving "1,215 cells produced no row" by subtracting summary fields is arithmetic, not diagnosis.
- A rejected figure records the ceiling AND the upstream field the ceiling came from: a ceiling of 2 against a reported 40,000 is an enrichment failure, not a mis-transcription, and only the source column says which.
- A ledger column named `unit` holds the unit of the value beside it, never the unit of a different number: record what the source said (`stated_value`, `stated_unit`) and what the machine uses (`value_persons`, `conversion_factor`) as separate columns, and count people in whole persons.
- A parse failure downstream of a billed LLM call must be logged — `llm_calls.status='ok'` means the HTTP call succeeded, not that the output was used.
- A member counts as present only with a complete ok SPD in `forecasts_raw`; any "N/N models" health figure must be computed from what was WRITTEN, never from which calls returned.
- When a response fails to parse, salvage the block that becomes the forecast (`spds`) before discarding the whole — a broken prose field must not cost a paid forecast.
- A per-country timing or grounding view must attribute HS calls by `call_type`, never by `phase` — every HS row carries `phase='hs_triage'`.
- A skip is not a failure; any status roll-up must run over the same list that decided what to actually do.
- Never map `sibyl` to `other`; any new phase must be added to both `phase_group`/`CANONICAL_PHASES` and the frontend `PHASE_ORDER`/`PHASE_LABELS`/`PHASE_COLORS`.

**Test-mode and dashboard correctness**
- An `is_test` parameter must default to None-and-inherit, never to `False` — a `= False` default converts a forgetful caller into silent production data.
- Any query that picks a "latest" run, or joins a test-stampable table (`questions`, `hs_runs`, `hs_triage`, `forecasts_ensemble`, `scores`) for a default dashboard view, must thread `include_test` and filter every table it touches.
- On the dashboard, a soft-fail must never look identical to "no data" — degrade visibly.

**DuckDB connection handling**
- Inside the pipeline, never open a raw read-only `duckdb.connect` on the Pythia DB path — use `pythia.db.schema.connect` (or `duckdb_io.get_db`).
- Any workflow step that opens the travelling DB read-only after a `continue-on-error` read-write step must strip `resolver.duckdb.wal` first and must itself be non-fatal — a crashed best-effort writer leaves a WAL a read-only open refuses to replay.
- Never assume a `duckdb_io` handle survives a call into forecaster/pythia loader code — re-acquire it, don't reopen once and hope. Anything holding a `get_db()` connection across `_load_structured_data` (or the food_security/acaps loaders) must reopen afterwards: those loaders `close_db()` the shared cached handle.
- `TransactionContext Error: Current transaction is aborted` is a statement about the *previous* statement — never convert it into an answer about the object being probed.

**Prompts and model configuration**
- When adding an `sd[...]` inject, grep `prompts.py` for a consumer — loading without rendering fails silently.
- Never hardcode a coverage-era label in a prompt — derive it from the rows.
- Never rebuild a `ModelSpec` field-by-field — use `dataclasses.replace()`, or new params are silently dropped at that seam.

**Connectors and upstream data**
- Never trust an ACLED request-side filter — verify by the returned rows' own country fields.
- Attribute ACLED events with the multi-key + name-fallback resolver, never a single `.get("iso3")`; and test connectors against the REAL response shape (`#country+code`/name-only), not a mocked `iso3` key.
- When a "0/N events resolve" warning appears for a country, check the ACLED name form against `countries.csv`/aliases first.
- Keep `_password_grant`'s params in lockstep with ACLED's documented curl example — the gateway reports missing/malformed params as 415, not 400.
- On a monthly publication, staleness must be measured in EDITIONS, not days.
- When a fallback silently changes WHICH edition of a monthly publication you get, it is not a retry — treat it as data loss and be patient first.
- When copying a fix for a parse-then-format crash, sweep ALL sibling scrapers for the same pattern.
- A monthly-edition inject must state its own age in the prompt once it is more than two editions old — a "(June 2026)" label beside an October forecast is not a warning.
- Where several upstream rows collapse onto one key, MERGE them — a plain assignment is last-write-wins and silently drops whatever the earlier rows knew. CrisisWatch publishes regional, bilateral and territory headings that resolve to a country already present.
- A table has ONE writer and that writer is the guard: `store_crisiswatch_entries` inserts a new key, rewrites a row (and moves `fetched_at`) only when its `content_hash` changed, and leaves an identical row alone — a guard honoured by the JSON file and not by the DB writer is no guard. A same-month republication whose content hash differs is a revision and is written; every parsed entry carries a reason code (`stored`, `merged_into_<ISO3>`, `unresolved_iso3`, `no_edition_month`) so parsed minus stored is always accounted for.
- A source refreshed from a flaky archive needs more than one attempt per cycle — every workflow that holds the canonical DB before the forecast is a chance to land the edition.
- A DR Phase 3+ question is only generated where the DB holds `phase3plus_in_need` rows for the country — the curated monitoring lists say who is watched, not who is published.
- A classification a connector could COMPUTE from a published index must never be scraped from a rendered page — guarding a scrape yields an empty row instead of a wrong one, which is not the same as being right.
- A record must be assembled field by field, each with its own source and outcome; a missing decoration field must never cost the load-bearing one.
- A phase, class or verdict may never be written beside a null measurement, and the absence of a measurement is never a default value — carry the last good record forward under its ORIGINAL observation date with its age stated, and say it is stale.
- Staleness is measured against the OBSERVATION, never the fetch: a record re-written every run keeps its fetch date current while the reading it carries ages.
- A repair is done when the next run leaves correct rows in the table, not when it logs that rows are wrong: prefer rewriting a row in place, prefer writing a correct value over nothing, and where no value can be established DELETE the row — an absent row is honest, a defaulted one is not. Every repair pass is idempotent, runs on every scheduled Resolver Update, logs repaired / deleted / untouched counts, and has a contradiction check that fails when the fault returns.
- A table that holds both a run's readings and a published history must say which is which on every row (`enso_state.row_kind`), and "the state as of today" must never be answered by a historical row.
- A seasonal index is never printed under a weekly label; when only the seasonal table answered, the weekly reading is stored as absent so a run one source short is visibly one source short.
- An age bound on a monthly three-month-mean index must exceed its publication lag (`DB_MAX_OBSERVATION_AGE_DAYS` = 100): a 30-day bound discards every correct reading the index can produce.
- A staleness label must state the actual age — ">45 days old" is true of a 46-day-old forecast and of a nine-month one, and they are not the same claim.
- A hand-maintained per-year URL list is a silent expiry date; generate the candidates and make a year that finds nothing say which URLs it tried.
- A source address is a candidate LIST, not a string — a portal that moves its download route has not lost its data, and a moved route should cost a YAML edit rather than an outage.
- Never let a first-match regex decide an identity a page also quotes for other reasons: anchor it to a phrase naming the thing, then to a plausibility window. BoM pages quote their climatology baseline in the same shape a season is written.
- A document that quotes the version it supersedes must be dated from its own header, and typed from what the SOURCE calls it (its filename) rather than from its prose.
- Two records of the same thing for the same period cannot share an issue date; when they do, one has been misread.
- An HTTP 200 is not an answer: a body that will not parse must raise the same described error a non-200 does, naming the status, the content type, the URL reached AFTER redirects and a redacted body snippet. A WAF page, a moved route, content negotiation and a dead credential are four different repairs behind one `JSONDecodeError`.
- An outbound API request asks for what it wants: `Accept: application/json` and a real User-Agent, or a gateway is free to hand it the website.
- A tier-0 connector that could not READ its source exits NON-ZERO; a deliberate skip and a genuinely empty window exit 0. A bool returned into a `__main__` that discards it is not an exit code.
- An HTML body from ACLED is a named connector failure whatever the status code says, never zero records: the gateway serves its "Unauthorized" page with a 200 even to a request that asked for JSON. Every ACLED response goes through `acled_auth.parse_json_response`, and the bundle fails a run that received a web page while reporting the connector ok.
- One ACLED token per run, shared across processes through a file outside the checkout; and a code path whose normal, expected outcome is an error line (the ~14-day refresh token on a monthly cadence) is deleted or reordered, never left to train the reader to skip errors.
- A stale forecast vintage is flagged on the ROW (`is_stale_vintage`, `vintage_age_days`), re-stamped every write, and a prompt reader filters on the TARGET month, never on `lead_months`, which is computed from a possibly frozen issue date.
- A fallback that would serve older data as current is worse than no data: an empty result stays empty, and any fallback pull must be ordered newest-first and verified by its maximum timestamp before a row is written.
- A country-month or a record that leaves a pipeline silently is the same fault as a figure that vanishes silently: deduplicate before any SUM and log the distinct-key count beside the raw count; name every dropped country-month with its reason; log the target months a vintage actually carries rather than inferring them from a horizon constant.
- A table keyed on the CLOCK is not keyed: `(basin, source, fetched_at)` appended a copy of every seasonal TC outlook on every run, and the duplicates masked the one real misread. Key on the thing (`basin, source, forecast_season, issue_date_key, category`) and upsert; a re-parse with a corrected date supersedes the misread row and says so.
- A date column holds a date or NULL with a reason, never free text: "October 2025" cannot be compared, aged or ordered, and a blank cannot say whether the source stated nothing or the parser missed it (`seasonal_tc.dates.parse_issue_date`; `issue_date_reason`).
- A document's category comes from its own TITLE, never from the month in its URL: the same calendar month names different products in different basins.
- When a document quotes the thing it supersedes, the date that matches the document's own filename month wins, and the file's metadata date is the second witness; a contradiction nothing can settle is stored WITH its flag, never silently.
- A publication date is what the source STATED, or the day we first saw the row; it is never the end of the period the figure describes, and it is never after today. `as_of_date` is a period end and may lie in the future (an IPC projection window, the current month); copying it into `publication_date` says a document was published on a day that has not happened.
- A rejected credential is a failure class of its own (`emdat.FAILURE_AUTH`): it never recovers on a re-run, it is announced with a `::error::` naming the secret, it is recorded on the `source_fetches` run-log stream, and the bundle fails a run that configured a key and read nothing.
- A filter literal must match what the writer writes: `score_views` compared `source = 'views'` against a column holding `'VIEWS'` and scored nothing for a year while logging "nothing to score" and going green. Compare case-insensitively, and pin the connector's literal in the scorer's test.

**Ground truth and base rates**
- A question's base rate must be drawn from the same source class and quantity definition that will resolve it. Precedence may operate WITHIN a definitional class; it must never operate ACROSS classes.
- `in_need` is modelled population exposure (GDACS: hazard footprint × population), never reported impact — it must never enter a PA series, in a base rate or in a resolution.
- When collapsing revisions of one fact, the LATEST informed report wins: source priority, then non-null value, then most-recent publication. `drop_duplicates(keep="first")` after an all-ascending sort keeps the earliest — check the sort direction before trusting it.
- An adapter can only carry what `CANONICAL_COLUMNS` lists (`resolver/transform/adapters/base.py`), and `load_and_derive` drops anything outside its own copy of that list — check both before assuming connector metadata reaches `facts_resolved`.

**PA resolution machine (`resolver/hazard_resolution/`)**
- "The source said nothing happened" and "we could not read the source" are different facts — a connector that blurs them manufactures a zero out of an outage. Every connector returns an outcome, never raises, and an unread rung is recorded as unread.
- A connector's no-raise contract applies per RECORD, not just per request — a malformed upstream record (e.g. a reversed date range) is clamped or skipped with a logged warning and its raw values kept in provenance; it never raises out of the fetch.
- A hazard is DETECTED in every month its date range overlaps, but its figure is attributed WHOLLY to the start month. Never split a figure across months — a source states one number for the event, and apportioning it invents a breakdown nobody reported.
- Absence of a figure before the freeze deadline is PENDING (write nothing), not NO_DATA. EM-DAT lands weeks late; declaring "no data" the week of an event records our impatience as a fact about the world.
- A sanity check flags, it never rewrites. The ladder's answer survives the GDACS ceiling, the population cap and every conflict rule — `conflict_rule: ladder_with_flag` means exactly that.
- A displacement figure is a LOWER BOUND on people affected, labelled at every layer (`value_type`, `rule_fired`, provenance). Losing the label turns a floor into an answer.
- An LLM-extracted figure is only admissible if its quote is FOUND in the document text that was actually sent. A prompt forbidding fabrication is not a mechanism; quote verification is.
- The extractor never converts units and never sums. A `households` figure stays households until `figures.py` converts it with a rulebook multiplier it records — coercing an unknown unit to `people` understates by ~5×.
- The extracted rung is ranked by attributed AUTHORITY then recency, not by size — carried as `Candidate.preference_rank`, which `_best_on_rung` honours over its largest-wins default. Several bodies quoting one flood is not a reason to take the biggest number.
- A budget-capped cell has UNREAD documents, not silence — the cap must be recorded on the row, never allowed to look like "ReliefWeb had nothing".
- The rulebook names an extraction ROLE, never a model id; model ids live in `pythia/config.yaml`'s registry. The rulebook's credential guard only fires on STRING values, so a numeric `*_tokens` budget is config, not a secret.
- IPC states how many people are in Phase 3+, never WHY — so a deterioration is only drought impact where an independent indicator says the country was in drought. Without that gate the machine files conflict-driven food insecurity as drought.
- A covering IPC analysis with no baseline resolves NO_DATA, never its own Phase 3+ population: that figure is a STOCK, and publishing it as people-affected-this-month overstates by the entire pre-existing caseload.
- A drought-indicator feed is cached as VALUES, never verdicts — thresholds are applied at evaluation time, so retuning the rulebook changes behaviour with no re-fetch. A feed where 0 of N records resolve to a country has changed shape; refuse it, never cache "no drought anywhere".
- Under `month_attribution: analysis_window` the same delta repeats in every month the IPC window covers. These are stocks and must NEVER be summed across months; the row says so in its own provenance.
- The backcast runs the SAME functions a live run uses; only `run_type` differs. A backcast-specific detection or ladder path would let a rulebook change move the live answers without moving the base rates they are compared against, and nothing would fail.
- A base-rate denominator counts cells ASSESSED, not cells in the calendar — a country-month with no trigger row was never looked at, and counting it as quiet manufactures a low base rate out of an ingestion gap.
- The acceptance rate divides by cells assessed, never by rows written; a hazard with zero assessed cells is "not assessed", never 0%. An absence of evidence is not a failure to resolve.
- The DFO archive is calibration only: it writes to `haz_raw_dfo` and the report, never to `haz_triggers`/`haz_impact_candidates`/`haz_resolutions`, and its comparison window must not overlap `backcast.flood`.
- A resolution-machine sampling report must be seeded, never `ORDER BY random()` — a page that changes on every run cannot be cited in a review.
- Every rule the base-rate prompt block states is GENERATED from `rulebook.yaml` at prompt-build time, never written into the prompt text. A prompt describing last quarter's thresholds is confidently wrong, which is worse than one describing none.
- The machine's base rates may anchor `PA` questions on its three hazards and nothing else — `PHASE3PLUS_IN_NEED` is a stock where the machine's drought value is the monthly increase in it, and `EVENT_OCCURRENCE` resolves from GDACS alert levels, not from this machine's detection.
- "Assessed and quiet" and "never assessed" get different renderings: occurrence rows with no severity row say "no historical events in record"; no rows at all get NO block. Claiming an empty record for a cell nothing ever looked at states a fact about the world out of an ingestion gap.
- A prompt block over its declared `max_chars` budget is logged and fails a test, never truncated — a base rate cut off mid-table still reads as a complete one.
- An extraction cache entry answers ONE cell's question: the key carries (iso3, hazard, month) because the prompt does, and an error row is never a cache hit — a failed call is retried on the next run, never inherited forever.
- Only calls that billed tokens consume the extraction budget — a call that never reached the provider (missing key, breaker cooldown) spent nothing and must cost nothing.
- Every paid machine call writes a rich `llm_calls` row (`phase='hazard_extraction'` → the `resolution` bucket) — spend that exists only in `haz_doc_extractions` is invisible to every cost view.
- One bad cell must not end the month, and one bad month must not end the run — failures are recorded per cell, surfaced in the exit code, and retried by the next run.
- Severity base rates admit FROZEN values only — a provisional row is still revisable, and a quantile that moves when a live month is revised is not a base rate. The label must move with the calendar: `finalize_frozen_provisionals` (run at the start of every base-rate rebuild) flips `provisional` off rows past their stored deadline, values untouched — without it the oldest month of every live trailing window stayed provisional forever.
- The nightly backcast's extraction spend is a CARVE-OUT of the monthly cap, never an addition (`extraction.backcast_max_calls_per_month` ≤ `max_calls_per_month`; live runs check only the total) — an unsplit cap let the backcast starve every live run's rung 2. Ledger rows with NULL `run_type` count as backcast (all pre-split rows were backcast-created).
- A canonical-DB producer that resumes its own work must include itself in its discovery candidates — otherwise every run silently starts from an older producer's artifact and discards its own progress.
- The attribution gate needs SOME independent physical evidence, never one nominated feed: nominating a required indicator makes that feed a single point of failure for every cell, which is how one dead ASAP URL left 756 drought cells inconclusive. `min_available` asks for evidence without naming which feed supplies it.
- An indicator may never be the quantity the rule differences — admitting IPC Phase 3+ as the drought attribution gate reduces it to "IPC rose, therefore drought, because IPC rose", and files conflict-driven food insecurity as drought.
- An indicator threshold set at a climatological split is not a signal: NMME's -0.43 sigma tercile boundary covers a third of countries in any month by construction.
- A month that assessed cells and wrote nothing is INCOMPLETE, whatever the exit code — recording it complete means the resume ledger never retries it, so a source outage becomes permanently missing history.
- A non-positive GDACS exposure means UNKNOWN, never a ceiling of zero: discovery carries no population figure and the per-event RSS fetch that fills it in tolerates 404s.
- The exposure ceiling multiplier must leave room between a modelled estimate and a reported one — the footprint is a hazard envelope, gridded population is coarse, and "affected" counts people beyond the envelope. It catches a mis-transcription, not a report that exceeds a model.
- An exposure below a plausible national monthly minimum (`sanity.min_plausible_exposure`) is a parse failure and means UNKNOWN exactly as a zero does; a feed's `unit` attribute is parsed and kept verbatim, and an unrecognised unit is never the bare number.
- A corrected rule must reach the rows it would have changed: any change to a rejection rule ships with an idempotent reconsideration pass that runs on every scheduled update, rewrites unfrozen cells and logs frozen ones.
- A document may supply a figure only to the cell it is ABOUT: the document's primary country must be the cell's, and the date the figure is about must fall inside the cell's reporting window. A country filter that matches any listed country is not attribution.
- An event GDACS names no country for is resolved from its own position against the vendored boundaries, never dropped; a stated country is never overridden, and an unplaceable event keeps none.
- A source that could not be fetched but whose cache covers the window is STALE, not unread — an unread rung suppresses answers, a cached one produces them, and only the second is true.
- A budget reserve must be stated outright, never left implicit as "the total minus someone else's share" — raising that share would remove the reserve with nothing to notice.
- An acceptance headline names the hazards it covers: a rate over one hazard's cells, printed without scope, reads as a score for the whole machine.
- A table the machine reads must be LOADED by the workflow that runs the machine — `haz_raw_population` was created by every entry point and populated by none, so the population cap never fired and a cell with no GDACS exposure had no upper bound at all. The acceptance report now counts resolved values by ceiling basis, and `none` is a number a reviewer must see.
- An input the machine reads from the travelling DB must be refreshed BEFORE the machine runs in the same job, never after — the drought gate read last cycle's `hdx_signals` and `seasonal_forecasts` for a month because the steps that refresh them sat below Phase 2.5.
- An answer inferred from ABSENCE is not coverage: an alerting feed answers for every country it does not list, so a country no feed has a reading for is "not looked at", never "quiet" (`drought.indicators.min_present_readings`; reason code `indicator_no_coverage`). And an indicator may never be the quantity the rule differences — the validator refuses a `where` clause that admits `ipc_food_insecurity`.
- When several rows answer for one country-month, the pick is deterministic and the warning wins — an `ORDER BY` on the month alone left the choice to the table's physical order.
- A month still in progress is PENDING for drought whatever the feeds say — drought has no detector coverage gate, and the workflow's trailing window includes the current month, so on 4 September the machine zeroed 159 countries for September.
- A zero on evidence of absence needs `drought.indicators.min_answered_for_zero` (2) feeds to have ANSWERED — one surviving feed of three is not evidence of quiet; below the floor the cell is INCONCLUSIVE. A measured zero (both IPC analyses read, no increase) rests on IPC and is exempt.
- A verdict that writes nothing must RETRACT the unfrozen row it contradicts (`drought.retract_unsupported_row`): PENDING and INCONCLUSIVE used to leave a provisional zero from a weaker rule standing forever. A frozen row is never touched; an unreadable feed retracts nothing.
- Every no-row verdict stamps its reason on the trigger row (`trigger_detail_json.no_row_reason`, via `detect.record_no_row_reason` for the sweep and ladder) — the run-log ledger is off unless `PYTHIA_RUN_LOG_DIR` is set, which the nightly backcast never sets, and 28,728 drought cells read `unexplained_no_row` from the database alone.
- An INCONCLUSIVE cell carries `assessed: false` and is OUTSIDE the occurrence base-rate denominator — counting an undecided drought cell as a quiet year manufactured a near-zero drought rate out of nine years no indicator covered.
- A feed whose records carry dates is a TIME SERIES and is parsed into one snapshot per month, never collapsed onto its newest date — the JRC hotspot archive is the only drought indicator that reaches 2016, and the backcast can only read it month by month.
- A pythia_table lookup with a date offset never pins one lead: every vintage that speaks for the month is a candidate and the shortest lead wins, and a miss describes the table (`describe_pythia_table`) rather than saying "no usable rows".
- A budget message names the limit that BOUND (`ExtractionBudget.binding_limit`): "monthly cap of 3000 reached" when the 2,000 backcast share was what bound sends the reader to the wrong knob.
- A machine step is wrapped in coreutils `timeout` below its step budget and writes its summary after EVERY month — a step-level SIGKILL fires no `::warning`, writes no summary and skips the remaining months, which is a partial month with no annotation.
- A table swap renames the old table aside, never DROPs the live name first, and drops its dependent index inside the same transaction — a kill between DROP and RENAME left the DB with no `haz_raw_reliefweb_docs` and `ensure_haz_schema` then recreated it empty.
- A BACKCAST cell the extraction budget cut short is DEFERRED, never resolved: no row, the reason on the trigger row, the month recorded `deferred` in the resume ledger with the cells it owes, and the next run walks exactly those cells. A live run never defers; it writes what it has with `budget_capped` in the provenance. The cap is not raised to make the month finish — the summary says `budget_policy: checkpoint_and_resume` and which limit bound.
- An extraction answer cut off at the output ceiling is a document READ and LOST, not a model failure: re-read it once at twice the budget, and record `truncated_after_retry` when even that is not enough. The extraction model is Haiku-class with no thinking requested, so `extraction.max_output_tokens` is all answer; `PYTHIA_ANTHROPIC_SPD_MAX_TOKENS` governs the SPD path and nothing here.
- A GDACS event that names no country is placed from its own position in EVERY path that reads the feed — the connector writing `facts_resolved` and the machine writing `haz_raw_gdacs` share one rule (`_iso3s_near_event`, 500 km), because a rule that exists in one path and not the other is a country-month that resolves in one table and vanishes from the other.

**Interpreter report**
- Materiality is TWO tests and they must stay apart: the LEVEL test (probability above a size worth mobilising against) decides "heavy burden", the MOVEMENT test (`max(delta_p50, delta_p90) >= threshold` at the peak horizon) decides "worsening". A level test cannot do both jobs — a chronically large caseload clears an absolute level every month by construction, so any positive excess reads as worsening.
- A gate must be stated in the SAME units as the recommendation it drives, so a reader can check it. A movement in people also cannot be inflated by a near-zero denominator, and it catches a widened tail honestly — which is why the report says which of the two figures moved rather than asserting a rise the planning figure does not support.
- Attention is ordered by expected EXCESS (`excess_nominal`, in people), never by a ratio — a ratio is dominated by whichever base rate sits closest to zero, which is how a cyclone question with 87% of its weight on "nobody affected" led a report whose second half was famine in Sudan. The worsening list is ordered by the MOVEMENT, the heavy-burden list by the burden; two lists, two orderings, and each heading states its own.
- "Worsening" is a claim about DIRECTION. Under the delta key a positive threshold IS the direction test; under the legacy `level` mode it needs an explicit `excess > 0`, without which a forecast expecting two million FEWER people than usual files under "potentially worsening situations".
- `js_vs_baserate` is undirected and may never order the report; it is only the "unusual" key of the gate.
- A thin historical anchor is DEMOTED and labelled, never dropped and never led with — a near-empty record may be an ingestion gap rather than an absence of impact, and the honest answer to not knowing is to rank it lower and say so.
- The report's account of itself — the selection panel, the per-entry gate tag and the appendix question table — is GENERATED from one counts dict, never composed by the model. Three numbers that ought to match and are computed three times will eventually not match, and the reader cannot tell which is right.
- A bucket probability table is a picture of uncertainty and nobody plans against a picture: entries carry p50/p90 at the named peak horizon, and the distributions live in the appendix with their bands named in words. The planning SENTENCE is generated, never written — it is a frame with two numbers and no judgement, and where both quantiles land in the open-ended top band it prints one floor rather than the same figure twice.
- Every figure a reader keeps is rounded to its own scale: the number comes from interpolating inside a bucket tens of thousands wide, so a digit past the third is a precision the scheme does not have. A placeholder carries its unit exactly once — a template that adds one produces "nine and a half million people more people".
- The appendix column that is the EVIDENCE for the gate is populated for every row; a cell that cannot carry a number carries the reason instead. A column of "n/a" makes the transparency table decorative and invites the reader to distrust the rest of it.
- A choropleth of a signed quantity must be diverging and its legend must name the direction — an unsigned scale shaded a country whose forecast had FALLEN exactly like one whose forecast had risen, on the same page as text saying so.
- A base-rate anchor rests on OBSERVATIONS, and a month a live source did not report is an observed zero. A window shorter than the thin-anchor cutoff makes that flag arithmetic rather than evidence; dropping quiet months makes an anchor say displacement happens every month in a country reported on twice a year. Fix the input before touching the cutoff.
- A stock-phrase count is the only check in the suite that can compare two models on one pack; it reports and never fails, because a proxy for writing quality must not stop a report being published.
- The analytical fields (reconciled contradictions, the challenge verdict, the source of a disagreement, the falsifier) are the reason a frontier model does this work. Everything numeric stays in SQL.
- A forecast that clears the worsening threshold is never relabelled "roughly stable" because a cap crowded it out; it is left out of both sections.
- The PACK decides which forecasts the report covers and under which heading; the model copies that decision and the validator holds it to the list. A report that promotes an entry is making a claim on the model's authority, not the system's.
- A question record budget is spent in REPORT order, never attention order — otherwise a row the report must cover loses its record to a question the report never mentions, and any covered row that still loses one is named rather than counted.
- The report prints names, never codes, and figures as sentences: no "% of maximum", no "1/87.5x". Both were shipped and both confused readers.
- A template may only name a `{{fig:...}}` key some producer declares (`packs._ATTENTION_FIG_KEYS` / `_RUN_SUMMARY_KEYS` / `_PERFORMANCE_KEYS`) — an undeclared key renders `[figure unavailable]` and fails the whole report's referential check.
- The style check's code test matches the REAL ISO3 set, never any three-capital run: FAO, IOM and "Phase III" are ordinary humanitarian prose, and a validator that cries wolf gets ignored.
- Which report box a forecast belongs in is the GATE's decision; selection may order and truncate, never re-derive membership. Two policies beside each other will disagree, and the one that ranks by a ratio is the one that leads with a cyclone question carrying 87% of its weight on "nobody affected".
- A decision deadline is DERIVED (peak horizon less the hazard's lead time) and overwritten from the pack; the model writes only the action. An entry with no decision attached is an entry that should not be in the report.
- Nothing is called novel evidence without enough of the main pipeline's own prompt to compare against — with no comparison text every sentence looks new.
- A sector rank gap may only be computed over countries BOTH rankings cover; absent coverage is not a low rank, and treating it as one manufactures disagreement.
- Persistence is counted from the REPORTS that flagged a risk, newest first, stopping at the first that did not. Re-running today's thresholds over an old run answers a different question.
- A run-to-run comparison is made on the CALENDAR months two runs share, never horizon index against horizon index — consecutive windows overlap by five of six months at different horizon numbers.
- A name in prose must appear in the pack's evidence. The guard reports rather than fails (a validator that cries wolf gets switched off) but its violations are quoted back in the correction pass and counted, so a correction that removes an invented name is not discarded as no improvement.
- Below the configured resolved count, a skill claim is DECLINED, not hedged: a printed number beside a caveat is the number the reader keeps. Every printed skill figure carries a seeded paired bootstrap interval; horizons are never pooled and score families never blended.
- A column that is written and never read is a column that was never written — when a producer adds fields, widen the consumer's SELECT in the same change and warn when they are absent.
- The printed map is drawn by `interpreter/mapviz.py` from the same table, preference order and scaling as `/v1/interpreter/attention_map` — the dashboard's choropleth is JavaScript and a PDF cannot run it, so the two must be kept honest by construction, not by hand.

**Release publishing**
- The published DB is built by `scripts/ci/build_release_db.py`, never uploaded raw — it strips the whole `haz_` family and compacts, and its non-zero exit is the guard that stops a publish BEFORE anything is clobbered.
- Never let a `--clobber` upload be the first thing that discovers an asset is unpublishable: validate size before the destructive step, because `gh release upload --clobber` deletes the old asset first.
- Only the API's own consumers belong in the published DB — the canonical ARTIFACT is the system of record, the release is a serving copy.
- The interpreter PDF is published from the newest report that exists, not only from the publish's source run — and never regresses the release, because an artifact expires at 90 days while a release asset does not.
- A publish step that cannot run because it is unconfigured must WARN, not echo: a silent skip on a green run is the same failure as no diagnostic at all.
- A content-hash dedup key must cover CONTENT ONLY: a wall clock inside the hashed payload silently converts an idempotent cache into an append-only log.
- Deleting rows never shrinks a DuckDB file — it reuses freed blocks but never truncates, so only a copy to a fresh database reclaims space. Collapse rows FIRST, then copy, or the copy's source is the un-shrunk file.
- A raw-cache compaction key is `(record_id, iso3, ym, hazard)`, never `record_id` alone — one ReliefWeb document is cached once per cell, and `load_raw_records` filters on the columns before it partitions.
- A size crisis you cannot attribute is one you fix twice: any size guard must report per-table sizes, and any estimate must print its unattributed remainder rather than let the numbers silently not sum.

**CI wiring**
- A file the bundle emits is only guarded if BOTH its test file and the module it tests are named in a workflow's `paths:` — `scripts/dump_pythia_debug_bundle.py` was in none, so the bundle could be changed with nothing running its tests.
- A lint that guards directory A must run when directory A changes — check the path filters of the workflow that owns the test, not just where the test file sits.
- When adding a test under `resolver/tests/` that imports another package, wire it into that package's workflow in the same PR — `test_ci_shell_sanity.py` will fail the PR otherwise. Known blind spot: nothing runs `pythia/tests/` broadly, so 8 of the 9 `pythia/tests/test_api_*.py` files run in no workflow.
- CI runs explicit test-file lists — **a new test file runs in NO workflow until it is named in one.**
- A red publish run does not mean the release wasn't updated — check the release asset timestamp before re-dispatching; and any workflow step that `python -m`-imports repo modules needs a checkout, unlike the inline-heredoc steps around it.
- Only workflows that download or upload the canonical DB belong in the `pythia-resolver-db` concurrency group — a group holds ONE pending run, so an unrelated pending run cancels a pending scheduled producer.
- A `python ... | tee ... || echo WARNING` step without `set -o pipefail` can never take the `||` branch — the pipeline's status is tee's, always 0, and the failure is invisible.
- A `schedule:` tick is best-effort and this repo's are delivered HOURS late (11h41m measured on the 2026-08-28 monthly ingest). Never reason about cron ordering as if the times in the file are the times that happen — and never let a workflow's only signal of life be somebody noticing.
- Every workflow whose silence would cost data must be in `scripts/ci/check_workflow_freshness.py`'s `WATCHED` table, and freshness is measured from the last SUCCESS, never the last run. The watchdog fails LOUD (the gates in `scripts/ci/` fail open; a watchdog that fails open is decorative).
- Nothing in CI may `git push` to `main` — branch protection rejects it (GH006). A workflow that needs to publish state writes it to the canonical DB, which already travels in the artifact.
- Every step AFTER a canonical upload is `continue-on-error`, and every canonical producer whose run can go red after uploading names itself in `include-failed-runs-from` — a diagnostics upload that fails turns a complete ingest red, blocks the `workflow_run` chain (which guards on `conclusion == success`) and orphans the artifact.
- The `pythia-resolver-db` group holds ONE pending run, and crons are delivered hours late: the nightly backcast skips the 27th and 28th so it cannot occupy the slot a queued `compute_resolutions` needs.
- `constraints-ci.txt` pins every runtime package the ingest workflows install — a constraints file that names only two pytest plugins constrains nothing, and pandas 3.0 landed on the monthly ingest first and nowhere else. A direct import (`pyproj`) is declared, never left to a transitive dependency.
- The operational debug bundle is dumped from exactly ONE workflow (`run_sibyl.yml`, the cycle's terminus); a stage whose environment or DB signatures the bundle must describe ships them forward as `pythia-stage-context`, and a bundle collector that reads the job it runs in must never be moved between jobs without its context following it.
- Attribution parsed from a model's reasoning trace is CLAIMED, never measured; any artifact that tabulates it says so where the reader cannot miss it, and `attribution_id`'s recipe is pinned because a downstream bundle will join on it.
- A diagnostics record written by one step is APPENDED to by the next, never truncated — `run_connectors.py` blanked the file the IDMC HELIX step had just written, and the summary reported IDMC as skipped beside a successful pull.
- A bundle query is checked against the schema it runs on: `every_bundle_query_runs_against_this_database` runs every `DB_QUERIES` entry whose tables exist, because a binder error (`MIN(ym)` on a table whose column is `month`) lands as one line in problems.md and a silently absent CSV.
- A bundle's account of the checkout says what the runner checked out: `dirty` counts TRACKED changes only (a run leaves hundreds of untracked outputs), HEAD is compared with `GITHUB_SHA`, and the commit list starts at HEAD with no path filter. A diagnostic the workflow never wrote (`PYTHIA_STEP_TIMINGS_JSON`) is not a diagnostic — the step timings are read from the jobs API with the token the step holds.

## Post-Edit Documentation Requirements

After making any code changes, evaluate whether the following files need updating and update them if so:

- **CLAUDE.md** – Update if you've changed architecture, environment variables, failure modes, component behavior, workflow steps, or database schema. This is the authoritative reference for future Claude Code sessions.
- **README.md** – Update if you've changed setup steps, dependencies, usage instructions, file structure, or how to run the project.
- **docs/fred_overview.md** – This is a plain-English description of the system for non-technical readers. Update it if you've changed what the system does, how it behaves, its inputs/outputs, or its overall logic. Avoid technical jargon; explain changes in terms of what the system now does differently from a user perspective. Assume readers understand forecasting and humanitarian data, but not code.

Do not update these files for trivial changes (e.g. formatting, minor refactors with no behavioral change). Use your judgment.

### How to update CLAUDE.md (so it stays usable)

CLAUDE.md is loaded into every session, so its size is a real cost and its contradictions are
real bugs. Three rules, each learned from what actually went wrong in this file:

1. **When a fix supersedes existing prose, EDIT THAT PROSE.** Appending a new "Known failure
   modes" entry is not a substitute — that is exactly how this file came to assert both the old
   and the new behaviour of the batch poller in three different places, while a reader who
   consulted only the reference section acted on refuted facts.
2. **Every new failure-mode entry's `**Rule:**` line also goes in `## Invariants`.** The rules
   are the durable part; the post-mortem is the evidence for them.
3. **When an entry's subsystem is rewritten, move the entry** to
   `docs/archive/claude_md_failure_modes.md` (verbatim, original order) and leave its rule in
   Invariants. Prefer archiving over deleting — the diagnosis is often still the fastest route
   to a regression.

**Core pipeline:**
```
Resolver facts/base rates -> Horizon Scanner per-hazard pipeline (RC grounding → RC → triage grounding → triage)
  -> Structured data connectors (ReliefWeb, ACAPS, IPC, ACLED political, ENSO, seasonal TC, HDX Signals, ACLED CAST, GDACS, GDACS event history, FEWS NET IPC)
  -> Adversarial checks (RC L1+) -> Forecaster SPD ensemble
  -> Sibyl parallel track (top-10 volatile questions, agentic web research)
  -> DuckDB -> FastAPI API -> Next.js Dashboard + CSV/Excel exports
```

**Post-forecast pipeline:**
```
compute_resolutions (ground truth from Resolver) -> resolutions table
  -> compute_scores (Brier, log loss, CRPS per horizon) -> scores table
  -> compute_calibration (weights per hazard/metric) -> calibration_weights
  -> generate_calibration_advice (per-hazard/metric prompt guidance) -> calibration_advice
```

## Repository layout

```
Pythia/
  horizon_scanner/     # Country/hazard triage via LLM (per-hazard RC + triage, reliefweb)
    enso/              #   ENSO index ladder + computed phase (indices.py); IRI Quick Look for decoration
    seasonal_tc/       #   Seasonal TC forecast scrapers (TSR, NOAA CPC, BoM) + country-basin mapping
    rc_prompts.py      #   Per-hazard RC prompt builders (ACE, DR, FL, TC; an HW builder exists but HW is blocked)
    hs_triage_prompts.py  # Per-hazard triage prompt builders
    rc_grounding_prompts.py  # RC-specific grounding queries
    hs_triage_grounding_prompts.py  # Triage-specific grounding queries
    hdx_signals.py     #   HDX Signals (OCHA automated crisis monitoring) connector
    conflict_forecasts.py  # Unified conflict forecast loader (VIEWS + CF.org + ACLED CAST)
  forecaster/          # LLM ensemble for SPD forecasts (structured data + SPD phases)
  sibyl/               # Parallel deep-research forecasting track (agentic open-web research)
  resolver/            # Fact ingestion, resolution, and ground truth DB
    connectors/        #   Connector protocol + registry (ACLED, IDMC, IFRC Montandon, GDACS, FEWS NET IPC, IPC API) + forecast registry (VIEWS, CF.org, ACLED CAST)
    diagnostics/       #   Run-scoped evidence: redaction, JSONL streams, the shared HTTP recorder
    hazard_resolution/ #   PA resolution machine (rulebook.yaml, haz_* schema, population loader; Phase 1: cyclone detection + zeros — IBTrACS, ReliefWeb silence sweep, vendored NE boundaries; Phase 2: GDACS flood detection + the impact ladder — EM-DAT/IFRC GO/IDMC IDU connectors, deterministic reconciler; Phase 3: ladder rung 2 — ReliefWeb document selection, LLM figure transcription, deterministic post-processing; Phase 4: drought — IPC Phase 3+ delta gated by drought indicators, no ladder and no LLM; Phase 5: backcast + base rates + DFO cross-check + acceptance report; Phase 6: the base-rate block injected into PA forecast prompts)
    ingestion/         #   Source-specific fetch/normalise clients (acled_client, idmc/)
    transform/         #   Adapters, normalisation, source resolution
    tools/             #   Pipeline orchestrator (run_pipeline.py), precedence, deltas, enrichment
    db/                #   DuckDB schema + helpers
  pythia/
    api/               # FastAPI service (50+ endpoints)
    db/                # DuckDB schema definitions + migrations (schema.py is authoritative)
    tools/             # Post-forecast compute scripts (resolutions, scores, calibration, calibration advice)
    web_research/      # Shared retriever backends (Brave [primary], OpenAI, Gemini, Claude). NB: the Exa/Perplexity
                       #   branches in web_research.py are unimplemented stubs returning `backend_unimplemented`
    prediction_markets/  # Prediction market signal retriever (Metaculus, Polymarket, Manifold)
    acaps.py           # ACAPS unified connector (INFORM, Risk Radar, Daily Monitoring, Access)
    acled_political.py # ACLED event-level political data connector
    adversarial_check.py # Counter-evidence checks for RC Level 1+ cases
    food_security.py   # Unified food security loader (FEWS NET primary, IPC API fallback)
    fewsnet_food_security.py  # Backward-compat shim → food_security.py
    market_snapshot.py # Manifold prediction market snapshot utility
    tests/             # Pythia-specific tests
  interpreter/         # Plain-language report generator (Opus 5 explains, never calculates; Phases 0-6 built and fully wired — see docs/interpreter.md)
  web/                 # Next.js 14 dashboard (TypeScript, Tailwind)
  tests/               # Cross-module integration tests
  docs/                # Component documentation
  scripts/             # Operational scripts
  .github/workflows/   # CI + scheduled pipelines
```

## Key files

- `pythia/db/schema.py` — Authoritative Pythia DuckDB schema (tables, migrations, bucket defs)
- `resolver/db/schema.sql` — Resolver DuckDB schema (facts_resolved, facts_deltas, snapshots)
- `pythia/buckets.py` — SPD bucket definitions (PA, FATALITIES, PHASE3PLUS_IN_NEED) and the **`NUM_HORIZONS = 6` forecast-window constant** (single-sourced; imported by `compute_resolutions.py` and by `forecaster/cli.py`'s month-label window checks — do not redefine locally). `BUCKET_SPECS` maps metric names to `BucketSpec` tuples. **Every SPD metric leads with a dedicated "0" bucket** (exactly zero impact, centroid 0, range [0,1)) so the next bucket starts at 1. PA has 6 buckets (0, 1-<10k, 10k-<50k, 50k-<250k, 250k-<500k, >=500k), FATALITIES has 7 (0, 1-<5, 5-<25, 25-<100, 100-<500, 500-<1000, >=1000), DR_PHASE3_BUCKETS has 6 for IPC Phase 3+ population (0, 1-<100k, 100k-<1M, 1M-<5M, 5M-<15M, >=15M). Helpers: `n_buckets_for`, `max_bucket_count` (=7, drives the wide SPD_1..SPD_7 CSV export), `centroids_for`, `label_index_map(lowercase=...)`, and `bucket_schema_version(metric)` (12-hex digest over the ordered bucket BOUNDARIES; stamps `bucket_centroids.schema_version` so EMA centroids learned under an old boundary layout are refused by `compute_scores._load_centroids` — NULL rows grandfathered; retuning centroid seeds/labels does NOT change the version, changing any boundary does) alongside the existing `thresholds_for`/`interior_thresholds_for`/`labels_for`.
- `pythia/config.yaml` — Central configuration (LLM profiles, hazards, bucket specs)
- `pythia/llm_batch.py` — Provider Batch-API execution layer (OpenAI/Anthropic/Gemini adapters, enqueue/submit/poll/collect/replay; see the Batch-API staged pipeline section)
- `horizon_scanner/hs_batch.py` — Staged-HS stage state + batch seam (family modes, enqueue/replay, grounding-pack reload, hs_stage_state carry, run-id/pipeline resolution)
- `scripts/ci/emit_batch_state.py` / `scripts/ci/poll_llm_batches.py` — Batch-state artifact emitter (submit stages; also records the stage's effective run-shaping settings as `dispatch_inputs`) + the 15-min poller that dispatches the next pipeline stage (and re-supplies those inputs, so smoke-run scoping survives the hand-off)
- `scripts/compare_prompt_order_runs.py` — A/B harness for the V3 prompt-order validation gate (test-mode run vs baseline: SPD JS-divergence, parse failures, RC/triage distributions)
- `horizon_scanner/horizon_scanner.py` — HS main entrypoint (~1700 LOC)
- `horizon_scanner/regime_change.py` — RC scoring (score = likelihood x magnitude, 4 levels)
- `horizon_scanner/regime_change_llm.py` — Per-hazard RC LLM pipeline (single pass by default since July 2026; `PYTHIA_HS_RC_PASSES=2` restores 2-pass, pair with a distinct `PYTHIA_RC_MODEL_PASS2` — two identical passes at temperature 0 are deterministic duplicates and the mean merge is a no-op)
- `horizon_scanner/triage.py` — Per-hazard triage LLM pipeline (single pass by default since July 2026, `PYTHIA_HS_TRIAGE_PASSES=2` restores 2-pass; gemini-3.5-flash, ACLED low-activity filter, RC-promoted skip for L1+ hazards)
- `horizon_scanner/rc_prompts.py` — Per-hazard RC prompt builders (ACE, DR, FL, TC with calibration anchors; HW builder retained as dead code)
- `horizon_scanner/hs_triage_prompts.py` — Per-hazard triage prompt builders (scoring anchors, RC context injection)
- `horizon_scanner/rc_grounding_prompts.py` — RC-specific grounding queries (TRIGGER/DAMPENER/BASELINE signals)
- `horizon_scanner/hs_triage_grounding_prompts.py` — Triage-specific grounding queries (SITUATION/RESPONSE/FORECAST/VULNERABILITY)
- `horizon_scanner/enso/indices.py` — **The numeric ENSO record and the computed phase (Sept 2026).** Three ranked sources, **every one read on every run** since 2026-09-04 (the ladder used to stop at the first answer, so the record could never say whether a second source agreed) — NOAA ERDDAP `ncepNinoSSTwk` (weekly Niño 3.4 anomaly; the request asks for EVERY column and `erddap_anomaly_column` finds the anomaly by name pattern, because the projected form `?time,NINO3_4,ANOM3_4` drew HTTP 400 from the live server and the live spelling could not be verified from the sandbox), CPC `wksst9120.for` (the 1991-2020 base-period weekly file; `wksst8110.for` froze at 2021-01-27 when CPC changed base period and was 2,046 days stale on 2026-09-04), CPC `oni.ascii.txt` (the published three-month ONI) — each parsed to dated observations, range-checked to ±4 °C (anything outside is a parse failure, never a reading), and refused when the newest observation is over `MAX_OBSERVATION_AGE_DAYS` (120) old. The record is taken from the lowest-ranked usable reading; `IndexResolution.nino34_weekly` is filled ONLY from a weekly rank, so a seasonal-only run stores the weekly reading as absent rather than printing the ONI under a weekly label. `classify_oni` applies NOAA's operational definition (El Niño >= +0.5, La Niña <= -0.5, Neutral between) with a strength band (weak/moderate/strong/very strong), and returns `("", "")` for None — there is no phase without a number. A seasonal source IS the ONI; a weekly one is averaged over 90 days and labelled `weekly_3month_mean` rather than passed off as the published index. `corroborate()` answers from the ranks already read this run and fetches only when a resolution was assembled some other way. Every function is pure apart from the injectable `GetFn` seam; nothing here parses HTML or touches a database.
- `horizon_scanner/enso/enso_module.py` — ENSO record assembly, validation and persistence. The phase is COMPUTED (see `indices.py`) and never read: `extract_enso` still parses the IRI Quick Look for the probability table, plume, IOD and narrative, but its statement of the phase lands in `scraped_state`, is compared against the computed one, and always loses. `build_enso_record` assembles field by field, so an unreachable IRI page costs only those fields. `validation_problems` refuses a write where a phase is stated with no valid Niño 3.4 or no ONI. `fetch_and_store_enso` writes a row every run: `fresh` when a number resolved and the move is ordinary, `carried_forward` otherwise — the last good record re-written under its ORIGINAL observation date with `age_days` stated, so the prompt block opens with a STALE READING line. Neutral is never a default. **Two passes run at the end of every `fetch_and_store_enso`, whatever the run's own outcome (2026-09-04)**: `classify_row_kinds` stamps `row_kind` on pre-column rows (a backfilled ONI row is recognisable by its own signature — `oni_table` basis, `cpc_oni_ascii` source, `fetch_date = observation_date`, age 0 — and gets `status='historical'`; everything else is `live`), and `repair_unbacked_rows` rebuilds every row stating a phase beside a null ONI or Niño 3.4 from the newest ONI observation dated at or before the row (sourced from the backfilled `oni_table` rows already in the table, so it needs no network), recomputing phase and strength, moving the original word into `scraped_phase`, appending the repair to `warnings_json`, and DELETING the row when no observation predates it; it logs repaired / deleted / untouched counts. `backfill_oni_history` writes `row_kind='historical'` / `status='historical'` rows worded "ENSO state for YYYY-MM" and never overwrites a live row on the same date. `load_enso_state_from_db` reads live/repaired rows only and admits observations up to `DB_MAX_OBSERVATION_AGE_DAYS` (100, not 30: the ONI is a three-month mean published monthly, structurally 30-70 days behind, and at 30 the seasonal TC module discarded a strong El Niño as "65 days old"). CLI: `--backfill-oni` (the whole published table, 1950 to present; run every cycle by resolver_update Phase 4, idempotent), `--recompute YYYY-MM-DD` (reclassify one stored row from the ONI table, taking the newest observation at or BEFORE the row's date — never hindsight), `--consumers YYYY-MM-DD` (which hs_runs and hs_triage rows read the record stored on a date).
- `horizon_scanner/seasonal_tc/seasonal_tc_runner.py` — Seasonal TC forecast orchestrator (TSR + NOAA CPC + BoM + Météo-France La Réunion SWI + IMD/NIO climatology)
- **Seasonal TC parser repairs (Sept 2026)**, all from the 2026-08 run. `bom_scraper.extract_season` anchors to a SEASON PHRASE first and falls back to the latest year-pair plausible for today: BoM quotes its climatology baseline as "since 1980-81", in the same shape a season is written, and a first-match regex filed both the Australian Region and South Pacific outlooks under season 1980-81. A page carrying only a baseline now yields nothing, which is better than a season nobody forecast. `tsr_seasonal_extractor.extract_issue_date` reads the header's own `Issued:` line and breaks ties on the LATEST date — a TSR update quotes the forecast it supersedes, and the August Atlantic update was stored under the May issue date with the May figures, colliding with the real May forecast on the `(source, basin, forecast_type)` dedup key and replacing it. `extract_forecast_type` now prefers the discovery URL's `issue_month` (TSR names its own product in its filename, and a filename is not prose), and `_warn_on_colliding_issue_dates` reports two outlooks for one basin and season sharing a date. `noaa_cpc_scraper.candidate_urls` generates per-year candidates across the three activity wordings NOAA titles its releases with, because `KNOWN_URLS` was hand-maintained and stopped at 2025 — every 2026 run asked for nothing, got nothing, and reported success; a year that finds nothing now says which URLs it tried, and the dispatch matches basins by `startswith` (a generated key is `CP_generated_0`, and an equality test on `"CP"` dropped all of them). Tests: `horizon_scanner/seasonal_tc/tests/test_seasonal_tc_parsers.py`. **Group F (2026-09-04) superseded two of those rules**: the header-first date search still returned May on the real August PDF (the quote sits inside the 1,500-character header), so `resolve_issue_date` now prefers the `Issued:` line whose MONTH matches the filename's, uses the PDF's own CreationDate (`extract_pdf_document_date`) to settle a line that contradicts the filename, and flags an unsettled contradiction on the row (`issued_line_disagrees_with_url_month`) rather than storing it silently; and the URL month no longer decides the category — `resolve_forecast_type` reads the document's TITLE ("Extended Range Forecast", "Pre-Season Forecast", "August Forecast Update"), because "May" is pre-season for the Atlantic and the extended-range product for the NW Pacific, and the URL rule relabelled the same NWP outlook between runs. `horizon_scanner/seasonal_tc/dates.py` is the one parser every scraper's `issue_date` goes through (`parse_issue_date` → ISO or NULL with a reason; a month alone becomes the first of the month with `month_precision_only`); BoM reads its page's own `Issued` line and no longer invents "October {year}", the SWI scraper reads the article's "publié le" line, and the NIO block states `climatology_context_has_no_issue_date`. NOAA's 2026 releases are curated in `KNOWN_URLS[2026]` and the fetch sends a browser User-Agent (the generated candidate existed; "0 reachable" was the bot UA being refused). Tests: `horizon_scanner/seasonal_tc/tests/test_group_f_outlooks.py`.
- `horizon_scanner/seasonal_tc/__init__.py` — Country-to-basin mapping + cached TC context reader
- `horizon_scanner/hdx_signals.py` — HDX Signals connector (OCHA automated crisis monitoring, indicator-to-hazard mapping)
- `horizon_scanner/conflict_forecasts.py` — Unified conflict forecast loader (VIEWS + conflictforecast.org + ACLED CAST). Since Sept 2026 `age_note()` renders the forecast's ACTUAL age in the prompt instead of a fixed "[WARNING: DATA >45 DAYS OLD]" — that label is true of a 46-day-old forecast and of a nine-month one, and ACLED CAST's December 2025 vintage understated the problem by a factor of six. At two months or more it says how many and adds "treat it as a historical prior, not a current outlook". The `*_stale` booleans remain in the dict for other callers, but the prompt computes from the issue date so the label and the date beside it cannot disagree
- `forecaster/cli.py` — Forecaster main runner (the largest module in the repo, after the July 2026 decomposition). **Decomposed (pure code motion, zero behavior change)** into three sibling modules, with cli.py re-importing every moved name as a backward-compat re-export (`forecaster.cli.X` and `from forecaster.cli import X` still work; the mutable `_CALIB_WEIGHTS_CACHE`/`_CALIB_WEIGHTS_LOCK` keep object identity): `forecaster/month_utils.py` (month/window arithmetic: `_anchor_month_for_question`, `_month_index_for_label`, `_expected_months`, `_parse_month_key`, `_add_months`, `_sanitize_month_series`, etc.), `forecaster/history_loaders.py` (IFRC/IDMC/ACLED PA history loaders, `_load_idmc_conflict_flow_history_summary`, `_build_gdacs_event_history`, `_format_base_rate_for_prompt`, `_load_country_names`, `NATURAL_HAZARD_CODES`/`IDMC_HZ_MAP`), and `forecaster/aggregators.py` (calibration-weight loading/caching + `_pythia_db_url_from_config`/`_pythia_db_path_from_config`). **Intentionally NOT moved** because tests monkeypatch cli-module attributes that the callees resolve at call time (moving them would silently break the patches): the DB writers `_write_spd_outputs`/`_write_binary_outputs`/`_write_spd_members_v2_to_db` (tests patch `forecaster.cli.connect`; writers also read the module-global `_IS_TEST`), `_build_history_summary` + `_build_natural_hazard_seasonal_profile` + `_build_conflict_base_rate` (patch `cli.connect`), `_load_fewsnet_phase3_history` (patch `cli.duckdb` + `cli._pythia_db_path_from_config`), `_resolve_member_weights` (patch `cli._load_calibration_weights_db`), and `_build_bayesmc_spd_obj` (patch `cli.aggregate_spd_v2_bayesmc`) — do not move these without reworking those test seams. **Seam caveat for the MOVED loaders**: the three history loaders now in `history_loaders.py` resolve `duckdb`/`_pythia_db_path_from_config` in the `forecaster.history_loaders` namespace — a test written by analogy to `test_fewsnet_history.py` (which patches `forecaster.cli.*` and works only because `_load_fewsnet_phase3_history` stayed in cli) must patch `forecaster.history_loaders.*` instead, or it silently hits the real DB. `_forecast_month_keys_from_question` in `prompts.py` is a thin wrapper over `month_utils._anchor_month_for_question` + `_expected_months` (July 2026 follow-up audit; sibyl already used the same pair — one implementation of the month-anchoring invariant). Supports both SPD (per-metric bucket count × 6-month; 6 buckets for PA/PHASE3PLUS_IN_NEED, 7 for FATALITIES) and binary (per-month probability) forecast pipelines. Binary questions (metric=EVENT_OCCURRENCE) are detected and routed to `_run_binary_forecast_for_question()`, which uses `build_binary_event_prompt()` for prompting and `parse_binary_response()` for output parsing. Binary storage convention: bucket_1 = P(yes), bucket_2 = P(no) = 1-P(yes), remaining buckets = 0 (EVENT_OCCURRENCE is not in BUCKET_SPECS; the binary writer is independent of the SPD bucket schemes). History builders: `_build_history_summary` dispatches to `_load_fewsnet_phase3_history` for DR/PHASE3PLUS_IN_NEED (null-aware, coverage-tracked), `_build_natural_hazard_seasonal_profile` for natural hazard PA, `_build_conflict_base_rate` for **both** ACE metrics (PA and FATALITIES — see below), and `_build_gdacs_event_history` for FL/DR/TC event occurrence (binary 1/0 + alertlevel from facts_resolved). `_format_base_rate_for_prompt` handles `fewsnet_phase3` type with null-aware formatting showing "null" for FEWS NET analysis cycle gaps, and takes an optional `metric` that only affects `conflict_trajectory`: it leads with the series the question actually resolves against (PA → IDMC displacement, FATALITIES → ACLED) and marks it "THIS QUESTION'S SERIES"; **omitting `metric` renders byte-identically to the pre-July-2026 output**, so any caller that doesn't pass it is unchanged. **ACE/PA used to route to `_load_idmc_conflict_flow_history_summary`, whose dict carries no `type` — so the formatter fell through to its legacy ```json dump and ACE/PA was the one hazard/metric with no curated base-rate block; that loader also never excluded the current partial month.** It is retained only as a `forecaster.cli` re-export and is no longer on any prompt path. GDACS event history is loaded in `_load_structured_data()` for FL/DR/TC hazards and injected into both SPD prompts (via `_format_gdacs_event_history_for_prompt` in prompts.py) and binary prompts (via `gdacs_event_history` parameter on `build_binary_event_prompt`).
- `pythia/api/app.py` — FastAPI application assembly (thin, after the July 2026 decomposition). **The former ~5,450-line monolith was split (pure code motion)**: shared infrastructure lives in `pythia/api/core.py` (the cached read-connection singleton `_READ_CON`/`_ensure_read_connection`/`_maybe_refresh_db`/`_con`, `_execute`, `_test_filter`, table/bucket helpers, `_run_filter_cte`, `_validate_iso3_param`, heavy-request semaphore, `_stream_csv`), and endpoints live in route-group modules under `pythia/api/routes/` (`questions.py`, `performance.py` [calibration+performance], `forecasts.py`, `risk_index.py`, `diagnostics.py` [diagnostics + HS debug], `resolver_explorer.py`, `downloads.py`, `costs.py`, `sibyl.py`, `interpreter.py` [interpreter reports + attention map, Phase 5 Aug 2026]), each exposing `router = APIRouter()`. app.py keeps app creation/CORS, the startup hook, `include_router` calls (original registration order preserved), and the routes entangled with connection state or test patch seams (`/v1/admin/force_sync`, `/v1/health`, `/v1/version`, `/v1/run`, `/v1/ui_runs/{id}`, `/v1/countries`). `/v1/run` is gated by `PYTHIA_ALLOW_INPROCESS_RUN` (default off → 503) and imports `pythia.pipeline.run` lazily inside the handler — the module-scope import would pull the full HS tree into the API process. **Back-compat**: app.py re-exports every moved name, and forwards the rebindable state names (`_READ_CON`, `_READ_CON_MTIME`, `_LAST_SYNC_CHECK`, `_POPULATION_BY_ISO3`, `_COUNTRY_NAME_BY_ISO3`) to core via module-class properties — so legacy seams like `pythia.api.app._READ_CON = None` (used by several tests) still reset the real singleton in core. Route modules must never import app.py (core's `_run_filter_cte` resolves `_table_has_columns` late through `pythia.api.app` when loaded, keeping the monkeypatch seam in `pythia/tests/test_api_input_validation.py` working). **Connection retry-once resilience**: `core._execute` catches connection-level DuckDB errors only (`duckdb.ConnectionException`, `duckdb.FatalException`, or generic errors whose message signals an invalidated/closed connection — never CatalogException/BinderException/etc.), closes the cached connection via `_reopen_read_connection()`, reopens from the same DB path, and retries exactly once (tests: `pythia/tests/test_api_connection_retry.py`). Phase 4 additions: `_get_risk_index_binary()` serves EVENT_OCCURRENCE probabilities (bucket_1 = P(event)), `/v1/diagnostics/resolution_rates` endpoint returns resolution rate by (hazard, metric). Risk index supports EVENT_OCCURRENCE (binary), PHASE3PLUS_IN_NEED (SPD), PA, and FATALITIES metrics. KPI scopes accept all four metric scopes. **Resolver accordion endpoints**: `/v1/resolver/source_inventory` returns per-source metadata (label, category, last_updated, row counts); `/v1/resolver/source_data` returns rows for a specific source with curated or all columns. Source registry (`_SOURCE_REGISTRY`) maps ~23 source keys to tables, filters, curated columns. `facts_resolved` sources filtered by `publisher` column (IFRC, IDMC, ACLED, GDACS / JRC, FEWS NET). Last-updated dates use ingestion-time columns (`created_at`, `fetched_at`) not data-period columns. **`/v1/version`** computes staleness fields from the synced DB (cached per `(DB-mtime, include_test)` via `_staleness_probes()` — the probes are full-column MAX scans and the dashboard homepage calls this endpoint on every page view; the cache invalidates when the sync layer's `os.replace` advances the mtime): `latest_forecast_month`/`latest_forecast_run_id` plus `latest_scores_at`, `latest_calibration_at`, and `latest_data_at` (= `MAX(created_at)` across `latest_hs_created_at`/`forecasts_ensemble`/`resolutions`/`scores`/`calibration_weights`/`calibration_advice`, excluding the manifest's publish-time `created_utc`). **Test-aware since 2026-07-15**: the endpoint accepts `include_test` (default false → probes exclude `is_test` rows, and a dedicated hs_runs probe overrides the manifest's unfiltered `latest_hs_run_id`/`latest_hs_created_at` with the latest production run; the homepage passes the Test toggle through). The dashboard homepage "Last updated" renders `latest_data_at` so the scoring/calibration loop counts as a data refresh even when it creates no new HS run; the HS-run date is shown separately as "Latest forecast scan".
- `web/src/app/questions/[questionId]/BinaryPanel.tsx` — Binary event forecast display: per-month probability bars, resolution outcomes (event/no event), per-horizon Brier scores.
- `pythia/tools/compute_resolutions.py` — Resolves forecasts against Resolver ground truth. Supports PA, FATALITIES, EVENT_OCCURRENCE, and PHASE3PLUS_IN_NEED metrics. Source-aware null handling: FATALITIES+ACE/ACO and EVENT_OCCURRENCE default to 0 when no data; PA and PHASE3PLUS_IN_NEED leave horizons unresolved (null) when no data exists. FEWS NET IPC resolution via `_try_fewsnet_ipc()` queries only `phase3plus_in_need` metric (not projections), guarded to DR hazard only.
- `pythia/tools/compute_bucket_centroids.py` — Computes data-driven bucket centroids from historical facts_resolved. Metric-aware SQL filter: PHASE3PLUS_IN_NEED queries `phase3plus_in_need`, PA queries `affected/people_affected/pa/displaced`, FATALITIES queries `fatalities`.
- `pythia/tools/compute_scores.py` — Brier/log/CRPS scoring per horizon
- `pythia/tools/score_views.py` — **External benchmark scoring (undocumented until 2026-08-03).** Converts the ingested VIEWS point forecasts into synthetic FATALITIES-bucket SPDs (bucket count derived from `pythia/buckets.py`) and scores them with the same Brier/log/CRPS functions as `compute_scores.py`, writing rows under `model_name='__ext_views'`. Runs every cycle in `compute_scores.yml` (right after compute_scores) and surfaces on the Performance page as "ViEWS (external benchmark)" via the `__ext_` prefix convention in `web/src/app/performance/PerformancePanel.tsx`; `generate_calibration_advice.py` also reads it. `AGGREGATE_MODEL_NAMES`-style exclusion is NOT what keeps it out of the calibration softmax — it is excluded because weights are computed over ensemble members only. The `__ext_` prefix is the extension point for any future external benchmark.
- `pythia/tools/base_rate_spd.py` — **Base-rate anchor as a bucket distribution (interpreter Phase 1, Aug 2026).** `base_rate_spd(con, iso3, hazard_code, metric, as_of) -> (probs, source, detail)` reconstructs the base rate the forecaster was shown at prompt time as a probability distribution over the metric's buckets, routing by (hazard, metric) exactly as `forecaster/cli.py::_build_history_summary` does (ACE/FATALITIES → `acled_monthly_fatalities`; ACE/PA → IDMC flow rows in `facts_deltas`; DR/PHASE3PLUS_IN_NEED → `phase3plus_in_need` 36m; FL/TC PA → GDACS occurrence × reported-PA severity mixture; FL/DR/TC EVENT_OCCURRENCE → binary `[p, 1-p]` with per-month rates in `detail.probs_by_month`).
