Only history STRICTLY BEFORE `as_of` (the question's window_start month) is used — no outcome leakage — and empirical counts get a Jeffreys 0.5 pseudo-count so no bucket is ever exactly zero. **v4 fixed two defects in the conflict anchors**: `CONFLICT_WINDOW_MONTHS` moved 6 -> 36 (a six-month window against a twelve-observation thin cutoff made that flag arithmetic rather than evidence), and `COUNT_QUIET_MONTHS_AS_ZERO` counts a month a live source did not report for a country as an observed zero — behind the same two gates `source_coverage` applies (the month must be one the source was live for globally, the country must appear in the source at all), because without both an ingestion gap becomes a run of quiet months. Built from present rows alone the IDMC anchor said displacement happens every month in a country reported on twice a year. Pairs with no prompt-time base rate (CU/PA, DI/PA, unknown) return `([], "NONE", reason)`; **callers must treat that as "no anchor", never invent one**. Severity uses `_pa_metric_in_clause` semantics (never `in_need` — the montandon class of bug). Tests: `pythia/tests/test_base_rate_spd.py`.
- `pythia/tools/compute_deviation.py` — Writes `forecast_deviation`: one row per (run, question, aggregate model ∈ {ensemble_mean_v2, ensemble_bayesmc_v2, track2_flash, sibyl}) with `js_vs_baserate` (JSD, reuses `_js_divergence` from generate_calibration_advice — the repo's single implementation), `log_ev_ratio` (signed, `ln(EV_f/EV_b)`), `eiv_nominal` (surge blend `max + 0.1*(sum−max)`, matching `/v1/risk_index`'s alpha; `centroids_for` seeds), `eiv_per_100k` (population from `resolver/data/population.csv`), `baserate_source`/`baserate_json` provenance, `score_family` (binary rows have NULL eiv fields), and — since v4 — `baserate_p50_peak`/`baserate_p90_peak`/`delta_p50`/`delta_p90`/`movement_threshold`, the movement key the worsening gate tests (both sides use the same in-bucket quantile interpolation, so the pair is one summary applied to two distributions; binary questions carry their movement in probability points on `delta_p50` and have no contingency figure). A question with no base-rate anchor gets NO row. Idempotent per run (delete-then-insert). Runs `continue-on-error` in `pythia_pipeline_stage.yml` fc_collect_finalize (after forecaster collect, scoped to `FORECASTER_RUN_ID`) and in `run_sibyl.yml` after Sibyl (unscoped recompute, which also covers the legacy sync pipeline that Sibyl chains off). Tests: `pythia/tests/test_compute_deviation.py`.
- `pythia/tools/score_baselines.py` — Reference forecasters `__ext_climatology` (the `base_rate_spd` anchor — "what you would have said with no model") and `__ext_uniform` (flat; 0.5 for binary), modeled on `score_views.py`: same scoring functions, `run_id IS NULL`, delete-then-insert, `is_test` inherited from the question, audit table `baseline_scored_forecasts`. Binary questions get binary Brier only — score families never blended. Skill (computed downstream, never stored) = `1 − fred_score/climatology_score` per (score_family, score_type). Runs in `compute_scores.yml` right after `score_views` (`continue-on-error`). Persistence is deliberately NOT implemented (one-hot → infinite log loss; smoothing is an arbitrary-parameter argument). Both sentinels are excluded from the calibration softmax by the existing `NOT LIKE '__ext_%'` filter and render generically as "climatology (external)"/"uniform (external)" on the Performance page. Tests: `pythia/tests/test_score_baselines.py`. **All three Phase-1 test files are named explicitly in forecaster-ci's "interpreter Phase 1" step** (nothing runs `pythia/tests/` broadly).
- `interpreter/run.py` — **Interpreter runner (Phase 3, Aug 2026; wired into production by Phase 6 — `run_sibyl.yml` runs `--kind combined` after the current-run bundle and BEFORE the canonical upload + publish dispatch, `compute_calibration_pythia.yml` runs `--kind scored` after the scored-bundle step; both `continue-on-error`, both with `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`).** `python -m interpreter.run --db ... --kind {current,scored,combined} --pack <bundle zip>`: loads a Phase 2 bundle (`interpreter/packs.py`), assembles versioned templates (`interpreter/templates/v2/`, `prompts.py` — v2 carries the Orwell house-style rules, the banned-word list and the em-dash ban), calls Opus 5 once (role `interpreter`, `ModelSpec.thinking` → `output_config.effort`, default `high`, 32k output ceiling, temperature never sent, `log_call=False` + a rich `llm_calls` row with `phase='interpreter'`), runs the validator (`interpreter/validate.py` — six separately-reported checks: jsonschema shape incl. kind-conditional requirements; referential — every cited question_id exists in the pack and every figure reference resolves; numeric — referenced figures re-derived with INDEPENDENT SQL from `forecast_deviation`/`hs_triage` and compared at rtol 1e-6, skipped-with-reason when no DB/tables; prose — bare-numeral lint with a calendar whitelist, lexicon band agreement with longest-first consumed phrase matching and ambiguous sentences skipped, per-field length cap; **style** — em/en dashes, banned phrases, "not X, but Y", and bare ISO3/metric codes in prose; **categories** — the pack decides which forecasts the report covers and under which heading, and the model may not promote, demote, invent or drop an entry (skipped when the pack has no categorised rows); a crashed check is a failed check; `PYTHIA_INTERPRETER_STRICT_VALIDATION=1` suppresses the publication artifacts while the row still stores), renders markdown with every `{{fig:...}}` placeholder substituted from pack-derived figure maps (`render.py` — the model's own numbers are never printed; unresolved refs render `[figure unavailable]` and are counted), and stores a versioned `interpretations` row (`store.py`, is_test None-and-inherit; `--force` appends a new version, default skips when an `ok` row exists; failures store as `failed_generation`/`failed_validation` and remain inspectable; `main()` always returns 0). The combined kind folds in the latest stored `scored` interpretation — its `figures_json` is persisted precisely so combined rendering never needs the scored pack. Model seam for tests: `interpreter.run._call_model`. The probability lexicon (`lexicon.py`) is fixed IPCC-style bands, printed in the report appendix. **Test-mode derivation (Phase 6, the Sibyl pattern)**: the runner probes `hs_runs.is_test` for the interpreted run and exports `PYTHIA_TEST_MODE=1` when it's a test run (the workflow step can't carry the upstream test_mode input) — an explicit env always wins, and the derivation only ever turns the flag ON. Full doc: `docs/interpreter.md`. Tests: `interpreter/tests/` (model mocked, temp DuckDB), run by `interpreter-ci.yml`.
- `interpreter/selection.py` + `interpreter/names.py` + `interpreter/charts.py` + `interpreter/mapviz.py` — **What the report covers, what it calls things, and its pictures (Aug 2026).** `selection.py` stamps `category` + `category_rank` onto the attention rows and is a pure function over plain dicts (no DB, no IO): four boxes — worsening/stable_major × climate(DR,FL,TC)/conflict(ACE,ACO). **Since v3 (Aug 2026) membership is the GATE's decision, not selection's**: `select_worsening` reads `gate == 'larger than usual'`, `select_stable_major` reads `gate == 'heavy burden'`, and this module only ORDERS — the worsening list by the MOVEMENT (`gating.movement_rank_key`), the heavy-burden list by the expected burden, thin anchors demoted in both, and each section heading states its own ordering (`CATEGORY_ORDERINGS`) — and cuts to `PYTHIA_INTERPRETER_MAX_ENTRIES` (5) across the whole report, split 60/40 with either side handing unused slots to the other. The v2 per-box knobs (`worsening_multiple`/`min_per_category`/`max_per_category`) were **removed** — ranking by a multiple is exactly the defect the excess ordering exists to fix, and a second policy beside the gate would let the two disagree. "A worsening entry is never relabelled roughly stable" is now structural: a row carries ONE gate and the two sections read different gates. `names.py` maps ISO3 (from `resolver/data/countries.csv`, `utf-8-sig` for the BOM) / hazard / metric codes to words and is the SINGLE naming implementation — the markdown, the PDF and the API's `_name_attention_entries` all call it, so the dashboard carries no second copy of the country table. `charts.py` draws a forecast distribution as inline SVG in Fred's palette (drawn from the PACK, never from model output — a chart that disagreed with the numbers beside it would be worse than none). `mapviz.py` is the printed attention map (see `pdf.py`). **`gating.py` + `panels.py` (v3, materiality key redesigned v4 Aug 2026)** sit in front of `selection.py` and decide what is even eligible. `gating.py` is pure functions over plain numbers with no DB, IO or config reads (every threshold arrives as an argument, so the caller owns the policy and the tests state it): in-bucket `exceedance`/`quantile`/`p_zero`/`expected_value` off `pythia/buckets.py` edges, `materiality_threshold` = `min(absolute, share x population)` (the share exists so a flat 50,000 floor does not silently exclude every small island state from a cyclone report), `percentile` written out so this repo's tests pin the definition, and `gate_rows`, which stamps `passed_unusual`/`passed_material`/`passed_worsening`/`material_movement`/`movement_shape`/`peak_horizon`/`baserate_thin`/`gate` and **returns the counts** the panel, the tags and the appendix table all read. **v4 split materiality in two**: `passed_material` is the LEVEL test (P(>= threshold) >= minprob in any horizon) and decides heavy burden; `passed_worsening` is the MOVEMENT test (`material_movement = max(delta_p50, delta_p90) >= movement_threshold` at the peak horizon, from `forecast_deviation`) and decides worsening. The three gates in the words the report prints: `larger than usual` (unusual AND moved), `heavy burden` (level only), `unusual, small scale` (the watchlist). `rank_key` orders by `excess_nominal` descending with thin anchors demoted; `movement_rank_key` does the same on the movement and is what the worsening list uses. `PYTHIA_INTERPRETER_GATE_MODE=level` restores the pre-v4 key without a deploy, and `gatecheck` runs both modes over every run in a DB, printing which questions cleared and what the near misses were (`run_sibyl.yml` does this every cycle into `pythia-interpreter-gate-diagnostics`). **`interpreter/anchorcheck.py`** reports what every anchor in a DB actually rests on, per (hazard, metric), and diagnoses a too-short window from dropped quiet months — the v3 report marked 138 of 185 anchors thin and both causes were in `base_rate_spd`, not in the data. `panels.py` generates the report's account of itself: `humanise_bucket_label` ("1 to 9,999 people", never `1-<10k`; "deaths" for FATALITIES), `selection_panel` (which describes the mode actually in force — a panel stating last month's rule is confidently wrong), `question_table` (v4: two MOVEMENT columns replace the chance column, and a cell that cannot carry a number carries the reason, never "n/a"), `planning_figures`, and **`planning_sentence`** — generated rather than written, because it is a frame with two numbers and no judgement, and the model's version produced "+5 people more deaths" and a contingency figure identical to the planning figure where both quantiles landed in the open-ended top band. `packs.report_extras(pack)` assembles all of it; `run.py` stores it under `figures_json['extras']` and threads it into `render.render_markdown(..., extras=...)`. Tests: `interpreter/tests/test_selection_names_charts.py`. **v3 additions (Aug 2026), all pure and all generated rather than written by the model**: `decisions.py` (the decision calendar — the model writes the ACTION, the deadline is DERIVED as the peak horizon less `PYTHIA_INTERPRETER_LEAD_TIME_{HAZARD}` and overwritten from the pack by `run._repair_decision_points`, the same repair contract as the identity fields); `secondopinion.py` (Sibyl's direction of disagreement on expected value, its unsettled-trials flag, and the novel-evidence extraction — trial sentences whose content words are absent from the ASSEMBLED SPD PROMPT, with nothing claimed novel below 500 chars of comparison text); `sector.py` (rank gaps against ACAPS INFORM Severity + Risk Radar, countries in BOTH rankings only, fixed `NOT_COMMENSURABLE` framing); `persistence.py` (matching on `(iso3, hazard_code, metric)`, movement over the CALENDAR months two runs share, persistence counted from stored reports and stopping at the first gap, drop reasons); `performance.py` (Part B: `upcoming_resolutions` — a window month resolves on the 28th of the month after it — plus the small-sample floor, the seeded paired bootstrap and the forecast diary); `gatecheck.py` (the CLI for `gating.calibration_table`, which had no caller). Tests: `interpreter/tests/test_v3_modules.py`, `test_performance.py`, `test_v3_report.py`, `scripts/ai_bundle/tests/test_current_run_bundle_v3.py`.
- `interpreter/pdf.py` — **PDF renderer (Phase 6, Aug 2026).** `python -m interpreter.pdf --db ... --kind combined --out-dir interpreter_out`: reads the newest `interpretations` row (test-filtered by default; `--interpretation-id` pins one), converts `content_md` to self-contained HTML with a small deterministic subset converter (only what `render_markdown` emits; inline-code spans protect underscored question ids from emphasis parsing), and renders via **WeasyPrint** (lazy import; test seam `interpreter.pdf._render_pdf`). The page carries Fred's masthead and palette, an inline SVG of each entry's forecast distribution (`interpreter/charts.py`, drawn from the PACK not from model output), absolute question links (`PYTHIA_PUBLIC_BASE_URL`), and the **attention map** — `interpreter/mapviz.py` projects the vendored Natural Earth boundaries the PA machine ships and fills countries from `forecast_deviation` with the SAME table/preference/scaling as `/v1/interpreter/attention_map`, so the printed and dashboard maps cannot disagree. **v4 made the scale DIVERGING over the SIGNED movement** (scaled by the metric's own threshold so deaths and people in crisis share one ramp; largest magnitude wins where a country carries several hazards, and the legend names the direction) — the old undirected scale shaded a country whose forecast had fallen exactly like one whose forecast had risen. The v4 PDF also drops the masthead (two titles above the report's own) and puts the map and the contents on one page (drawn here because the dashboard's choropleth is JavaScript and a PDF cannot run JavaScript; coordinates rounded to the pixel and duplicates dropped, or the 1:50m source would put megabytes into every PDF; a missing boundary file degrades to no map, never a failed render). Writes `report__{YYYY-MM}__v{n}.pdf` + the constant-name `interpreter_report_latest.pdf`. A render failure keeps the HTML as evidence and exits 0; a non-ok row renders behind a validation banner unless `PYTHIA_INTERPRETER_STRICT_VALIDATION=1` suppresses the PDF (row stays stored); `main()` always returns 0. Runs in `run_sibyl.yml` right after the combined report (apt pango top-up + `pip install weasyprint`, `continue-on-error`), uploads as the **`pythia-interpreter-report`** artifact. `publish_latest_data.yml` fetches that artifact best-effort from the source run, and **falls back to the newest non-expired `pythia-interpreter-report` artifact repo-wide when the source run has none** — a canonical-DB producer that is not an interpreter producer (the calibration chain, a compaction, a backfill, a purge) legitimately has no report, and on 2026-09-01 that left September's report stranded in an artifact while the release served August's. The fallback searches by artifact NAME, not by workflow, so it does not become a fourth hand-maintained producer list; and it takes a candidate only when strictly newer than the published asset's `updatedAt`, because an artifact expires at 90 days while a release asset does not — so the newest artifact can be OLDER than what is published. With neither a fresh nor a newer artifact, the release keeps the PDF it has. It uploads the PDFs to the `pythia-data-latest` release with `--clobber`, and stamps `interpreter_report_asset`/`interpreter_report_url`/`interpreter_report_versioned_asset` into `manifest.json` — reflecting what is downloadable AFTER the publish (fresh or carried over). `/v1/version` passes manifest keys through (`result = dict(manifest)`), which is where the dashboard's Download PDF button reads the URL. A backfilled report is named for the month it is ABOUT — `month_label` parses `hs_run_id` and only falls back to `created_at` — so July's report generated in August is not filed as August's. Tests: `interpreter/tests/test_pdf.py`.
- `.github/workflows/interpreter_backfill.yml` — **Manual interpreter backfill/recovery (Aug 2026).** Runs the interpreter over already-completed forecast runs: `compute_deviation --run-id` + `build_current_run_bundle --run-id` + `interpreter.run` + `interpreter.pdf`, per run, nothing special-cased (it rebuilds exactly what the live path would have had). Inputs: `run_ids` (blank = latest production run), `kind`, `force`, `include_test`, `db_run_id`, `upload_canonical` (default true — without it the rows die with the runner), `publish` (default **false**), `force_during_pipeline`. Gated by `check_pipeline_active` and in the `pythia-resolver-db` concurrency group (a run landing mid-pipeline would write rows the pipeline's final canonical upload discards). **It re-uploads the canonical DB, so it is a canonical-DB producer: named in all three discovery lists and self-included via `extra-workflows`** (otherwise a second backfill starts from a DB predating the first). Per-run PDFs go to the `pythia-interpreter-backfill` diagnostics artifact; one top-level render of the newest row goes to `pythia-interpreter-report` so the publish step finds exactly one `interpreter_report_latest.pdf`.
- `pythia/api/routes/interpreter.py` + `web/src/app/interpreter/` — **Interpreter API + dashboard (Phase 5, Aug 2026).** Endpoints: `/v1/interpreter/latest` (highest version of the newest run per kind; `has_interpretation:false` on a pre-interpreter DB, never a 500), `/versions`, `/{interpretation_id}` (declared LAST so the literal routes win), `/attention_map` (per-ISO3 max `js_vs_baserate`/ln2 from `forecast_deviation` via the bayesmc→mean→track2 preference; every table test-filtered). **Placeholders are resolved SERVER-side** via `interpreter/render.py::resolve_content` (`content_resolved` beside the raw `content`) so figure formatting has exactly one implementation, and `_name_attention_entries` stamps `country_name`/`hazard_name`/`metric_name` on each entry from `interpreter/names.py` — the alternative was a second copy of the country table in TypeScript. Frontend: `/interpreter` (Nav "Fred's Monthly Risk Report") renders `content_resolved` grouped into the report's **four named sections** (`groupAttention`/`sectionHeading`/`entryHeading` in `lib.ts` mirror `interpreter/selection.py`'s SECTION_ORDER; anything the model failed to place lands under "Other situations of note" rather than disappearing) with full names in every heading, question links, a version selector, visible warn/error banners for non-ok statuses, an explicit no-report empty state, a **run selector defaulting to the most recent run** (`groupVersionsByRun` in `web/src/app/interpreter/lib.ts`, labelled by the month the report is ABOUT via `runMonthLabel`, which parses `hs_run_id` exactly as `pdf.py::month_label` does; the per-run version selector shows only when a run has >1 version), the shared glossary, and the **attention map** — `RiskIndexMap` gained optional props (`valuesByIso3`/`colorFor`/`valueLabelFor`/`legendItems`/`onCountryClick`/`showRcOverlay`, all optional so existing call sites are byte-untouched) coloured by attention with click-to-scroll to the country's report section; `/interpreter/print` is the print/PDF fallback (no nav, page breaks) — the CI PDF renderer (`interpreter/pdf.py`) deliberately does NOT load this page (that would need a running Next.js + API in CI; it builds equivalent HTML from the stored row instead), and the page gained a **Download PDF** button linking to the release asset via `/v1/version`'s `interpreter_report_url` (hidden when no asset exists). **When the release advertises a report PDF but the API serves no report, the page says "still syncing" rather than the no-report empty state** — the API's DB lagging the release it just read the manifest from is a soft-fail, and a soft-fail must never look like "no data". **Navigation is one dropdown at every breakpoint**, driven by the single `NAV_LINKS` array in `web/src/components/Nav.tsx` (it was previously an inline desktop row plus a separate mobile list — two hand-maintained copies that could drift); the interpreter entry is labelled "Fred's Monthly Risk Report". `web/scripts/ci/ui_guardrails.py`'s /about check accepts both `href="/about"` and the data form `href: "/about"`. Score/deviation tooltip copy is single-sourced in `web/src/lib/score_glossary.ts` (PerformancePanel imports the same constants — the AboutScores drift rule). Tests: `pythia/tests/test_api_interpreter.py` (named in interpreter-ci — nothing runs `pythia/tests/` broadly) + `web/src/app/interpreter/__tests__/lib.test.ts` (web-ci vitest).
- `docs/interpreter_phase0_discovery.md` — Phase 0 discovery report for the interpreter module (base-rate coverage per pair, the unwired Anthropic thinking knob and the `output_config.effort` change needed, the map that was never actually deleted → extend `RiskIndexMap` via optional props, the release-asset mechanism for the future PDF, and the calibration-advice/AboutScores reuse map). Read it before building interpreter Phases 2–6.
- `pythia/tools/_db_utils.py` — Shared rollback-safe DuckDB helpers (`rollback_quietly`, `table_exists`, `row_count`, `column_exists`, `add_column_if_missing`) imported by all four post-forecast tools. Do not re-implement these locally — the DuckDB ≥1.5 aborted-transaction ROLLBACK scrub lives here.
- `pythia/tools/source_coverage.py` — Rebuilds the `source_coverage` table (PK: metric, iso3, ym) from the metric source tables at the start of every `compute_resolutions` run; `months_with_source_data` / `countries_with_source_data` readers gate zero-defaulting (month gate for FATALITIES + EVENT_OCCURRENCE; country-universe gate for FATALITIES only — EVENT_OCCURRENCE is deliberately exempt because GDACS is satellite-global). Extend `_COVERAGE_QUERIES` together with compute_resolutions' zero-default rules.
- `docs/audit_2026-07.md` — July 2026 full-codebase audit: what was fixed, deferred recommendations, and investigated-but-NOT-bugs (check it before re-reporting a previously-assessed issue). The deferred S4 items (decompositions, source_coverage, centroid versioning, precedence consolidation, test gaps) were implemented in the S4 follow-up PR. A **follow-up audit section (2026-07-07)** covers the post-audit PRs #780–#783 (Sibyl, API memory, model registry, lineup): fixes FU1–FU9, its own not-bugs list, and carried-forward deferred items. A **post-batch-pipeline audit section (2026-07-28)** covers PRs #808–#812 (batch-state pipeline scoping, cost-accounting integrity, the prompt-cache wiring, reporting fixes) with its own not-bugs and deferred lists — check ALL sections before re-reporting.
- `docs/audit_2026-08_pa_machine.md` — August 2026 full-integration audit of the PA resolution machine PRs #843–#849: what was unwired, the extraction-path bugs fixed before first production run, and the deliberately deferred items (the shadow-mode resolution-bridge flip above all). Check it before re-reporting a machine issue.
- `docs/montandon_assessment.md` — Assessment of IFRC Montandon/GO as a PA resolution source (Aug 2026). Explains why PA resolves at ~4%/2%/0% (FL/ACE/DR), and records the naming trap: the `ifrc_montandon` connector talks to the **IFRC GO operational API** (`goadmin.ifrc.org/api/v2/`), not Montandon's STAC Global Crisis Data Bank — which we have never evaluated. §3 is the improvement list to send IFRC; §4 is what is ours to fix. Read it before changing anything on the PA resolution path.
- `pythia/tools/compute_calibration_pythia.py` — Calibration weights + LLM advice
- `resolver/hazard_resolution/` — **PA resolution machine (Phase 0 scaffold + Phase 1 cyclone path + Phase 2 flood path & impact ladder + Phase 3 ReliefWeb LLM extraction + Phase 4 drought via IPC + Phase 5 backcast/base rates/acceptance + Phase 6 prompt integration, Aug 2026; see the dedicated section below).** Replaces the ~4%-coverage IFRC-GO-only PA resolution path with a two-layer machine: Layer 1 detection (IBTrACS/GDACS/IPC — did a qualifying hazard occur?), Layer 2 impact (fixed ladder EM-DAT → ReliefWeb LLM-extracted → IFRC GO → IDMC IDU lower bound). Target ≥80% of country-month-hazard cells resolved to a number or credible zero. **Hard rules**: every resolution carries provenance (source, record ids/URLs, retrieval timestamp, rule fired); reconciliation is deterministic (no LLM calls); LLM extraction transcribes stated figures only; resolutions freeze at month-end + `freeze_days` and are never reopened (revisions audit to `haz_revisions`); GDACS is detection + plausibility ceiling only, never a resolution value; all thresholds live in `rulebook.yaml` (never hard-code one — the loader validates strictly and rejects credential-looking keys, since API keys are env-only: `EMDAT_API_KEY`, `IDMC_API_KEY`, `RELIEFWEB_APPNAME`, existing `IPC_API_KEY`). Files: `rulebook.yaml` (single source of truth for behaviour), `rulebook.py` (load/validate, dotted `rb.get("cyclone.buffer_km")`), `rules.py` (deterministic predicates: cyclone buffer/wind, GDACS level ordering, freeze deadline, sanity ceiling + population cap, ladder rank, lower-bound membership, order-of-magnitude conflict, event months / attribution month), `schema.py` (idempotent `haz_*` DDL + `_COLUMN_MIGRATIONS` for columns added after a table shipped; spec ENUMs as TEXT+CHECK; JSON as `*_json TEXT`), `migrate.py` (CLI `python -m resolver.hazard_resolution.migrate`), `population.py` (loads `resolver/data/population.csv` into `haz_raw_population`, filters World Bank aggregates via `countries.csv`); Phase 1 adds `ibtracs.py`, `geometry.py` + `data/` (vendored NE boundaries), `detect.py`, `reliefweb_sweep.py`, `resolutions.py`, `cli.py` (the `resolve-hazards` poetry script); Phase 2 adds `sources.py` (shared raw-cache plumbing), the four ladder connectors `gdacs.py`/`emdat.py`/`ifrc_go.py`/`idmc_idu.py`, `candidates.py` (builds `haz_impact_candidates`), `reconcile.py` (the pure-function ladder engine), and `impact.py` (per-month orchestration); Phase 3 adds `reliefweb_docs.py` (document selection + caching), `extract.py` (the one LLM step + strict parser + `haz_doc_extractions` cache/cost ledger), and `figures.py` (deterministic households conversion, dedup, authority precedence, ceiling rejection); Phase 4 adds `ipc.py` (IPC/CH current-period ANALYSES into `haz_raw_ipc`, two acquisition paths), `drought_indicators.py` (the ASAP/tabular feed layer + `haz_raw_drought_indicators`), and `drought.py` (the pure-function drought rule + per-month orchestration — no ladder, no LLM); Phase 5 adds `backcast.py` (the historical replay driver + `haz_backcast_progress` resume ledger), `base_rates.py` (occurrence + severity into the `haz_base_rates_*` tables, plus the shared `last_frozen_month`/`backcast_window`/`months_back_window` helpers), `dfo.py` (the Dartmouth Flood Observatory calibration cross-check + `haz_raw_dfo`), and `acceptance.py` (the one-page markdown acceptance report); Phase 6 adds `prompt_block.py` (the base-rate block the forecaster is shown, generated from the rulebook — the machine's only outward-facing module, and the only one imported from outside the package). Tests: `resolver/tests/test_hazard_resolution_{rulebook,schema,population,ibtracs,geometry,sweep,cyclone,ladder,sources,flood,extraction,drought,backcast,base_rates,dfo,acceptance,prompt_block}.py` (run by resolver-ci-fast, which runs `resolver/tests` wholesale; `resolver/hazard_resolution/**` is in its path filters). `rulebook.yaml` and `data/*.geojson.gz` (the vendored boundaries) are named in pyproject's poetry `include` list (packaged data files, like `series_semantics.yml`). Poetry scripts: `resolve-hazards`, `haz-backcast`, `haz-base-rates`, `haz-acceptance`, `haz-migrate`. **Wired into production 2026-08-05** (resolver_update Phase 2.5 + nightly `haz_backcast.yml`, shadow mode — see the machine section's "Production wiring" block).
- `resolver/connectors/protocol.py` — Connector protocol (21-column canonical schema contract)
- `resolver/connectors/__init__.py` — Connector registry (discover_connectors: ACLED, IDMC, IFRC Montandon, GDACS, FEWS NET IPC, IPC API — six entries) + FORECAST_REGISTRY (VIEWS, CF.org, ACLED CAST)
- `resolver/connectors/fewsnet_ipc.py` — FEWS NET IPC Phase 3+ population connector (DR hazard; Current Situation + Most Likely scenarios)
- `resolver/connectors/acled_cast.py` — ACLED CAST connector (event-count forecasts by type: total/battles/ERV/VAC)
- `resolver/tools/run_pipeline.py` — Pipeline orchestrator (fetch -> validate -> enrich -> precedence -> deltas -> DuckDB)
- `resolver/tools/enrich.py` — Enrichment (registry lookups, ym derivation, defaults)
- `resolver/tools/precedence_config.yml` — Precedence tier policy
- `pythia/prediction_markets/retriever.py` — Prediction market signal retriever (disabled in production via `PYTHIA_PREDICTION_MARKETS_ENABLED=0`, pinned in both workflows — note the *code* default is ON, see the env table; Metaculus returns 403, Polymarket returns 422, each times out at 30s)
- `scripts/print_forecaster_ensemble.py` — Ensemble diagnostic script (must be invoked as `python -m scripts.print_forecaster_ensemble`, not directly)
- `pythia/acaps.py` — ACAPS unified connector (4 datasets: INFORM Severity, Risk Radar, Daily Monitoring, Humanitarian Access)
- `pythia/gdelt.py` — GDELT conflict indicators connector (media-derived conflict intensity for ACE hazard). Downloads GDELT 1.0 daily event exports from `data.gdeltproject.org` (no auth, no cost), classifies events by CAMEO tier (T1 direct violence 18/19/20, T2 escalatory posture 15/17, T3 political deterioration 14/16), computes per-country daily aggregates and rolling 7/30/60-day windows. Provides `fetch_and_store_gdelt_indicators()`, `load_gdelt_conflict_indicators(iso3)`, `format_gdelt_for_prompt(iso3)`, `format_gdelt_for_rc_prompt(iso3)`. Wired into ACE RC, ACE triage, and ACE SPD prompts. Self-storing source. FIPS→ISO3 mapping is a static dict (GDELT uses FIPS 10-4 country codes). Env: `GDELT_EVENTS_DAYS` (default 90), `GDELT_EVENTS_REQUEST_DELAY` (default 1.0).
- `pythia/food_security.py` — Unified food security loader (FEWS NET primary, IPC API fallback); provides `load_food_security()`, `format_food_security_for_prompt()`, `format_food_security_for_spd()`. Routes: tries FEWS NET (publisher='FEWS NET') first, falls back to IPC (publisher='IPC'). Wired into RC, triage, and SPD prompts.
- `pythia/fewsnet_food_security.py` — Backward-compat shim that re-exports from `food_security.py`. Deprecated; existing callers still work.
- `resolver/tools/repair_publication_dates.py` — **The publication-date repair pass (Sept 2026, Group E).** Runs on every Resolver Update (Phase 5, before the counts are verified), idempotently: every `facts_resolved`/`facts_deltas`/`emdat_pa` row whose `publication_date` is after today is rewritten in place — a delta from the publication date of the fact it was derived from, otherwise from the row's own `created_at`, otherwise today — and the run logs repaired / untouched counts per table with the source of each repair. Nothing is deleted: the figure was right, the date beside it was not. `count_future` is the acceptance query. The writers it cleans up after are fixed too: `enrich.fix_publication_date` keeps a stated date and fills a missing one from `as_of_date` only when that date has passed (the old rule raised EVERY date to the period end and then clamped it to today, so an IPC projection window ending in March 2027 read as published on the run date, every run); `make_deltas.process_group` carries the fact's `publication_date` into the delta; and `duckdb_io._publication_date_fallback` caps the storage-layer fill at today. (The legacy `pythia/ipc_phases.py` connector and its `ipc_phases` table were deleted in the same change — dead since the IPC API connector replaced them.)
- `resolver/connectors/ipc_api.py` — IPC API connector for non-FEWS NET countries. Fetches Phase 3+ data from api.ipcinfo.org, excludes FEWS NET countries, writes to facts_resolved. Env: `IPC_API_KEY`, `IPC_API_MONTHS` (default 24), `IPC_API_REQUEST_DELAY` (default 1.0).
- `pythia/acled_political.py` — ACLED event-level political data (protests, riots, strategic developments); wired into bulk ingest via `_bulk_fetch_acled_political` in `ingest_structured_data.py`. Country attribution is by each event's OWN returned country (never the request param — the ACLED API silently ignores unknown filters), resolved robustly via `_event_iso3` (tries `iso3`/`country_iso3`/`#country+code`, then the `country` name via `resolve_iso3`) since the live API's ISO3 key is not always literally `iso3`; server-side filter uses the documented numeric `iso` param via pycountry.
- `pythia/adversarial_check.py` — Counter-evidence searches for RC Level 1+ (devil's advocate)
- `horizon_scanner/reliefweb.py` — ReliefWeb humanitarian reports connector
- `forecaster/hazard_prompts.py` — Hazard-specific reasoning guidance for SPD prompts. DR/PHASE3PLUS_IN_NEED dispatches to `_DR_PHASE3` (FEWS NET IPC-specific); DR/PA still uses `_DR` (IFRC Montandon). FEWS NET projection context injected into DR/PHASE3PLUS_IN_NEED prompts via `_load_fewsnet_projection()` in prompts.py.
- `forecaster/scoring.py` — Scoring utilities: `multiclass_brier()`, `log_score()`, `binary_brier()` (Brier score for binary forecasts: `(forecast_p - outcome)^2`)
- `forecaster/trace_validation.py` — Diagnostic validation of structured reasoning traces from SPD ensemble members. Checks prior consistency, delta arithmetic, and magnitude consistency. Produces `trace_quality_score` (0-1) per model. Never blocks forecasts.
- `scripts/ci/snapshot_prompt_artifact.py` — Prompt version snapshot script
- `scripts/ai_bundle/build_forecast_attribution_bundle.py` + `signal_taxonomy.csv` — Forecast attribution bundle builder (claimed attribution from reasoning traces; see the "Forecast attribution bundle" section)
- `scripts/ci/write_stage_context.py` / `scripts/ci/sibyl_bundle_context.py` — The stage-context hand-off that lets the Sibyl job build the operational bundle for the forecaster stage (see "Bundle production at the Sibyl terminus")
- `scripts/ai_bundle/build_scored_forecast_bundle.py` — Scored-forecast analysis bundle builder (one AI-consumable zip per calibration round joining forecast reasoning to outcomes; see the "Scored-forecast analysis bundle" section)
- `scripts/ci/diagnose_acled_cast.py` — **The ACLED CAST diagnostic (Sept 2026).** CAST has served a 2025-12-01 vintage since the 2026-01 cycle while the transport stays healthy (auth succeeds, ~9,879 records return), so this is not an outage. Three explanations fit and they call for three different fixes, so the script decides between them from evidence in one authenticated GET: it dumps the response **ENVELOPE** — `status`/`success`/`count`/`last_update`/`messages`/`data_query_restrictions`, the half `acled_cast.py` discards, and where ACLED reports a spent free-tier quota — alongside the row shape (distinct year/month, max `timestamp`, undocumented and period-shaped columns). Verdicts: `account_tier` (a quota or recency limit stated in the envelope → contact access@acleddata.com, do not change the connector), `schema_change` (period-shaped columns, or a recent `timestamp` beside a stale `month`/`year` — CAST's methodology page describes a four-week rolling period while the Sept 2025 API docs still say month/year → re-key the connector on the period), `upstream_stopped` (nothing points at either → escalate and build a baseline from the ACLED history already in the DB), or `inconclusive`. Read-only, always exits 0. Run via `ingest-structured-data.yml`'s `diagnose_cast` dispatch input, since only CI holds the credentials. Tests: `scripts/ci/tests/test_diagnose_acled_cast.py` (named in resolver-ci-fast).
- `scripts/ci/diagnose_acled_auth.py`: **The ACLED OAuth diagnostic (Sept 2026).** ACLED's token endpoint answered both grants HTTP 200 with a body that was not JSON, and four causes produce that one symptom: a Cloudflare challenge, a moved route, a gateway content-negotiating HTML to a request that never asked for JSON, or a dead credential. The script probes the configured route with a GET, runs the password and refresh grants, repeats the password grant in the PRE-FIX request shape (no `Accept`, no User-Agent) so content negotiation can be told apart from the rest, and only when nothing has answered does it try the candidate alternate routes (guesses, labelled as such in the report). Verdicts: `waf_challenge`, `endpoint_moved`, `html_not_json`, `credentials_rejected`, `upstream_error`, `ok`, `inconclusive`. `decide()` is pure and the transport is injected. Bodies are redacted against the environment's own secrets before they are printed or stored, because an OAuth refusal quotes the username back and the report lands in a public artifact. Read-only, always exits 0. Run via `resolver_update.yml`'s `diagnostics` dispatch input (`acled_auth` or `all`), since only CI holds the ACLED secrets. That input is a CHOICE, not a boolean per diagnostic: GitHub caps `workflow_dispatch` at TEN inputs and this workflow is at the cap, so the whole diagnostic family shares one slot. Tests: `scripts/ci/tests/test_diagnose_acled_auth.py` (named in resolver-ci-fast).
- `scripts/build_resolver_debug_bundle.py` — **The resolver debug bundle (Sept 2026).** One zip per `resolver_update.yml` run, uploaded as `resolver-debug-bundle-<run_id>`, built by an `if: always()` step so the runs worth diagnosing are the ones it covers. It exists because the `backfill-diagnostics` artifact could not settle, from the artifact alone, any of: which URL each connector called, what the ACLED CAST envelope said, what the rulebook thresholds were, why 1,215 of 2,223 hazard cells produced no row, or which code and config the run was executing — all of it had to be inferred from log prose. Sections: `run/` (workflow identity, the effective environment and the windows each phase resolved, git, versions), `logs/` (the phase logs plus `log_index.md`, an ERROR/WARNING histogram normalised by replacing ISO3 codes, dates and numbers with placeholders so one fault repeated 252 times reads as one row), `http/` (`requests.jsonl` per outbound call and `envelopes/<connector>.json` — every top-level response field EXCEPT the bulk payload, plus the payload's columns and the maximum of every date-like column, which is the arithmetic behind a vintage claim), `db/` (both signatures, `table_counts.csv` over EVERY table, `freshness.csv` per date column with its threshold and verdict, and `queries/*.csv` each carrying the SQL that produced it), `hazard/` (`cell_ledger.csv`, `figures_ledger.csv`, `extraction_budget.csv`, `backcast_progress.csv`, the run summaries and the acceptance report), `config/` (rulebook, connector configs and the three workflows, verbatim), `code/` (the source files the logs named plus one hop of their repo-internal imports, with a hashed `file_index.csv`), and `checks/` (`contradictions.md` and `reconciliation.md`). **Regenerable locally**: `python -m scripts.build_resolver_debug_bundle --db ... --out ...`, degrading section by section when the database, the CI variables or the phase logs are absent. **80 MB compressed ceiling**: when it binds `code/` goes before any evidence does, then the largest logs are cut to head+tail — every drop and truncation named in the manifest with the original size. **Redaction is verified, not assumed**: values are fingerprinted (`<redacted:sha256:xxxxxxxx>`) at capture and again at assembly, then the whole staging tree is scanned for every secret in the environment and a hit raises `SecretLeak`, deletes the zip and exits 2. Tests: `resolver/tests/test_resolver_debug_bundle.py` (one test per question the bundle must answer) — `scripts/build_resolver_debug_bundle.py` is in resolver-ci-fast's `paths:`, since the builder lives under `scripts/` but every question it answers is a resolver question.
- `resolver/diagnostics/redaction.py` / `run_log.py` / `http_recorder.py` — **The instrumentation the bundle reads (Sept 2026).** `redaction.py` redacts by NAME (`KEY|TOKEN|SECRET|PASSWORD|CREDENTIAL|EMAIL|CLIENT_ID|AUTH|BEARER|COOKIE`, minus a small allowlist so `RELIEFWEB_APPNAME` stays readable) and by VALUE (every credential in the environment, longest first). A URL keeps its host and path and loses its key — "which URL was called" is the question and the answer is worthless with the route removed. `run_log.py` is the run-scoped JSONL appender: OFF unless `PYTHIA_RUN_LOG_DIR` names a directory, never raises, disables itself after one write failure, and its reader skips any unparseable line so a run killed mid-write costs one record. `http_recorder.py` patches `requests.Session.request` ONCE rather than threading a logger through ten connectors; attribution comes from the call stack, so a connector written tomorrow is recorded with no edit. `maybe_install_from_env()` is called from the five CLI mains the workflow invokes AND from `resolver/ingestion/__init__.py`, because every ingestion connector runs as its own `python -m resolver.ingestion.<name>` subprocess. Tests: `resolver/tests/test_diagnostics_recording.py`.
- `resolver/hazard_resolution/cell_ledger.py` — **Why a cell got no row, stated where the decision is made (Sept 2026).** One JSONL record per assessed country-month-hazard from `cli.sweep_and_resolve_zeros`, `impact.resolve_triggered_cells` and `drought.run_month`, and one per extracted figure from `figures.build_candidates`. The reason codes each name a different repair: `pending_before_freeze` (the calendar), `sweep_inconclusive` (ReliefWeb unreadable, so silence is unproven), `coverage_gate_suppressed_zero` (an ingestion gap must not read as quiet), `indicator_inconclusive`, `frozen_row_unchanged`, `cell_raised`, `flipped_to_triggered_by_sweep`. The bundle merges these with a `haz_triggers` LEFT JOIN `haz_resolutions`, so a bundle built from a database alone is still most of the picture, and a cell neither source accounts for is labelled `unexplained_no_row` — a bug, not a silence.
- `scripts/ci/inspect_resolver_db.py` — Resolver DuckDB comprehensive inspection report generator (extracted July 2026 from the ~1,400-line heredoc formerly embedded in `inspect_resolver_duckdb.yml`; the workflow is now a thin wrapper). Sections: Data Freshness at-a-glance (observations split from `phase3plus_projection` so the newest real observation isn't masked by future-dated projections; ACAPS `%b%Y` snapshot labels parsed chronologically — SQL MIN/MAX sorted them alphabetically producing "Apr2026 → Sep2025"), **Inject Readiness** (per-source coverage of the 122-country `hs_country_list.txt` target list with missing-country details — the direct "are all injects ready for a full run" view), pipeline stage completeness, per-table detail, data-quality checks (conflict-forecast staleness >45d; the IDMC IDU note is informational since the forecaster matches `IN ('ACE','IDU')`; Stdlib + duckdb only (the inspect workflow installs no pandas). Tests: `scripts/ci/tests/test_inspect_resolver_db.py` (run by `resolver-ci-fast.yml`).
- `scripts/refresh_crisiswatch.py` — CrisisWatch scraper, two acquisition paths sharing one parser: `--source wayback` (used in CI by `refresh-crisiswatch.yml` — fetches the server-rendered page from the Wayback Machine, no browser needed) and `--source live` (local Playwright path, kept for manual runs). Writes `crisiswatch_latest.json`.
- `scripts/purge_acled_political_events.py` — Purge cross-contaminated `acled_political_events` rows (pre-July-2026 global-fetch bug); `--force` wipes unconditionally. The weekly ingest also self-heals via `purge_contaminated_events()`.
- `pythia/tools/generate_calibration_advice.py` — Per-hazard/metric calibration advice generation
- `tools/compare_gdacs_ifrc.py` — GDACS vs IFRC Montandon PA comparison diagnostic (side-by-side coverage, ratios, blind spots)
- `tools/probe_montandon_stac.py` — Read-only evaluation spike for the Montandon (Monty) STAC API, which we have never consumed (the `ifrc_montandon` connector polls the IFRC GO operational API instead). Answers eight gates and recommends one of four outcomes. **G1 (can impact records be filtered by upstream source?) is a KILL gate**: Montandon ingests GDACS, whose figures are modelled population exposure, so an unfilterable feed would blend exposure with reported impact in one column. **G4 (latency vs the resolution cutoff) decides resolution-vs-base-rates** and is the gate most likely to fail, since Montandon leans on the slow EM-DAT. Always exits 0 — "the endpoint is down" is a finding, not a build failure. Needs egress to `*.ifrc.org`; run via the `Probe Montandon STAC` workflow. Tests: `resolver/tests/test_probe_montandon_stac.py` (gate/verdict logic only — the network path is untestable in CI).
- `scripts/create_questions_from_triage.py` — Creates forecast questions from HS triage output. Supported hazards: ACE (FATALITIES+PA), CU (PA), DR (PHASE3PLUS_IN_NEED+EVENT_OCCURRENCE), FL (PA+EVENT_OCCURRENCE), TC (PA+EVENT_OCCURRENCE), DI (PA). HW excluded (no resolution source). DR/PA for food-security-monitored countries is remapped to metric=PHASE3PLUS_IN_NEED with IPC Phase 3+ wording (via `_build_dr_phase3_question_wording`); other countries get no DR/PA question (binary DR/EVENT_OCCURRENCE still generated). **Food-security eligibility (July 2026)**: `_is_fewsnet_country` (curated git-tracked `resolver/data/fewsnet_countries.json`, fail-open if missing) OR `_is_ipc_country` (`resolver/data/ipc_countries.json`, fail-closed — the file is connector-written and never committed, so it typically doesn't exist) OR the **DB fallback** `_db_food_security_countries(con)`: distinct iso3 with `phase3plus_in_need` rows in facts_resolved within the last 36 months (cached per connection) — this is what actually enables DR questions for IPC-only countries. EVENT_OCCURRENCE questions generated for ALL countries (GDACS global coverage). Binary question wording references GDACS Orange/Red alerts. Epoch-specific question_ids with `_{epoch_label}` suffix.
- `forecaster/binary_prompts.py` — Binary event prompt builder for EVENT_OCCURRENCE questions. Contains: `build_binary_event_prompt()` (6-section prompt: role/task, base rate, current situation, GDACS event history, hazard reasoning, output instructions; accepts optional `gdacs_event_history` dict), `build_binary_base_rate()` (queries facts_resolved for seasonal event rates), `get_binary_hazard_reasoning_block()` (DR/FL/TC-specific reasoning), `parse_binary_response()` (JSON parser with code fence stripping, probability clamping to [0.01, 0.99]).
- `scripts/db/update_bucket_centroids.py` — Seeds bucket definitions and centroids into DuckDB from `pythia/buckets.py` BUCKET_SPECS. Iterates all metrics (PA, FATALITIES, PHASE3PLUS_IN_NEED).
- `tools/analyze_fewsnet_distribution.py` — Diagnostic script to analyze FEWS NET Phase 3+ data distribution in facts_resolved. Computes distribution stats, histogram by DR_PHASE3_BUCKETS, country-level summary, and bucket balance assessment.
- `resolver/data/fewsnet_countries.json` — List of 48 FEWS NET-monitored country ISO3 codes. Used by question generator to restrict DR/PA questions to FEWS NET countries. Written by the FEWS NET IPC connector during backfill.

## Databases

Two DuckDB databases:

**Pythia DB** (`PYTHIA_DB_URL`): system of record
- `hs_runs`, `hs_triage` — Horizon Scanner outputs (triage scores, RC fields)
- `hs_hazard_tail_packs` — RC/triage grounding evidence packs (name is historical: the tail-pack subsystem was removed July 2026; the RC and triage grounding passes persist their packs here, query-prefixed `rc_grounding:` / `triage_grounding:`, and the forecaster injects them into SPD prompts as "HS GROUNDING EVIDENCE")
- `hs_adversarial_checks` — Counter-evidence for RC Level 1+ (devil's advocate)
- `seasonal_forecasts` — NMME country-level temp/precip anomalies (monthly from CPC)
- `conflict_forecasts` — VIEWS + conflictforecast.org + ACLED CAST conflict predictions (PK: source, iso3, hazard_code, metric, lead_months, forecast_issue_date)
- `questions`, `question_research` — Seeded questions + research briefs
- `forecasts_raw`, `forecasts_ensemble` — Per-model + aggregated SPDs. Both include `reasoning_trace_json TEXT` column storing structured reasoning traces (prior, updates with deltas, point estimate, RC assessment) as JSON. NULL for binary forecasts, Track 2, or models that don't emit traces.
- `sibyl_runs` — Sibyl run records (hs_run_id, K, aggregation, run cost split Opus/Brave, `budget_capped` flag, coverage counts)
- `sibyl_forecasts` — Per-question Sibyl provenance (status incl. `skipped`/`run budget cap`, asOf, per-trial belief traces + quantiles JSON, pooled quantiles, bucket probs, JS divergences vs standard + inter-trial, cost split, leakage stats). The scoreable SPD itself lives in `forecasts_raw`/`forecasts_ensemble` under `model_name='sibyl'`.
- `resolutions` — Ground truth values per (question_id, horizon_m). Not all 6 horizons may have rows — source-aware null handling skips unresolvable horizons.
- `scores` — Brier/log/CRPS per (question, horizon, model). Also carries the external reference rows (`run_id IS NULL`): `__ext_views`, and since Aug 2026 `__ext_climatology`/`__ext_uniform` from `score_baselines.py`
- `forecast_deviation` — Deviation-from-base-rate per (run, question, aggregate model): `js_vs_baserate`, `log_ev_ratio`, `eiv_nominal`/`eiv_per_100k`, `score_family`, `baserate_source`/`baserate_json` provenance (written by `compute_deviation.py`; the interpreter's attention index reads it)
- `baseline_scored_forecasts` — Audit trail for the `__ext_climatology`/`__ext_uniform` reference scores (the exact SPD scored per question/horizon/model, PK on all three)
- `interpretations` — Interpreter reports, versioned per (kind, run key): content_json (validated model output), content_md (rendered report), figures_json (resolved figure maps), status ok/failed_validation/failed_generation, prompt/pack hashes, cost/tokens (written by `interpreter/store.py`; in `mark_test_runs`' RUN_ID_TABLES)
- `calibration_weights`, `calibration_advice` — Per hazard/metric weights + LLM advice
- `bucket_centroids` — SPD bucket centroids per (hazard_code, metric, bucket_index); seeded from `pythia/buckets.py`, updated via EMA from resolution data. Carries `schema_version` (bucket-boundary digest from `bucket_schema_version(metric)`): rows stamped with a stale version are refused by `compute_scores._load_centroids`; NULL (pre-stamp) rows are grandfathered
- `source_coverage` — Per-(metric, iso3, ym) coverage map of the resolution source tables (PK: metric, iso3, ym); rebuilt at the start of every compute_resolutions run; gates zero-defaulting
- `bucket_definitions` — SPD bucket boundary definitions per (metric, bucket_index)
- `reliefweb_reports` — ReliefWeb humanitarian situation reports
- `acled_political_events` — ACLED event-level political data
- `acaps_inform_severity`, `acaps_inform_severity_trend` — ACAPS INFORM severity scores
- `acaps_risk_radar` — ACAPS forward-looking risk assessments
- `acaps_daily_monitoring` — ACAPS analyst-curated daily updates
- `acaps_humanitarian_access` — ACAPS humanitarian access scores
- `crisiswatch_entries` — ICG CrisisWatch monthly arrows + alerts (PK: iso3, year, month; `content_hash` since Sept 2026 — the row is rewritten, and `fetched_at` moves, only when it changes)
- `hdx_signals` — HDX Signals (OCHA automated crisis monitoring) persisted from CSV (PK: iso3, indicator, signal_date)
- `gdelt_conflict_indicators` — GDELT per-country daily conflict indicators (T1/T2/T3 CAMEO tier counts, Goldstein + tone averages, top codes) (PK: iso3, event_date)
- `enso_state` — ENSO records (PK: fetch_date). Beyond the original columns: `oni` + `enso_strength` + `oni_basis` (`oni_table` | `weekly_3month_mean`), `observation_date` (when the index was OBSERVED, which is not `fetch_date` — a carried-forward row keeps its original), `source_rank_used`/`nino34_source`, `status` (`fresh` | `carried_forward` | `historical`) + `age_days`, `scraped_phase` (what the IRI page said, kept only for the disagreement check), `index_evidence_json` (every rank's answer, ok or not, with each rank's newest observation) and `warnings_json`. **Since 2026-09-04**: `row_kind` (`live` | `historical` | `repaired` — the published ONI table lives in this table one row per season, and "the state as of today" reads live/repaired rows only) and `nino34_weekly` (the newest weekly Niño 3.4, NULL when only the seasonal ONI table answered — never filled from the ONI). `nino34_anomaly` is the anomaly the phase rests on (weekly value, or the ONI itself when only rank 3 answered) and is never NULL beside a phase
- `seasonal_tc_outlooks` — Seasonal TC outlooks from TSR/NOAA CPC/BoM/Météo-France/IMD, one row per OUTLOOK since Sept 2026: PK `(basin, source, forecast_season, issue_date_key, category)` where `issue_date_key` is the ISO `issue_date` or `undated:<reason>`; `issue_date DATE` (never free text), `issue_date_reason`, `season_reason`, `named_storms_forecast` + `named_storms_reason`, `raw_json`, `fetched_at` (first seen) and `updated_at`. The old shape was keyed on `fetched_at` and appended a full copy every run; `_ensure_seasonal_tc_outlooks_table` rebuilds it on the new key, keeping the earliest fetch per outlook and deleting climatology baselines misread as seasons
- `seasonal_tc_context_cache` — Pre-formatted per-country TC context text (PK: iso3)
- `llm_calls` — Full telemetry (cost, tokens, latency, errors)
- `llm_batches`, `llm_batch_requests` — Provider Batch-API state (batch ids, per-request bodies/results); the cross-stage state of the staged pipeline (see the Batch-API section)
- `hs_stage_state` — Staged-HS carry state: merged RC results per country (hs_rc_collect → hs_finalize), plus two run-scoped rows under the sentinel `iso3='__RUN__'` — `brave_breaker` (circuit-breaker stats carried across stages) and `pipeline_run_id` (keyed by **pipeline_id**, records the hs_run_id that pipeline minted; the fallback that lets a collect stage resolve its run id when hs_submit created no `llm_batches` row at all)

**Resolver DB** (`resolver/db/schema.sql`): fact ingestion
- `facts_resolved` — Precedence-resolved facts (unique on ym, iso3, hazard_code, metric, series_semantics). Includes `alertlevel` column (GDACS-specific, NULL for other sources).
- `facts_deltas` — Monthly flow changes
- `snapshots`, `manifests` — Snapshot-export metadata, written only by the snapshot CLI (`resolver/tools/freeze_snapshot.py`), never by the pipeline; empty in the canonical DB by design. (`meta_runs`, `pm_checks`, `ipc_phases` and the two `gtmc1_*` tables were declared for a year and written by nothing; they are dropped when empty on schema init — `pythia.db.schema.DEAD_TABLES`.)
- `haz_*` — Hazard resolution machine tables (created by `haz-migrate` / `python -m resolver.hazard_resolution.migrate`, idempotent — and self-created by every machine entry point via `ensure_haz_schema`; **populated since 2026-08-05 by two workflows**: `resolver_update.yml` Phase 2.5 runs `resolve-hazards` over the trailing 3 months for all three hazards each cycle, and the nightly `haz_backcast.yml` fills history in time-boxed chunks — Phase 1 `haz_raw_ibtracs` for TC, Phase 2 `haz_raw_{gdacs,emdat,ifrc_go,idmc_idu}` + `haz_impact_candidates` for TC and FL, Phase 3 `haz_raw_reliefweb_docs` + `haz_doc_extractions` for triggered cells): `haz_raw_{ibtracs,gdacs,emdat,idmc_idu,ifrc_go,reliefweb_docs,ipc,drought_indicators,dfo,population}` thin per-source caches with retrieval timestamps and content hashes (identical re-fetch is a no-op, upstream revision appends, readers take the newest), `haz_triggers` (Layer-1 detection verdicts + evidence of absence), `haz_impact_candidates` (Layer-2 ladder inputs; `value_type` ∈ affected / displaced_lower_bound / exposed_ceiling — the last is GDACS exposure, filtered out of the ladder's choices; `preference_rank` + `detail_json` carry the extracted rung's own precedence and its verbatim quote), `haz_doc_extractions` (the LLM extraction cache AND monthly cost ledger, keyed doc+model+prompt_version+**cell** (iso3, hazard, year, month) since 2026-08-05 — the prompt names the cell, so an answer for one cell is not an answer for another; only `status='ok'` rows are cache hits, and only rows that billed tokens count toward the monthly cap), `haz_resolutions` (RESOLVED_ZERO/RESOLVED_VALUE/NO_DATA + provenance_json + rule_fired + flagged + **provisional** + **run_type** + frozen_at), `haz_revisions` (post-freeze audit, never alters resolved values), `haz_backcast_progress` (the backcast's per-(hazard, month) resume ledger; status `ok` / `failed` / `deferred`, the last with `deferred_cells` naming the ISO3s the extraction budget cut short), `haz_base_rates_{occurrence,severity}`. **`run_type` (`live`/`backcast`) is on `haz_resolutions` and `haz_triggers`** — provenance on the row, never part of the key: a cell has one answer, and the freeze guard decides whether a later run may replace it. The migrated column cannot carry the DDL's CHECK, so `schema.validate_run_type` enforces it in Python at every writer

## Resolver architecture

The Resolver was refactored in PR #610 to a connector-based architecture. Defunct legacy connectors (DTM, EM-DAT ingestion, HDX, IPC, ODP, ReliefWeb, UNHCR, WFP, WHO, WorldPop) were removed. The GDACS connector was re-implemented as a new Connector protocol source.

**Connector protocol** (`resolver/connectors/protocol.py`): Every data source implements a `Connector` protocol with a `name` attribute and a `fetch_and_normalize()` method that returns a DataFrame with exactly 21 canonical columns (event_id, iso3, hazard_code, metric, value, as_of_date, publisher, etc.). Connectors may include supplementary columns beyond the canonical set (e.g. `alertlevel` for GDACS) by passing `extra_columns` to `validate_canonical`; `run_pipeline` auto-detects and passes these through.

**Active connectors** (`resolver/connectors/__init__.py` REGISTRY):
- `acled` — ACLED conflict/fatalities data (wraps `resolver/ingestion/acled_client`)
- `idmc` — IDMC internal displacement data (wraps `resolver/ingestion/idmc/`)
- `ifrc_montandon` — IFRC GO connector (active; pulls Field Reports, Events, Appeals, and Situation Reports via `resolver/ingestion/ifrc_go_client.py`, writes `publisher='IFRC'` rows to `facts_resolved`). Tier-0 source for natural-hazard PA (FL, DR, TC). Three sources of an `affected` row, in precedence order: (1) the source record's `num_affected` (and `gov_`/`other_` variants) — `method='api'`, `confidence='med'`; (2) for IFRC GO Appeals (DREF + Emergency Appeals), the `num_beneficiaries` headline "people targeted" figure, promoted to `affected` by `_maybe_use_beneficiaries_as_affected` — `method='api'`, `confidence='high'`, event_id suffix `-appeal`, source-field marker `appeal:num_beneficiaries`; (3) when neither is available but at least 2 of `{fatalities, injured, displaced, missing}` are present, the sum is synthesised as a floor estimate by `_maybe_derive_affected` — `method='derived'`, `confidence='low'`, event_id suffix `-derived`, source-field marker `derived_sum:...`. The `iso3_pairs_from_go` helper accepts both the plural `countries_details` shape (Field Reports / Events span multiple countries) and the singular `country` dict (Appeals tie to one country). The `as_of_date` fallback chain prefers `report_date` → `disaster_start_date` → `start_date` (Appeals' funding-period start, ~the disaster date) → `updated_at` → `created_at`.
- `gdacs` — GDACS disaster population exposure (FL, DR, TC). No auth required. **Two data sources**: (1) static RSS feeds (`xml/rss_fl_3m.xml`, `xml/rss_tc_3m.xml`) for FL/TC in ≤3-month window (fast, has population data); (2) JSON search API (`gdacsapi/api/events/geteventlist/SEARCH`) + per-event RSS enrichment for >3-month backfill and **always for DR** (DR RSS feed returns 404). The original `rss.aspx?profile=ARCHIVE` endpoint is broken. Depth controlled by `GDACS_MONTHS` (default 3; use 135 for full backfill to 2015). Multi-country events use population-weighted allocation. TC zero-fills no-event months; FL/DR do not. **Two metrics per country-month-hazard**: `in_need` (population exposed, existing) and `event_occurrence` (binary 1/0 based on alertlevel: Orange/Red=1, Green=0). The `alertlevel` column (Green/Orange/Red) is stored as a supplementary column in `facts_resolved` (NULL for non-GDACS sources). Entry point: `resolver/ingestion/gdacs.py` (for `run_connectors.py`); also integrated into `pythia/tools/ingest_structured_data.py`. Env vars: `GDACS_MONTHS` (default 3), `GDACS_REQUEST_DELAY` (default 1.0s, applied per worker), `GDACS_ENRICH_WORKERS` (default 6; thread-pool size for per-event RSS enrichment, 1 = sequential), `GDACS_FORCE_RSS`/`GDACS_FORCE_JSON` (override auto-detection).
- `fewsnet_ipc` — FEWS NET IPC Phase 3+ population estimates (DR hazard). No auth required. Fetches from `https://fdw.fews.net/api/ipcpopulationsize.csv`. Two metrics: `phase3plus_in_need` (Current Situation, used for resolution) and `phase3plus_projection` (Most Likely, context for prompts). ISO2→ISO3 conversion via pycountry. Deduplicates by latest `reporting_date` per (iso3, scenario, month). Merges discovered countries into `resolver/data/fewsnet_countries.json` (union with the existing file — never shrinks; a short `FEWSNET_MONTHS` window only sees countries with a recent analysis cycle) for question generator + IPC-exclusion consumption. Entry point: `resolver/ingestion/fewsnet_ipc.py`; also integrated into `pythia/tools/ingest_structured_data.py`. Env vars: `FEWSNET_MONTHS` (default 12; 120 for backfill to 2016), `FEWSNET_REQUEST_DELAY` (default 1.0s).
- `ipc_api` — IPC API Phase 3+ population estimates for non-FEWS NET countries (DR hazard). Requires `IPC_API_KEY`. Fetches from `https://api.ipcinfo.org/population`. Same two metrics as fewsnet_ipc. Excludes countries in `resolver/data/fewsnet_countries.json` (FEWS NET always takes priority). Merges discovered countries into `resolver/data/ipc_countries.json` (union, never shrinks) — note the file is runner-local and never committed, so the question generator's DR eligibility relies on its DB fallback, not this file. Entry point: `resolver/ingestion/ipc_api.py`; also integrated into `pythia/tools/ingest_structured_data.py`. Env vars: `IPC_API_KEY` (required), `IPC_API_MONTHS` (default 24), `IPC_API_REQUEST_DELAY` (default 1.0s).

**Pipeline orchestrator** (`resolver/tools/run_pipeline.py`):
```
discover_connectors() -> fetch_and_normalize() per connector
  -> validate_canonical(extra_columns auto-detected) -> enrich() + derive_ym()
  -> precedence_engine (tiered source resolution, publisher used as source) -> make_deltas()
  -> write to DuckDB (facts_resolved + facts_deltas, supplementary columns preserved)
```

**Precedence policy** (`resolver/tools/precedence_config.yml`):
- Tier 0: IFRC Montandon/ACLED (highest priority; IFRC Montandon is the active source for natural hazard PA: FL, DR, TC)
- Tier 1: IDMC
- Tier 2: EM-DAT (historical read-only, no active connector; replaced by IFRC Montandon)
- **Tier entries must match the lowercased `publisher` values connectors write** (`run_pipeline` uses publisher as the precedence source id) — tier 0 lists both `ifrc` (what the connector writes) and the legacy `ifrc_montandon` slug. The YAML carries `tiebreak: [as_of_desc, value_nonnull, source_alpha]` and `defaults: {require_nonnegative, prefer_full_row_coverage}`. **`resolve_facts_frame` is the single engine** — the pre-connector-era standalone CLI (`--facts/--cutoff`, with its `metric_preference`/`source_precedence`/`source_mapping`/`conflict_rule` config keys) was removed in the July 2026 S4 consolidation; recognized config keys are exactly `tiers`/`tiebreak`/`defaults`/`metrics`. (`tools/precedence_engine.py` at the repo ROOT is a different library — `apply_precedence`, used by the IDMC ingestion CLI with the root `tools/precedence_config.yml` — and is unrelated.) When adding a connector whose rows can collide with tiered sources on the same (iso3, hazard, ym, metric), add its publisher slug to a tier.

**Transform adapters** (`resolver/transform/adapters/`): ACLED and IDMC adapters normalize source-specific schemas to the common format used by the precedence engine.

**NMME seasonal forecasts** (`resolver/ingestion/nmme.py` + `resolver/tools/ingest_nmme.py`):
Separate from the Connector pipeline. Fetches NMME ensemble mean anomalies from CPC FTP, computes area-weighted country averages using xarray + regionmask (`countries_10` resolution for 170+ countries), and writes to the `seasonal_forecasts` table in Pythia DB. Injected into HS triage/RC prompts via `horizon_scanner/seasonal_context.py` (`climate_data` kwarg) and into forecaster prompts via `research_json["nmme_seasonal_outlook"]`. **Now integrated into `pythia/tools/ingest_structured_data.py`** as the `nmme` source (NMME failures are caught as warnings, non-fatal, since FTP files are published ~9th-10th of each month). The standalone `python -m resolver.tools.ingest_nmme` entry point still works independently. The standalone `ingest-nmme.yml` workflow was removed (July 2026) — NMME now runs only via `resolver_update.yml` Phase 3 and `ingest-structured-data.yml`. Uses `decode_times=False` in `xr.open_dataset()` calls because the new CPC multi-lead files use `months since 1960-01-02 21:00:00` as time units, which xarray cannot decode.

**Conflict forecast connectors** (`resolver/connectors/views.py`, `resolver/connectors/conflictforecast.py`, `resolver/connectors/acled_cast.py`):
Separate from the Connector pipeline (use `FORECAST_REGISTRY`, not `REGISTRY`). VIEWS connector fetches ML-based fatality predictions from the VIEWS API (`views_predicted_fatalities`, `views_p_gte25_brd`, leads 1–6). conflictforecast.org connector fetches news-based risk scores from Backendless API (`cf_armed_conflict_risk_3m`, `cf_armed_conflict_risk_12m`, `cf_violence_intensity_3m`). ACLED CAST connector fetches event-count forecasts via OAuth2 API (`cast_total_events`, `cast_battles_events`, `cast_erv_events`, `cast_vac_events`, 6-month lead), aggregated from admin1 to country level. All three write to `conflict_forecasts` table. `fetch_and_store` deduplicates before writing (keeps only the latest `forecast_issue_date` per source), and `_write_to_db` prunes old vintages (keeps only the 2 most recent issue dates per source) and then **re-stamps `is_stale_vintage` / `vintage_age_days` on EVERY row** (`stamp_vintage_flags`, from each source's own threshold in `STALENESS_THRESHOLD_DAYS`, all 45 days) — a stale vintage is a fact on the row, never only a log line, and the age moves with the calendar. **Every prompt reader in `horizon_scanner/conflict_forecasts.py` serves only rows whose `target_month` has not passed** (`SERVED_TARGET_FILTER_SQL`, imported by the bundle's `no_conflict_forecast_served_has_a_target_month_in_the_past` check), never a lead-months window: lead is computed from the issue date, so on CAST's frozen December 2025 vintage "leads 1 to 3" in September 2026 meant January to March 2026. `score_views.py` is the one reader exempt by design (it grades forecasts against outcomes, so every row it wants has a past target month). **The CAST connector has no unfiltered fallback** (the unfiltered endpoint pages the archive oldest first, so the fallback would have ingested 2023-24 rows and reported success), deduplicates on `(country, admin1, month, year)` before its country SUM and logs the distinct-key count beside the raw count, logs the target months a vintage actually carries (December 2025 carries five, January to May 2026; June is absent from the API), names every country-month dropped by the ISO3 map or the lead filter and every country carrying fewer months than the vintage (`connector.summary`, carried into the connectors report as `extras.connector_summary`), and reads epoch timestamps in UTC. Loaded into ACE prompts via `horizon_scanner/conflict_forecasts.py`. The conflictforecast.org connector uses suffix-priority column selection: when multiple columns match a metric pattern (e.g. `ons_armedconf_03`), it prefers `_all` (combined forecast) over `_text`/`_hist` sub-models, and explicitly skips `_target` (ground truth, NaN for future) and `_naive` (baseline). Also includes a Backendless metadata skip set and a median-value sanity check (onset metrics only; intensity metrics use log-scale values). **Now integrated into `pythia/tools/ingest_structured_data.py`** as sources `views`, `conflictforecast`, `acledcast` (with `conflict` as a convenience alias for all three). The standalone `python -m resolver.tools.fetch_conflict_forecasts` entry point still works independently.

**ENSO state and forecast** (`horizon_scanner/enso/`, rebuilt Sept 2026):
The phase is **computed from a machine-readable index, never scraped**. `indices.py` walks three numeric sources in rank order and `classify_oni` applies NOAA's operational definition; the IRI Quick Look supplies the 9-season probability table, the plume, the IOD and the narrative, and its own statement of the phase is kept as `scraped_state` for comparison only. Injected into RC and triage prompts for DR, FL, HW, TC via `get_enso_prompt_context()`.

**Why the design changed.** Until Sept 2026 the connector asked the rendered page for a classification and believed it. In August 2026 that stored "Neutral" through a strong El Niño, and every drought and cyclone prompt in the run read it as current. Guarding a scrape produces an empty row rather than a wrong one, which is not the same as being right — so the classification became arithmetic over a published index.

**Write policy: the table gets a row every run.** `fresh` when a numeric source answered and the move is ordinary. `carried_forward` when none answered, or when the move is extraordinary and no second source confirms it — the last good record re-written under its ORIGINAL observation date, with `age_days` on the row and a STALE READING line opening the prompt block. Neutral is a computed result and is never what the code writes when it does not know; with no previous record and no reading, NOTHING is written, because an absent row is honest and a defaulted Neutral is not.

**Continuity.** An ONI step over `ONI_JUMP_LIMIT` (1.0 °C) since the last record, or a phase transition skipping Neutral, calls `indices.corroborate()` for a reading from a rank the first answer did not use. Agreement within `ONI_AGREEMENT_TOLERANCE` (0.5 °C) writes the move; disagreement or silence carries the previous record forward, because the cheapest explanation for an implausible jump is a changed column. `cross_check_seasonal_tc` is a free second opinion: the TSR and BoM outlooks already cached in this run carry ENSO notes in prose, and in August 2026 they disagreed with the stored record while nothing compared them.

**DB-first pattern unchanged**: `fetch_and_store_enso()` is called by `resolver_update.yml` Phase 4, preceded now by a `--backfill-oni` step (the whole published ONI table, 1950 to present, in one small request — idempotent, and what gives the continuity check something to compare against). `get_enso_prompt_context()` reads from DB first; the DB loader measures staleness against `observation_date` where there is one, since a carried-forward row's `fetch_date` stays current while its reading ages. (The standalone `refresh-enso.yml` workflow was removed July 2026 — it only uploaded an unconsumed run-scoped artifact; Phase 4 does the real fetch+store.)

**Unverified from the build environment**: the three numeric endpoints could not be reached (the sandbox proxy denies NOAA and CPC hosts, and web.archive.org). The 2026-09-04 repairs (ERDDAP asked for every column, rank 2 moved to `wksst9120.for`) rest on the ERDDAP info page's column spellings as reported by search and on CPC's base-period change, not on a live response. A wrong URL or changed shape fails LOUD and SAFE — the rank is recorded as failed with the server's own reason (`_default_get` now names the status and body), the ladder moves on, and a total failure carries the previous record forward rather than defaulting. Verify on the first live run via the Phase 4 logs, which name every rank's answer, and via the `enso_ladder_has_two_live_ranks_in_the_last_30_days` contradiction check in the resolver debug bundle.

**Seasonal TC forecasts** (`horizon_scanner/seasonal_tc/`):
Aggregates basin-level seasonal TC forecasts from TSR (PDF extraction), NOAA CPC (press release scraping), BoM (outlook scraping), Météo-France La Réunion (SWI seasonal outlook article, `mfr_swio_scraper.py`), and an IMD/NIO climatology-first context block (`imd_nio_scraper.py`) across 8 basins. Country-to-basin mapping in `__init__.py`. Cached as JSON; `get_seasonal_tc_context_for_country(iso3)` returns prompt-ready text. DB-first pattern: `fetch_and_store_seasonal_tc()` is called by `resolver_update.yml` Phase 4 (fetches fresh and persists to `seasonal_tc_outlooks` + `seasonal_tc_context_cache` tables); `get_seasonal_tc_context_for_country()` reads from DB first, falls back to cached JSON. (The standalone `refresh-seasonal-tc.yml` workflow was removed July 2026 — it only uploaded an unconsumed run-scoped artifact; Phase 4 does the real fetch+store.) The HS workflow no longer downloads the TC artifact separately — TC data is in the DB artifact.

**HDX Signals** (`horizon_scanner/hdx_signals.py`):
Downloads OCHA's HDX Signals CSV from CKAN API. Indicator-to-hazard mapping (acled_conflict→ACE, ipc_food_insecurity→DR, etc.). Filtered by country, hazard, recency (180 days). Injected into RC and triage prompts for all hazards via `format_hdx_signals_for_prompt()`. DB-first pattern: `bulk_fetch_and_store_hdx_signals()` persists signals to `hdx_signals` table during backfill; `format_hdx_signals_for_prompt()` reads from DB first, falls back to live CSV download.

**ICG CrisisWatch** (`horizon_scanner/crisiswatch.py` + `scripts/refresh_crisiswatch.py`):
Monthly fetch of ICG CrisisWatch data. **Primary source**: local JSON file (`horizon_scanner/data/crisiswatch_latest.json`) produced by `scripts/refresh_crisiswatch.py`. **The refresh runs in CI via the Wayback Machine** (July 2026): crisisgroup.org's Cloudflare blocks CI-runner Playwright fetches, but the page is fully server-rendered and archive.org's crawler gets through — so `refresh-crisiswatch.yml` (cron 3rd/5th/7th/10th monthly, 06:17 UTC) runs `python -m scripts.refresh_crisiswatch --source wayback --only-if-newer --max-age-days 60 --expect-edition-by-day 7`. **It no longer commits the JSON** (Aug 2026): branch protection began rejecting the bot's push to `main` around 2026-08-06 (GH006, "Changes must be made through a pull request"), so every run since scraped successfully and then went red at the commit step — while the checked-in edition froze at 2026-07-29 and `resolver_update.yml` Phase 4 carried on storing that stale edition into `crisiswatch_entries` behind a green step. The consumer is the DB, not the file, so **Phase 4 now runs the same Wayback refresh on its own runner** (`continue-on-error`, 30-min timeout, degrading to the checked-in edition) immediately before `bulk_store_crisiswatch()`, where no push is involved; this workflow keeps its cron and its artifact as the early-month monitoring path and is now `contents: read`. Do not reintroduce a push here. The wayback path: best-effort Save Page Now capture request (authenticated when the optional `IA_S3_ACCESS_KEY`/`IA_S3_SECRET_KEY` secrets are set; CDX is polled afterwards even when the SPN request times out, because anonymous SPN renders the capture synchronously and often succeeds server-side after the client gives up) → CDX snapshot walk newest-first → gzip-sniffed raw `id_` download (`_download_snapshot_html`) → validation (`_accept_snapshot_html`: ≥20KB AND ≥20 `c-crisiswatch-entry` divs, rejecting Cloudflare-challenged captures) → the existing parser. **The download and validation halves are deliberately separate** because their failure modes differ: a freshly-created SPN capture is routinely indexed before it is *servable*, so a download failure gets patient retries (`_FRESH_CAPTURE_ATTEMPTS=6`, `_FRESH_CAPTURE_BACKOFF_SEC=20`, `_FRESH_CAPTURE_TIMEOUT_SEC=45`), while a capture that downloads and then fails validation is Cloudflare-challenged and will never improve, so it falls back immediately. Job timeout is 30 min to cover the worst case. `--only-if-newer` compares the parsed **edition month** against the on-disk JSON and skips the write when not strictly newer (idempotent cron; never regresses to an older edition); `--max-age-days 60` turns runs red when the on-disk edition ends >60 days ago. **`--max-age-days` alone cannot see a single missed monthly edition** (it measures the age of the edition's END date, so a one-month gap sails under the threshold) — that is what `--expect-edition-by-day 7` is for: it asks whether the previous complete month's edition is present, warning inside the grace period and turning the run red after it. Note `fetched_at` now marks edition acquisition (no-op runs don't advance it), so the loader's 45-day staleness warning can fire mid-cycle — harmless, data is still used. The local Playwright path (`--source live`, the default; `--channel chrome` recommended) is kept for manual refreshes. Tests: `tests/test_refresh_crisiswatch.py` (fixture-based, no network), run by `crisiswatch-ci.yml` with the same minimal deps as the refresh workflow (requests + beautifulsoup4 + PyYAML — a new heavy import in the script's import chain breaks CI instead of silently breaking the monthly refresh via the ImportError stub that disables ISO3 resolution; the script also hard-fails when 0 entries resolve to ISO3). The `/crisiswatch/print` endpoint is broken (stale Oct 2019 data); the main page renders correctly. **Fallback**: Gemini grounding calls (`_call_gemini_grounding` in `crisiswatch.py`) — only attempted when the JSON file is missing or empty (stale-but-present JSON is used with a warning). Called once per HS run, cached in-memory, persisted to `crisiswatch_entries` DuckDB table. Injected into ACE RC prompts (via `crisiswatch_context` + deprecated `icg_on_the_horizon`), ACE triage prompts, and ACE SPD prompts (SPD render block added July 2026 — it was previously loaded but never rendered).
