Country name → ISO3 mapping via hardcoded `_ICG_COUNTRY_ISO3` dict. **Several scraped rows can resolve to one ISO3** — a regional heading the scraper expands via `_REGIONAL_ENTRY_MAP` (Nile Waters → ETH/SDN/EGY), a bilateral heading resolved by name (`China-U.S.` → CHN), or a territory (`Somaliland` → SOM) — so `_load_from_json` MERGES them through `_merge_country_rows` rather than assigning (strongest `arrow`/`alert_type` wins; identity from the country's own entry, marked by the scraper's `regional_source` stamp; every distinct summary kept and labelled, bounded by `_MERGED_SUMMARY_MAX_CHARS`). It is the single chokepoint: `bulk_store_crisiswatch` feeds off it, so the same merge protects `crisiswatch_entries`. Staleness: the debug bundle health check FAILs when the latest entry month is >2 months old (the July 2026 run was silently serving February data) and lists every edition the table holds; **since 2026-09-03 `format_crisiswatch_for_prompt` prepends a STALENESS WARNING to any entry three or more editions old** (`crisiswatch_edition_age_months`, threshold `_STALE_EDITION_MONTHS`), so a stale edition is labelled in the RC, triage and SPD prompts rather than read as current. **Three runners now attempt the Wayback refresh + store each cycle**: `resolver_update.yml` Phase 4 (28th), the weekly `ingest-structured-data.yml` (scheduled runs, or the `refresh_crisiswatch` input) and the `hs_submit` stage of `pythia_pipeline_stage.yml` (best effort, 15-min cap) — all `continue-on-error`, all `--only-if-newer`, so the edition can only move forward.

Run the pipeline: `python -m resolver.tools.run_pipeline [--connectors acled idmc] [--db path/to/resolver.duckdb]`

## People-affected resolution machine (`resolver/hazard_resolution/`, Aug 2026 — Phases 1-6 live)

A parallel resolution system being built to replace the IFRC-GO-only PA resolution path (~4% of PA questions resolve per month; target ≥80% resolving to a number including zero). Two layers: **detection** (did a qualifying hazard occur in this country-month? no + silent ReliefWeb sweep → `RESOLVED_ZERO` with evidence of absence) and **impact** (fixed precedence ladder EM-DAT → LLM-extracted ReliefWeb figures → IFRC GO → IDMC IDU lower bound; GDACS is detection/ceiling only, NEVER a resolution value; drought resolves against IPC instead). Phase 0 (PR #843) scaffolded the package — rulebook + strict validator, `rules.py` deterministic predicates, `haz_*` schema, population loader; Phase 1 the cyclone detection path; **Phase 2 the flood detector (GDACS) and the whole of Layer 2** — the ladder, its four source connectors, the deterministic reconciler, and the provisional/freeze lifecycle; **Phase 3 ladder rung 2** — ReliefWeb document selection, LLM transcription of stated figures, and the deterministic post-processing that turns them into candidates; **Phase 4 the drought path** — the IPC Phase 3+ delta rule, its two IPC acquisition paths and the drought-indicator attribution gate (no ladder, no LLM, no spend); **Phase 5 the backcast** and the base rates, DFO cross-check and acceptance report derived from it; **Phase 6 the loop closed** — those base rates, plus a rulebook-generated description of the resolution process, injected into the SPD prompt of every PA question the machine resolves. Full doc: `resolver/hazard_resolution/README.md`.

**Hard rules future phases must preserve**: (1) every resolution row carries provenance in `haz_resolutions.provenance_json` (source, record ids/URLs, retrieval timestamp, rule fired — enforced by tests); (2) reconciliation is deterministic, no LLM calls — `reconcile.reconcile` is a pure function of (candidates, population, rulebook, today), which is what makes this testable; (3) LLM extraction extracts stated figures only, Haiku-class models, and admissibility is enforced by QUOTE VERIFICATION against the sent text rather than by the prompt's instructions; (4) resolutions freeze at month-end + `freeze_days` (60) and are NEVER reopened — post-freeze re-runs log to `haz_revisions` and leave the row untouched; (5) every threshold lives in `rulebook.yaml` (strictly validated on load, all problems reported at once, credential-looking keys rejected — API keys are env-only: `RELIEFWEB_APPNAME`, `EMDAT_API_KEY`, `IDMC_API_KEY`); (6) trigger decisions go through the `rules.py` predicates (`cyclone_track_qualifies`, `flood_alert_qualifies`, `freeze_deadline`, `orders_of_magnitude_apart`, …) so changing the YAML changes behaviour with no code change.

**Phase 1 mechanics**: `resolve-hazards --hazard cyclone --month YYYY-MM` (poetry script; module form `python -m resolver.hazard_resolution.cli`) fetches IBTrACS v04r01 CSV (last3years default, `--scope ALL` backcast) and stores it in the generic `haz_raw_ibtracs` cache as **one row per storm** (record_id = SID, full track in `payload_json`, `content_hash` makes identical re-fetches a no-op while upstream best-track revisions append rows; detection reads the newest revision). A country-month triggers when any track point ≥ `cyclone.min_wind_kt` (34) lies within `cyclone.buffer_km` (200) of territory — distances via AEQD projection centred on the track point against a **vendored** slimmed Natural Earth 1:50m Admin-0 layer (`data/ne_50m_admin_0_countries.slim.geojson.gz`; 50m not 110m because 110m drops the TC-exposed island states; vendored so CI is network-free; regenerate via `data/build_boundaries.py`). `haz_triggers` gets a row for BOTH triggered and non-triggered country-months (year/month INTs). Non-triggered months get a two-query ReliefWeb sweep (disaster-type filter, then keywords; window month-start → month-end + `publication_pad_days`); silent → zero (sweep record also lands in the trigger row's `evidence_of_absence_json`); hits → `triggered=true, trigger_source='reliefweb_sweep'` for the Phase 2 ladder; API failure → inconclusive, never a zero (fail-closed). **Zero-safety coverage gate**: zeros are suppressed unless the newest stored IBTrACS point reaches `month_end - coverage_grace_days` — an ingestion gap must never read as a quiet month (same principle as the `source_coverage` month gate).

**Phase 2 mechanics**: `resolve-hazards --hazard flood --month YYYY-MM` (also `--months A B C` for a multi-month run; `--no-ladder` for detection+zeros only). **Flood detection** reads GDACS events from `haz_raw_gdacs` and triggers a country-month when an event naming that country, overlapping that month, is at or above `flood.gdacs_trigger_level` (green<orange<red), via `rules.flood_alert_qualifies`; an unrecognised colour is recorded as a data problem and does NOT trigger. Same ReliefWeb sweep, fail-closed handling and coverage gate as cyclones (`flood.gdacs.coverage_grace_days`).

**Event-to-month attribution** is two different decisions and they must not be conflated: **detection** uses overlap (a flood running 28 Jan–4 Feb triggers both months), **the figure** is attributed wholly to the START month (`event_attribution.figure: start_month`). Figures are NEVER split — a source states one number for the event and apportioning it would invent a monthly breakdown nobody reported; the full span rides along in provenance as `event_span`.

**The ladder** (`candidates.py` → `reconcile.py` → `resolutions.py`, orchestrated by `impact.py`): candidates are collected per cell into `haz_impact_candidates` with three value types — `affected` (EM-DAT, IFRC GO), `displaced_lower_bound` (IDMC IDU), `exposed_ceiling` (GDACS). `ladder_candidates()` filters the ceiling out of the ladder's view, which is the code-level guard that modelled exposure can never be published as reported impact. The first populated rung wins; within a rung the largest stated figure wins (ties break on `source_ref`, so the choice is stable across runs). A lower rung that disagrees **flags, never substitutes** — `conflict_rule: ladder_with_flag`. Flags: `ceiling_exceeded` (> `sanity.ceiling_multiplier` × the effective ceiling), `population_cap_exceeded`, `adjacent_rung_order_of_magnitude` (> `conflict_detection.order_of_magnitude_factor`, default 10, between adjacent POPULATED rungs), `no_candidate_past_freeze`.

**The effective ceiling (Sept 2026).** GDACS exposure when there is one; a share of the national population (`sanity.population_fallback_share`, 0.5) when there is not; `none` otherwise — and `provenance.decision.ceiling.basis` records which, because a flag raised against a population share is a different statement from one raised against a GDACS footprint. Two repairs behind this:

* **A non-positive exposure means UNKNOWN, never a ceiling of zero.** GDACS discovery carries no population figure at all; it is filled in by a per-event RSS fetch that tolerates 404s and network errors, leaving the field at its 0.0 default. Read as a ceiling that rejected every figure for the event, and did so most often for the events GDACS knew least about — in the August 2026 run **147 of 199 rejected extracted figures were rejected against a ceiling of zero**. Both `reconcile._ceiling` and `candidates.exposure_ceiling` (the stricter one extraction uses) now filter non-positive exposures out.
* **`sanity.ceiling_multiplier` moved 1.0 → 3.0.** At 1.0 there was no room between a modelled estimate and a reported one, so any report above the model was flagged — and reported impact routinely exceeds modelled exposure for good reasons: the footprint is a hazard envelope rather than an impact assessment, gridded population is coarse, and "affected" counts people beyond those inside the envelope. The ceiling exists to catch a mis-transcription, not to hold reports to a model's arithmetic.
* **An exposure below `sanity.min_plausible_exposure` (1000 people) is UNKNOWN, exactly as a zero is** (Sept 2026, run 33841370196). The figures ledger carried three distinct ceilings across 310 rows — 5, 20 and 955, i.e. exposures of 1.67, 6.67 and 318 people for national monthly flood totals — and 72 well-sourced figures were rejected against them ("306 houses were affected, impacting 1,917 people" against a ceiling of 20). `rules.usable_exposure` is the single test, applied by `within_sanity_ceiling`, `reconcile._ceiling` and `candidates.exposure_ceiling_basis` (which now also reports `n_events_below_plausible_floor` and `exposure_units_seen`). Below the floor the population-share fallback and the national cap are the bounds in force. **The unit hypothesis could not be verified from the sandbox** (gdacs.org is denied): the public GDACS RSS fixtures show `gdacs:population` carrying a `unit` that names the MEASURE (`Pop74`, `Population in 100km`, `""`, and `people` on the flood feed) beside a `value` in people and an explanatory text — so `resolver/connectors/gdacs.py::parse_gdacs_population` passes people-units through, scales a multiplicative unit (`Million`, `Thousand`), treats any other unit as UNKNOWN with a warning, and the connector and `haz_raw_gdacs` now keep `population_unit`/`population_text`/`population_raw`/`population_parse` (`exposed_population_*` in the machine's payload) verbatim, so the next run's bundle shows what the feed said. **A corrected ceiling reaches the rows it would have changed**: `resolve-hazards --hazard {flood,cyclone} --reconsider-rejected` (`impact.reconsider_rejected_cells`, run by Phase 2.5 on every Resolver Update) finds every resolved cell whose provenance carries an `exceeds_gdacs_exposure_ceiling` rejection and replays the ladder for it from the caches — no model call, no document fetch; unfrozen cells are rewritten, frozen cells log a `haz_revisions` row (they are never reopened), and the summary JSON says which.

**GDACS country resolution (Sept 2026).** GDACS leaves `affectedcountries` empty while a tropical cyclone is still over open ocean, and a record with no country is written and then read by no cell — 16 TC events were dropped that way in the 2026-08 run (ids 1001273..1001315). The connector now keeps the GeoJSON feature's coordinates (`_feature_point`), and `gdacs._iso3s_near_event` resolves countries within `_GEOMETRY_ATTRIBUTION_KM` (500) against the same vendored Natural Earth boundaries cyclone detection uses, nearest-first. The boundary layer is loaded at most ONCE per month via `_CountryGeometries` (parsing the 1:50m file costs a few hundred milliseconds, which is fine per month and not per event) and only when an event actually needs placing — deliberately not a module-level cache, since the tests inject synthetic geometries by monkeypatching the loader and a process-wide cache would serve the first test's boundaries to every later one. A stated country is never overridden; an unplaceable event keeps none; the derived attribution is recorded on the row as `iso3_from_geometry`, because a derived claim is weaker than a stated one.

**No candidate → the clock decides**: before the freeze deadline the verdict is `PENDING` and **no row is written at all** (EM-DAT lands weeks late; writing NO_DATA the week of a flood records our impatience as a fact about the world); on or after it, `NO_DATA` + flagged. `PENDING` is not a stored status — the `haz_resolutions` CHECK constraint would reject it — and the literal is duplicated in `resolutions.WRITE_PENDING_STATUS` to avoid an import cycle, pinned by a test.

**Provisional**: the resolver core has no provisional concept (`facts_resolved.confidence` is source quality, not finality), so `haz_resolutions` gained its own `provisional BOOLEAN` plus an idempotent `ALTER ... ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS` migration in `schema._COLUMN_MIGRATIONS` (the ALTER omits NOT NULL — DuckDB refuses that on a populated table — so **readers must COALESCE**). Rows written pre-deadline are provisional and may be revised; from the deadline they are final and immutable.

**Source connectors** (`gdacs.py`, `emdat.py`, `ifrc_go.py`, `idmc_idu.py`, on the shared `sources.py` raw-cache plumbing): each returns a `FetchOutcome` and **never raises**, because "the source said nothing happened" and "we could not read the source" are different facts and only the first can justify a zero — an unread rung is recorded on the resolution as `decision.sources_unavailable`. **Since Sept 2026 EM-DAT distinguishes a THIRD state**: a failed live call (or a missing key) with a populated cache covering the window returns `ok=True` with `detail.served_from_cache` and the live error preserved — the rung is STALE, not unread, so it still answers and the row says where the answer came from. `api.emdat.be` returned 500 on all six month-hazard passes of the 2026-08 run, and every impact decision lost its top rung while `haz_raw_emdat` held the previous pull. A failed call with an EMPTY cache is still `ok=False`, which is the same error in the other direction. All share the Phase 1 content-hash idiom (identical re-fetch is a no-op, upstream revision appends, readers take the newest). **Reuse, deliberately**: GDACS event discovery/enrichment borrows `resolver/connectors/gdacs.py` (`GdacsConnector._search_events`/`._enrich_with_population`, `_build_session`, `_load_countries`) and IFRC GO borrows `ifrc_go_client.req_json`/`iso3_pairs_from_go`. **Consequently the GDACS endpoints live in that connector, NOT in `rulebook.yaml`** — duplicating a URL would create two sources of truth for one address; `gdacs._connector_api()` raises a clear AttributeError if a borrowed helper is renamed, guarded by a test.

**Phase 3 mechanics (rung 2 — the ONLY paid step)**: `reliefweb_docs.py` → `extract.py` → `figures.py`, orchestrated per TRIGGERED cell by `impact.extract_rung_for_cell`; `--no-extract` disables it entirely. **A document supplies a figure only to the cell it is ABOUT (Sept 2026)**: `figures.apply_attribution` runs before the ceiling and rejects `document_primary_country_mismatch` (the document's `primary_country.iso3` is not the cell's — ReliefWeb files a regional round-up under every country it mentions and the API's `country.iso3` filter matches any of them, which is how two March-to-May flood reports about 82,000 people landed on AFG / FL / 2026-07) and `outside_reporting_window` (the date the figure is about — its own stated date, else the document's `date.original`, else `date.created` — outside month start .. month end + `publication_pad_days`; an undated figure is kept). Both are rulebook switches (`reliefweb.documents.require_primary_country`, `enforce_reporting_window`), and the cached document now carries `primary_country_iso3`, `country_iso3s` and `date_original` (a pre-September cache entry has none and passes the country test). The bundle check `no_accepted_figure_lies_outside_its_cell_reporting_window` re-runs the window and country tests over the ledger's accepted rows. **The figures ledger is honest about units**: every row carries `stated_value`/`stated_unit` (what the source said), `value_persons` (a whole number — households × multiplier is rounded, so 15,600 × 4.1 is 63,960 and never 63,959.99999999) and `conversion_factor`, beside `figure_date`, `doc_date`, `doc_date_original` and `doc_primary_country`; `value` keeps its legacy meaning (people, as the ladder uses them). **Document selection** queries ReliefWeb once per cell (country + the hazard's sweep disaster types + `reliefweb.documents.formats` + month window + `publication_pad_days`), ranks hits by `documents.source_priority` (OCHA → governments → UN → …, ties on recency then doc id) and caches the top `max_docs_per_cell` (30) in `haz_raw_reliefweb_docs` with the body **already truncated** to `body_char_limit` — the quote verifier checks against the text that was sent, so storing more than was sent would let an unverifiable quote pass later. **Extraction** is one call per document; the model transcribes stated people-affected figures with a verbatim quote, unit (`people`/`households`), `stated_by`, area, date and `cumulative_or_new`, and is forbidden to estimate, convert or sum. `parse_response` is strict: non-numeric values, unknown units (never coerced to `people`), missing quotes and quotes not found in the body are each rejected WITH A REASON, because a silent drop and "the document stated nothing" are indistinguishable downstream. **Post-processing** (`figures.py`, pure) converts households with the rulebook multiplier and records the conversion, **rejects** figures above the GDACS exposure ceiling (deliberately stricter than `reconcile`, which flags and keeps — an extracted figure over modelled exposure is far likelier a mis-transcription than a discovery), collapses restatements (by quote, then by value+area), and ranks survivors by `reliefweb.authority_precedence` (government > un_agency > ifrc_ngo > media; unmatched ranks last but is NOT discarded) then recency. Survivors land in `haz_impact_candidates` with the quote/doc_url/extraction_model in the new `detail_json` column plus `preference_rank` (0 = preferred), which `reconcile._best_on_rung` honours instead of largest-wins. **Cost guards**: per-cell doc cap; `extraction.max_calls_per_month` (4000 since Sept 2026) counted over `haz_doc_extractions` rows created in the current CALENDAR month so the cap spans runs; `extraction.backcast_max_calls_per_month` (2000) as the backcast's own share; an optional `extraction.max_calls_per_run` (absent by default) capping one run whatever the month's remainder says, for a live pass that must fit a workflow step; `ExtractionBudget.binding_limit` names whichever of the four bound; **`extraction.live_reserve_calls` (1500), calls the backcast may never take whatever its share says**; and `extraction.skip_when_higher_rung_populated` (default true) skips any cell EM-DAT already answers, since rung 2 cannot outrank it. The reserve was implicit until Sept 2026 — whatever the total minus the share happened to be — so raising or dropping the share would have removed it with nothing to notice; stated outright, the validator holds `live_reserve_calls + backcast_max_calls_per_month <= max_calls_per_month`. It exists because the backcast spends any budget it is given: in August 2026 it took the whole allowance by mid-month and cyclone August then ran out at Jamaica, with documents fetched and never read. A LIVE run checks only the total — the reserve is for its benefit, so it does not also bind it. `haz_doc_extractions` is both the cache (keyed doc+model+`prompt_version`, so a re-run is free and bumping the version is what invalidates it) and the ledger. A capped cell records `budget_capped` in provenance — its documents are UNREAD, not silent. The model is addressed by ROLE (`extraction.model_role: hazard_extraction` → `pythia/config.yaml` roles → `_ROLE_FALLBACKS`, Haiku-class), never by id. ~$0.009/call × 4000 ≈ $36/month, inside the $50 ceiling the rulebook names.

**Phase 4 mechanics (drought — no ladder, no LLM, no spend)**: `resolve-hazards --hazard drought --month YYYY-MM` (`--no-ladder`/`--no-extract`/`--no-sweep` are accepted and logged as inapplicable). `ipc.py` → `drought_indicators.py` → `drought.py`. The rule: **people affected = max(0, Phase3+(analysis covering the month) − Phase3+(previous current-period analysis))**, admitted as DROUGHT impact only where the indicators show drought. `decide_drought` is a pure function of (analyses, indicator verdict, rulebook, population, today) — the drought-side equivalent of `reconcile.reconcile`.

- **IPC analyses** (`haz_raw_ipc`, one row per ANALYSIS not per month): two acquisition paths, `ipc_api` (the IPC public API with the documented `key=` param, `IPC_API_KEY`) and `facts_resolved` (the `phase3plus_in_need` rows the FEWS NET / IPC connectors already write, where `ym` is the window's first month and `as_of_date` its last). The second needs **no key** and covers the FEWS NET countries `resolver/connectors/ipc_api.py` deliberately EXCLUDES — the countries where drought resolution matters most. An analysis seen by both collapses per validity window per `drought.ipc.source_priority`. Only the CURRENT period is cached (the projection is a forecast, never a resolution source). Period-date parsing is **borrowed** from `resolver.connectors.ipc_api._parse_period_dates` via `ipc._ipc_connector_api()`, guarded by a test — same reuse contract as `gdacs._connector_api()`.
- **Indicators** (`haz_raw_drought_indicators`): one fetch per feed per run, cached as **values not verdicts** so a retuned threshold changes behaviour with no re-fetch. Guards: 0-of-N country resolution REFUSES the snapshot (the feed's shape changed; that is not "no drought anywhere"), `max_observation_age_months` bounds staleness (a dateless HTTP feed is stamped with its RETRIEVAL month, so backcast months correctly fail the test), and a LATER snapshot is never read backwards. `combine: any|all`.
  - **Four providers.** `asap` (JRC ASAP hotspot classes; since 2026-09-04 the shipped entry is `asap_hotspots`, the monthly hotspot ASSESSMENT time series since Oct 2016 — `time_series: true`, one snapshot per month, `hs_code 3` "not assessed" is no reading, absence is UNKNOWN because it assesses ~80 countries, HDX mirror via `hdx-ckan://asap-hotspots-monthly` — while the legacy warnings entry `asap` is dormant with no url); `tabular` (a pre-computed `(iso3, ym, value)` CSV/JSON anomaly feed vs a threshold — CHIRPS/SPEI ship with an empty url and `required: false`, because raster zonal statistics have no business in the resolution path); and since Sept 2026 **`pythia_table`**, which reads a table this repo already ingests, with no new request and no new credential: `hdx_agricultural_stress` (HDX Signals, whose `jrc_agricultural_hotspots` indicator IS ASAP reached through a maintained connector; an alerting feed, so `absence_means_no_drought: true`) and `nmme_precip_anomaly` (NMME country-mean precipitation in sigma, `required: false` because it is a FORECAST for the month rather than an observation of it; absence is unknown, not dry). An entry declares `match: classes|threshold` rather than inheriting it from its transport, and `absence_means_no_drought` per entry — what absence MEANS is a property of the feed.
  - **`min_answered_for_zero` (2) before a zero on absence, and a month in progress is PENDING** — see the Group D entry in Known failure modes; PENDING/INCONCLUSIVE verdicts retract the unfrozen row they contradict (`retract_unsupported_row`) and stamp `no_row_reason` on the trigger row.
  - **`min_available` (>= 1), not a nominated required feed.** Until Sept 2026 ASAP was the only `required: true` entry, so when its download route vanished every drought cell in the run came back INCONCLUSIVE — 756 of them. Naming a different feed as required just relocates that failure. `min_available` asks for SOME independent evidence without saying which feed must supply it; the validator accepts either mechanism, and the shipped rulebook uses this one. An unavailable `required` entry still blocks, and nothing is required today.
  - **A source address is a candidate list.** `urls:` is tried in order and the first that parses wins, so a portal that moved its route costs a YAML edit rather than an outage. When every candidate fails the entry error names the LAST attempt's reason — "the shape changed" and "the route is dead" want different repairs.
  - **NOT offered, deliberately: FEWS NET / IPC Phase 3+.** It is the quantity the drought rule differences, so admitting it would reduce the gate to "IPC rose, therefore drought, because IPC rose" and file conflict-driven food insecurity as drought impact — the exact failure the gate exists to prevent. NMME is thresholded at **-1.0 sigma, never -0.43**: the tercile boundary is a climatological split covering a third of countries in any month, and under `combine: any` that would attribute a third of the world's food insecurity to drought.

- **Five outcomes**: `RESOLVED_VALUE` (qualifying delta); `RESOLVED_ZERO` — **two distinct zeros with distinct `rule_fired`**, absence (`drought_zero:no_indicator_signal+no_ipc_deterioration`, the `ZERO_RULE_BY_HAZARD['DR']` entry) vs a measured non-increase (`drought_zero:ipc_phase3plus_no_qualifying_increase`); `NO_DATA` + flagged (indicators dry but no analysis by the freeze deadline — the spec's explicit gap; **no baseline analysis**, because the covering analysis alone is a STOCK; or a deterioration the indicators do not attribute to drought); `PENDING` pre-freeze; and `INCONCLUSIVE` when a required indicator could not be read (fail-closed, same as a failed ReliefWeb sweep). `PENDING` and `INCONCLUSIVE` both write nothing — `as_reconciliation()` maps both to `WRITE_PENDING_STATUS`.
- **Month attribution** is `drought.month_attribution`, and it deliberately DIFFERS from `event_attribution.figure`: `analysis_window` (shipped) repeats the delta in every month the window covers — a slow-onset stock — and the row's provenance note says the values must never be summed; `start_month` puts it in the first month only and zeroes the rest.
- Same tables and same freeze logic as every other hazard: `haz_triggers` (indicator readings + evidence of absence, carried via the previously-unused `CountryTrigger.sweep_evidence`, which `detect.write_triggers` now persists), `haz_impact_candidates` with `source='ipc'` / `value_type='affected'`, and `resolutions.write_reconciliation` / `write_zero_resolution`. The population cap applies and flags; **the GDACS exposure ceiling deliberately does not** (GDACS drought exposure is an agricultural-footprint quantity and the value is a delta, not a stock — the two are not comparable).
- **Unverified**: the JRC ASAP hotspot archive routes in `drought.indicators` (`files/hotspots_ts.zip` per the download page, `files/hotspots_html_ts.zip` per HDX Signals' own reader, and the HDX CKAN mirror) could not be reached from the build environment. A wrong URL or changed shape fails LOUD and SAFE (snapshot refused naming the columns seen → indicator UNAVAILABLE), and the fix is YAML plus at most a key name in `drought_indicators._CLASS_KEYS` or a vendored `asap0_id`→ISO3 map if the CSV carries no country name. The consequence is bounded: `min_available` is satisfied by the `pythia_table` entries, so an ASAP outage no longer suppresses the whole hazard — but without it the backcast has no drought indicator before the HDX/NMME ingests began. Verify on the first live run and the first nightly backcast.

**Phase 5 mechanics (backcast, base rates, cross-check, acceptance — no new rules)**: `python -m resolver.hazard_resolution.{backcast,base_rates,acceptance}` (poetry scripts `haz-backcast` / `haz-base-rates` / `haz-acceptance`).
- **`backcast.py`** — since Sept 2026, `month_is_complete` refuses to mark a month done when it assessed cells and wrote NOTHING. The runner exits 0 whenever it walks every cell without raising, which it does even when a required source was unreadable and every cell came back INCONCLUSIVE; recording that as complete means the resume ledger never retries it, so a week-long source outage becomes permanently missing history. The August 2026 ASAP outage did exactly this to twelve months of drought. A month with no cells to assess is complete — there was nothing to do. It replays each hazard from `rulebook.backcast.<hazard>` (cyclone 2000, flood 2010, drought 2017) through `last_frozen_month`, calling the SAME `cli.run_{cyclone,flood,drought}_month` a live run uses, with `run_type='backcast'`. `--from`/`--to` may NARROW the window, never widen it. Every backcast month is frozen by definition, so the freeze guard means a backcast **fills gaps and never rewrites** — an existing answer survives and the attempt lands in `haz_revisions`. The `haz_backcast_progress` resume ledger is therefore load-bearing, not a convenience: re-walking a completed month writes one revision row per already-answered cell and buries the genuine post-freeze revisions (`--no-resume` overrides). Cyclone hoists the one-file IBTrACS ALL download out of the month loop via the new `run_cyclone_month(skip_detector_fetch=...)`, which skips the DETECTOR fetch only — `skip_fetch` would also skip the ladder's per-month windows. **How far a drought backcast reaches** changed in Sept 2026. The ASAP feed publishes a dateless "latest" snapshot, so it cannot speak for a past month — it is stamped with its RETRIEVAL month and correctly falls outside `max_observation_age_months`. Before the `pythia_table` providers that meant EVERY backcast month resolved INCONCLUSIVE; now `hdx_agricultural_stress` and `nmme_precip_anomaly` carry their own per-row dates, so the backcast reaches as far back as those tables were ingested and earlier months resolve INCONCLUSIVE rather than zero (still fail-closed). `check_backcastable` says which of the two cases a run is in before it starts, and the fix for the HTTP feeds remains pointing their url at a per-month archive (`{ym}`/`{year}`/`{month}` are substituted).
- **`base_rates.py`**: occurrence = share of backcast years that TRIGGERED per (iso3, hazard, calendar month) from `haz_triggers`, denominator = **years ASSESSED** (a month with no trigger row was never looked at), gated by `base_rates.occurrence.min_years` (3). Severity = q10..q90 of RESOLVED_VALUE amounts from `severity_base_rate_window_start` (2015), with `n_events` and `provenance_mix_json` (share by source **per year**, plus `lower_bound_share` — a distribution made of IDMC displacement figures is a distribution of floors, and the quantiles alone cannot say so). `quantile()` is written out rather than delegated so the interpolation definition is pinned by this repo's tests. Both tables are pure derivations: drop them and a re-run reproduces them.
- **`dfo.py`**: the Dartmouth Flood Observatory archive, cached in `haz_raw_dfo`, compared against `haz_base_rates_occurrence` for FL over the years strictly BEFORE `backcast.flood` — the validator rejects a `cross_check_end_year` past it, because comparing the machine against years it already resolved confirms nothing. Countries diverging by more than `dfo.divergence_factor` (3x) are listed in the report. **Calibration only**: never a trigger, never a resolution value, guarded by `test_dfo_never_writes_to_any_resolution_table`. A 0-of-N ISO3 resolution REFUSES the snapshot (same rule as the drought indicators). **Unverified endpoint** (`dfo.url`), same loud-and-safe failure mode as ASAP.
- **`acceptance.py`**: one page of markdown over the last 12 fully frozen months. **The headline names the hazards it covers** (`_headline_scope`, Sept 2026): it printed 0.0% as a whole-machine score when only drought had assessed cells in the window, and a rate over one hazard's cells read as a score for the machine. A window in which nothing was assessed says outright that the headline is not a measurement of anything. **The denominator is cells assessed** (`haz_triggers` rows), never rows written — dividing resolutions by resolutions would score 100% against a machine that answered one cell — and cells that produced no row (fail-closed / coverage-gated) count against the rate in their own column. A hazard with ZERO assessed cells reports as "not assessed", never 0%. The IFRC-GO-only baseline is **recomputed from the DB** (cells where the `ifrc_go` rung alone held a figure) rather than only quoting the documented ~4%. Sampling is seeded (`--seed`), never `ORDER BY random()`. The report measures and never tunes: the gap section states the shortfall per hazard and says so explicitly.

**Phase 6 mechanics (the base rates reach the forecaster — no new resolution rules, no new sources, no spend)**: `resolver/hazard_resolution/prompt_block.py` renders what the backcast learned into the SPD prompt of every PA question the machine resolves. `forecaster/prompts.py::build_spd_prompt_v2` calls `_load_haz_base_rate_block` (shaped after `_load_fewsnet_projection`, same `duckdb_io.get_db`/`close_db` handling) and injects the block **immediately after the Resolver history text**, not among the structured injects — it is the same kind of thing, a prior anchor, and STEP 1 tells the model to build its prior from what it finds there.
- **The block**: occurrence probability for the question's own six forecast months (from `haz_base_rates_occurrence`, in the forecast window's order, `n/a` where `min_years` withheld a rate — a withheld rate rendered as 0% would read as "this never happens"); conditional severity q10..q90 with `n_events` and window; the provenance mix ordered by share, with `lower_bound_share` called out in words; a hazard caveat where one is warranted; and the rulebook-generated process description.
- **Every parameter in the process description is read from `rulebook.yaml` at prompt-build time** — `cyclone.buffer_km`/`min_wind_kt`, `flood.gdacs_trigger_level`, `ladder`, `sanity.*`, `freeze_days`, and for drought `drought.ipc_phase_threshold`/`min_delta_*`/`month_attribution`. Change a threshold and the prompt moves on the next run with no code edit; tests pin the propagation for five of them. **Rule: never write a rulebook value into prompt text — generate it.**
- **Eligibility** (`prompt_block.is_eligible`) is `metric == 'PA'` and hazard in `MACHINE_HAZARDS` (derived from `HAZARD_CODE_BY_RULEBOOK_NAME` — note that map is a hardcoded dict in `rulebook.py`, NOT read from the YAML: adding a fourth hazard means editing `rulebook.py`'s map, which the validator's backcast-key check then forces into the YAML too). `PHASE3PLUS_IN_NEED` and `EVENT_OCCURRENCE` are refused on quantity grounds, not caution — see the Invariants entry.
- **Three states**: occurrence rows + severity row → the full block; occurrence rows + no severity row → "no historical events in record" (assessed, never resolved a value); **no rows at all → no block** (never assessed). Below `base_rates.prompt.min_events_for_quantiles` (3, deliberately stricter than the storage-side `severity.min_events` of 1) the event count replaces the quantiles.
- **Token discipline**: shipped blocks render at 800–1000 chars (~200–250 tokens) against `base_rates.prompt.max_chars` (1200). Over-budget is **logged and test-failed, never truncated**. A shared occurrence denominator is factored into the header (`16y assessed`) instead of repeated six times, and it goes back per-cell the moment the years differ. A small non-zero rate renders `<1%`, never `0%`.
- **Drought caveat** is generated from `backcast.drought`: record depth plus partial IPC coverage, and under `month_attribution: analysis_window` the "never sum them" warning the row provenance already carries.
- **New rulebook keys**: `base_rates.prompt.{min_events_for_quantiles, max_chars}`, both strictly validated. **CI**: `forecaster/tests/test_haz_base_rate_prompt.py` is named in forecaster-ci's base-rate step, and `resolver/hazard_resolution/prompt_block.py` + `rulebook.yaml` are in its `paths:` — a rulebook edit now changes forecaster prompt text, so it must re-run those snapshots.

**Tests**: `resolver/tests/test_hazard_resolution_*.py` (all network-free; run wholesale by resolver-ci-fast, whose path filters include `resolver/hazard_resolution/**` — no per-file CI wiring needed since none of them import a non-resolver package). Phase 1's acceptance suite pins Haiyan Nov-2013 triggering PHL, quiet-month zeros with complete evidence, and a ~250 km near-miss NOT triggering at the 200 km rulebook buffer (but triggering at 300 — the buffer is honoured from the rulebook, not code). Phase 2 adds `_ladder.py` (ladder order, lower-bound labelling, ceiling/population enforcement, OOM conflict flagging, freeze/provisional/pending, attribution, determinism), `_sources.py` (the four connectors via their injectable transport seams + the reuse guard), and `_flood.py` (GDACS detection + the three-past-months end-to-end acceptance run asserting every triggered cell has candidates or a flagged NO_DATA and every zero has evidence of absence). **Two Phase 1 CLI tests were updated, not suppressed**: they asserted a triggered cell gets no resolution row, which was true only while the ladder did not exist — those 2013 months are long past freeze, so the ladder now correctly resolves them `NO_DATA`/flagged. Phase 3 adds `_extraction.py`, driven by a **golden set** (`resolver/tests/fixtures/hazard_resolution/reliefweb_golden_set.json`: 10 documents + their recorded model responses + expected output, covering no-figure, households, cumulative, a verbatim duplicate across reports, a figure above the ceiling, a quote the document does not contain, and per-province figures that must not be summed) replayed through an injected model seam — so the suite asserts the deterministic half of extraction without a network or a model. Mutation-checked: disabling quote verification, ignoring `preference_rank`, dropping the ceiling, bypassing the cache or removing the budget cap each fail it. Phase 5 adds `_backcast.py` (window clamping, `run_type` stamping, the freeze guard still owning history, resume/retry semantics, the drought pre-flight warning — the month runner is injected), `_base_rates.py` (assessed-years denominators, min-years gate, quantile arithmetic, provenance mix incl. `lower_bound_share`), `_dfo.py` (tolerant parsing of both csv and xlsx, 0-of-N refusal, archive-span denominators, and the never-writes guarantee), and `_acceptance.py` (the cells-assessed denominator, no-row accounting, not-assessed vs 0%, seeded reproducible sampling, flag counts by reason). Phase 6 adds `_prompt_block.py` (rendering, eligibility, rulebook propagation, thin/absent records, budget) — still resolver-only imports, so still no CI wiring — plus `forecaster/tests/test_haz_base_rate_prompt.py`, which asserts the block reaches the ASSEMBLED prompt for one cyclone, one flood and one drought PA question. That second file is not redundant: a loader whose text nobody renders costs money and changes nothing, and asserting on the loader alone would not have caught either of the two injects the repo lost that way.

**Production wiring (2026-08-05, post-audit — SHADOW MODE).** The Phase 0–6 PRs (#843–#849) shipped the machine with no caller: no workflow ran any of its CLIs, the `haz_*` tables never existed in the canonical DB, the Phase 6 block therefore rendered `""` on every production prompt, and extraction spend wrote no `llm_calls` row. The audit-fix PR wired it in three places, all shadow-mode — **`compute_resolutions` still does not read `haz_resolutions`; the flip to scoring is a deliberate follow-up gated on the first acceptance reports**:
- **Monthly live run**: `resolver_update.yml` Phase 2.5 (after GDACS, non-fatal, own step timeouts) runs `resolve-hazards --hazard {flood,cyclone,drought} --months <M-2 M-1 M>` (the trailing unfrozen window — provisional answers are revised each cycle until they freeze), then `haz-base-rates` (so the 1st-of-month forecast reads fresh base rates from the same artifact), then `haz-acceptance --out diagnostics/haz_acceptance_report.md` (lands in `backfill-diagnostics`; it is the shadow-mode go/no-go evidence). The job env gained `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, `RELIEFWEB_APPNAME` and `IDMC_API_KEY` (the IDU `client_id` credential, which falls back to `IDMC_HELIX_CLIENT_ID` — the same credential the ingestion path already sends as `client_id` to the same host; **not** `IDMC_API_TOKEN`, a bearer token for a different host whose presence is also the `RESOLVER_SKIP_IDMC` feature flag; neither set → the IDU rung reports UNAVAILABLE, by design). Non-reset job timeout rose 150→240 min: the sweeps are paced patience, not slack. `haz_*` tables joined `SIGNATURE_OPTIONAL_TABLES`.
- **Nightly backcast**: `haz_backcast.yml` (cron `30 20 * * *` + dispatch; moved from 22:00 and its default budget trimmed 300→240 min on 2026-08-26 so even the slowest night frees the `pythia-resolver-db` concurrency group before the 28th 03:00 UTC resolver-update cron — the group holds ONE pending run, so a backcast still holding it at 03:00 puts the monthly ingest into a pending slot any third run can cancel) downloads the canonical DB (composite action **with self-inclusion via `extra-workflows` AND `include-failed-runs-from: Hazard Backcast`** — the terminal "any hazard failed" gate deliberately goes red AFTER the canonical upload succeeds, so without the failed-runs input a success-only discovery orphans every red night's artifact and the next night silently redoes + discards the work; observed 11 consecutive nights, 2026-08-15..25), runs `haz-backcast` per hazard under the new `--time-budget-min` (stops cleanly between months, defers the rest, exit 0; the `haz_backcast_progress` ledger makes nights convergent), refreshes base rates, and re-uploads canonical `pythia-resolver-db`. It is gated by `scripts/ci/check_pipeline_active.py` (same reason as the weekly ingest) and sits in the `pythia-resolver-db` concurrency group. **It is a canonical-DB producer, so it is named in all three discovery lists** (composite action + the run_horizon_scanner and publish_latest_data bespoke blocks). Extraction stays ON in backcast (owner decision), bounded by its `extraction.backcast_max_calls_per_month` share (2000 of 4000) of the monthly cap — the live trailing-window runs check only the total, so the difference is in effect their reserve. **When the share binds mid-month the policy is checkpoint and resume (Sept 2026)**: the cells the budget cut short get no row, the month is recorded `deferred` with its `deferred_cells`, and later nights walk exactly those cells once the allowance resets — the summary and the step summary say so. Once history is covered, the nightly run is a seconds-long no-op that rolls forward as months freeze.
- **Cost + diagnostics**: `extract.py` writes a rich `llm_calls` row per REAL call (`phase='hazard_extraction'`, iso3/hazard, is_test inherited; the injected test seam never logs) — mapped to the new `resolution` canonical phase in `resolver/query/costs.py` and the frontend `PHASE_ORDER`/`PHASE_LABELS`/`PHASE_COLORS` ("PA resolution"). `scripts/ci/inspect_resolver_db.py` gained section 2.5 (triggers/resolutions/base-rate coverage/extraction ledger/backcast progress; explicit "machine has not run" line when tables are absent); `summarize_all_phases.py` gained a Phase 2.5 table with an empty-base-rates warning; `resolve-hazards`/`haz-backcast` gained `--summary-out` (JSON run summaries into the diagnostics artifacts — logs expire, artifacts are the record).

## Per-horizon architecture

Forecasts cover a 6-month window. Each question has `window_start_date` and `target_month` (= month 6).

**Month-anchoring convention (critical):** horizon/month index 1 = the `window_start_date` month, everywhere — resolutions (`horizon_to_calendar_month`), prompts (`_forecast_month_keys_from_question` prefers `window_start_date`), and all forecast writers (`_month_index_for_label` maps each month label to its window offset; off-window labels are dropped, never positionally renumbered). The forecaster's internal anchor variable is `anchor_month` (first window month, from `_anchor_month_for_question`); `target_month` is the LAST window month and must never be used as a month-label anchor (see the +5-month shift entry in Known failure modes).

- **Resolutions**: `compute_resolutions` resolves each horizon independently with **deterministic source precedence**: candidate rows are ranked by metric preference (`affected` > `people_affected` > `pa` > `new_displacements` > `displaced`), then publisher tier (IFRC/ACLED tier 0 > IDMC tier 1 > others; mirrors `precedence_config.yml`), then `created_at` — never recency alone (last-write-wins produced value flips up to 145×). Each resolution logs its winning `source_desc`. Source-aware null handling: FATALITIES+ACE/ACO defaults to 0 (ACLED continuous coverage), EVENT_OCCURRENCE defaults to 0 (GDACS binary) — but **only for calendar months where the source has ANY row globally** (`_months_with_source_data`); months inside ingestion gaps or before source coverage stay unresolved instead of becoming false zeros. **FATALITIES additionally requires the country to be inside the source universe** (`_countries_with_source_data`): a country that has never appeared in ACLED's tables stays unresolved instead of zero-defaulting (`skipped_outside_source_universe` counter). Both gates read the `source_coverage` table, rebuilt at run start by `pythia/tools/source_coverage.py`. EVENT_OCCURRENCE is deliberately exempt from the universe gate — GDACS coverage is satellite-global and only writes rows where events occurred, so no rows genuinely means no qualifying events. PA and PHASE3PLUS_IN_NEED leave horizons unresolved when no data exists. Unresolvable hazards: DI, HW.
- **Scoring**: `compute_scores` scores each (question, horizon_m) pair separately. SPD questions (PA, FATALITIES, PHASE3PLUS_IN_NEED) get multiclass Brier (range **0–2**), log loss, and **normalized RPS stored under score_type='crps'**: `RPS = Σ_{k=1..K−1}(F_k − H_k)²/(K−1)`, range **0–1** (the standard discrete form of CRPS for ordinal buckets — Epstein 1969/Murphy 1971; the pre-July-2026 code divided by K, deflating scores ~20% vs the standard). Binary questions (EVENT_OCCURRENCE) get single Brier score (range **0–1**): `(forecast_p - outcome)^2`. Missing resolution horizons are naturally excluded via JOIN. **Every score row is keyed by an explicit `model_name`** — ensemble aggregations are scored via their `ensemble_mean_v2` / `ensemble_bayesmc_v2` / `track2_flash` rows in `forecasts_raw`; the NULL-model score convention is retired (it duplicated the named mean row and its value depended on write order). Degenerate inputs are skipped, not scored: zero-sum stored SPDs (never scored as uniform) and negative/non-finite resolved values (previously fell through to the TOP bucket). **Brier-family separation**: binary Brier (0–1, structurally near-zero for rare events correctly forecast as unlikely) and multiclass SPD Brier (0–2) are on different scales and must **never** be averaged together. Aggregations expose a `score_family` (`binary` / `spd`) so consumers can keep them apart — a naive mean across both families (e.g. averaging an `ensemble_mean` scores CSV that mixes 37 SPD + 24 binary questions) is meaningless and was the cause of the 0.200-vs-0.6237 dashboard/CSV discrepancy.
- **Bucket thresholds/labels are single-sourced** from `pythia/buckets.py` (`thresholds_for` / `interior_thresholds_for` / `labels_for` / `n_buckets_for` / `centroids_for` / `label_index_map`, derived from `BUCKET_SPECS`); `compute_scores.py`, `forecaster/scoring.py`, `forecaster/cli.py` (`SPD_CLASS_BINS_*`), `forecaster/ensemble.py`/`aggregate.py` (centroid defaults), and `forecaster/scenario_writer.py` expose their legacy constant names as derived values. Do not reintroduce literal threshold/label/centroid lists. **July 2026 bucket restructure**: every SPD metric gained a leading "0" bucket (centroid 0, next bucket starts at 1) and FATALITIES gained a >=1000 top bucket → K is now 6 (PA), 7 (FATALITIES), 6 (PHASE3PLUS_IN_NEED). The whole SPD chain takes an explicit per-metric `n_buckets` (no `=5` defaults anywhere); wrong-length model vectors are strictly rejected (no zero-pad/truncate — that would suppress tail buckets) and fall down the existing insufficient-month-coverage path; `compute_scores._load_spd` skips (with `[warn]`) any stored forecast whose bucket_index set ≠ {1..K} so pre-restructure 5-bucket DBs resurfacing via canonical-DB discovery are never mis-scored. The SPD CSV export is one wide schema `SPD_1..SPD_7` (`max_bucket_count()`); 6-bucket metrics have SPD_7=0.0. The `SPD_BUCKET_TEXT_*` constants in `forecaster/prompts.py` must remain LITERAL triple-quoted strings (the About page's `prompt_extractor.ts` regex-extracts them from source); `forecaster/tests/test_prompt_bucket_text.py` enforces their sync with `BUCKET_SPECS` in CI. The change landed with the fresh-DB July 2026 restart — no legacy 5-bucket compatibility layer exists by design.
- **Calibration**: `compute_calibration_pythia` aggregates scores across horizons to produce per-model weights. Groups by (hazard_code, metric), so EVENT_OCCURRENCE questions form their own calibration pool separate from PA/FATALITIES. Aggregate score rows (`ensemble_mean_v2`, `ensemble_bayesmc_v2`, `track2_flash`, NULL model_name) are excluded from the weight softmax — weights cover real ensemble members only. The forecaster consumes these weights on the next run via `_resolve_member_weights()` (BayesMC evidence weights + weighted mean), gated by `PYTHIA_USE_CALIBRATION_WEIGHTS` (default on).

## Phase 4: Dashboard + Calibration Integration

**API endpoints (Phase 4):**
- `/v1/risk_index?metric=EVENT_OCCURRENCE` — Returns binary P(event) as the risk value (0-1). No centroid multiplication. Per-capita returns raw probability unchanged (already a rate).
- `/v1/risk_index?metric=PHASE3PLUS_IN_NEED` — SPD-based risk index for IPC Phase 3+ population. Supports `normalize=true` for per-capita (fraction of population in Phase 3+).
- `/v1/diagnostics/resolution_rates` — Returns resolution rates by (hazard_code, metric): total/resolved/skipped/pending_too_new questions with rate. Supports `hazard_code` filter. `pending_too_new` is the count of questions whose `window_start_date` is after the resolution pipeline's calendar cutoff (previous complete month) — i.e. structurally unresolvable until the calendar advances. The Performance page's Resolution Coverage panel renders a muted "Awaiting first horizon — questions too new, will resolve next month" tile (instead of red 0%) when every question in the (hazard, metric) group is `pending_too_new`, so brand-new epoch questions don't look like calibration failures. Blocked hazards (DI, HW, CU, ACO) are excluded by default; pass `include_blocked=true` to see them. The dashboard derives the **scored** percentage from `resolved / (total - pending_too_new)` (eligible denominator) rather than `resolved / total`, so pending questions aren't counted against the rate.
- `/v1/diagnostics/kpi_scopes?metric_scope=EVENT_OCCURRENCE` — KPI scopes now accept EVENT_OCCURRENCE and PHASE3PLUS_IN_NEED alongside PA and FATALITIES.
- `/v1/performance/scores?metric=EVENT_OCCURRENCE` — Performance scores work for all metrics; binary questions produce Brier score only (no log/CRPS). Every `summary_rows`/`run_rows` row carries a derived `score_family` field (`binary` for EVENT_OCCURRENCE, `spd` otherwise) so the dashboard can render the two Brier scales separately and never blend them. Response includes `track_counts.total`: distinct scored questions across all tracks, including legacy questions that pre-date the Track 1/2 split (where `q.track IS NULL`). The Performance page Summary section uses this for the "Scored Questions" KPI; the `track1` / `track2` breakdown is rendered as a separate "Track Split" KPI. The Performance page Detailed Scores tables show **separate** "SPD Brier" (0–2) and "Binary Brier" (0–1) columns plus "SPD Q"/"Binary Q" counts; the "Ensemble Brier" KPI shows both family averages side by side. The scores CSV downloads (`/v1/downloads/scores_ensemble_mean.csv`, `scores_ensemble_bayesmc.csv`) include a `metric_family` column (after `metric`) so a blind average of the `brier` column across families is visibly suspect — consumers must group by `metric_family` first. (Binary EVENT_OCCURRENCE forecasts are scored under the *mean* ensemble, not bayesmc, so the bayesmc CSV / bayesmc dashboard view is SPD-only.)
- **`/v1/performance/sibyl_comparison`** — Fair covered-set head-to-head between the Sibyl deep-research track (`model_name='sibyl'`) and the main pipeline. Because Sibyl writes its pooled SPD into `forecasts_raw`/`forecasts_ensemble` under the **same run_id/question_id** as the standard track, `compute_scores` already scores it head-to-head on identical `(question_id, horizon_m, score_type)` keys — **this endpoint is read-only; no scoring change was needed**. It restricts the comparison to the exact set Sibyl actually forecast (Sibyl only covers the top-volatility subset, so an all-questions average would be unfair) and joins a **user-selectable standard baseline** (`?baseline=`; default best-available per question via `STANDARD_MODEL_PREFERENCE` bayesmc→mean→track2_flash, resolved with a ROW_NUMBER window). Params: `include_test`, `baseline`, `sibyl_run_id`. Returns `has_sibyl`, `baseline_used`, `available_baselines` (only aggregates actually present in `scores`), `pairs` (per question×horizon×score_type, with Sibyl's `js_divergence_vs_standard`/`volatility_score`/`cost_usd` from `sibyl_forecasts`), `aggregate` (keyed `score_family`→`score_type`: paired means, `mean_delta` = sibyl−standard so **negative = Sibyl better**, question-level `win_rate`/wins/ties), `by_hazard_metric` rollup, and `runs` (coverage/cost/`budget_capped` from `sibyl_runs`). **Never blends score families** (Sibyl's eligible pairs are all SPD). Tolerates pre-Sibyl / post-DB-reset DBs: `has_sibyl:false` with empty `pairs` but `runs` still populated so the UI shows an "awaiting resolutions" state. Tests: `pythia/tests/test_api_sibyl_comparison.py`.

**Frontend views (Phase 4):**
- `RiskView` type expanded: `PA_EIV`, `PA_PC`, `FATALITIES_EIV`, `FATALITIES_PC`, `EVENT_OCCURRENCE`, `PHASE3PLUS_EIV`, `PHASE3PLUS_PC` (7 total).
- RiskIndexMap uses fixed probability thresholds (0-5%, 5-15%, 15-30%, 30-50%, 50-100%) for EVENT_OCCURRENCE instead of Jenks breaks.
- RiskIndexTable displays probabilities as percentages for binary metrics.
- BinaryPanel.tsx: Per-month probability bars with resolution outcomes and Brier scores for question detail page.
- PerformancePanel: Two **page-level tabs** at the top — **Dashboard** (the live scoring sections below) and **About performance scores** (`web/src/app/performance/AboutScores.tsx` — a static methodology reference: per-score technical formula + plain-language description + interpretation guidance for SPD Brier / Binary Brier / Log Loss / CRPS-RPS, plus a quick-reference range/baseline table; keep in sync with `compute_scores.py`/`forecaster/scoring.py`). The Dashboard tab contains four sections — **Summary** (KPI cards including "Resolved Questions" `resolved / eligible`, "Scored Questions" total across all tracks, "Track Split" T1/T2 breakdown), **Resolution Coverage** (per-(hazard, metric) tiles showing `resolved of eligible · N too new`, color-coded green >90%, yellow 50-90%, red <50% of the eligible denominator), the **Sibyl — Deep-Research Track** section (see below), and **Detailed Scores** (filters Total/By Hazard/By Run/By Model + metric + track + ensemble selector grouped in the same card as the scores table they control). Metric filter includes EVENT_OCCURRENCE and PHASE3PLUS_IN_NEED. Blocked hazards (DI, HW, CU, ACO) are filtered out of the coverage panel both at the API and as a defensive frontend filter. In Detailed Scores, `sibyl` now renders with a friendly "Sibyl (deep research)" label + a "DEEP RESEARCH" chip (via the shared `web/src/lib/model_names.ts` prettifier, which also maps `ensemble_bayesmc_v2`/`ensemble_mean_v2`/`track2_flash` to friendly names); `sibyl` stays out of the ensemble selector and the "Ensemble Brier" KPI (`isEnsembleModel` unchanged).
- **Sibyl — Deep-Research Track section** (`web/src/app/performance/SibylComparison.tsx`, collapsible, between Resolution Coverage and Detailed Scores; prefetched by `performance/page.tsx` as `initialSibyl`, additive — never blocks the page): renders the `/v1/performance/sibyl_comparison` head-to-head. Baseline selector (best-available or a pinned aggregate; refetches). KPI row: Win Rate, Sibyl vs Baseline mean Brier, Mean Δ Brier (green<0/red>0), Coverage `n_forecast/n_selected`, Cost/forecast (opus+brave per covered question), Budget (OK / red "Capped"). Five inline-SVG charts (no deps, validated light-surface palette matching CostsClient; **green = Sibyl better, red = worse**): (1) flagship skill scatter — Sibyl vs Baseline Brier with a 45° diagonal, dots below = Sibyl won, size ∝ volatility; (2) per-question dumbbell sorted by margin; (3) score-type summary bars (Brier/Log/CRPS with Δ + win%); (4) divergence-vs-skill scatter (does diverging from the ensemble help?); (5) cost-effectiveness (cost/question vs Δ Brier). Renders an "awaiting resolutions" empty state (driven off `runs`) when `has_sibyl` is false or no covered pairs exist yet, and hides entirely when there are no Sibyl runs at all. New TS types in `web/src/lib/types.ts` (`SibylComparison*`).
- QuestionsTable: Binary questions display as "Binary (event)", PHASE3PLUS_IN_NEED as "Phase 3+".
- **Resolver page**: Redesigned as a source-level accordion data explorer grouped by category (Resolution Data, Conflict Forecasts, Weather and Climate, Situation Reports, Other Alerts, Other). Each data source gets a collapsible row with summary metadata (name, last updated, global/country row counts) and a lazy-loaded data table on expand. Shared country selector at top filters all country-filterable sources. Old tab-based layout (`tabs/` directory) removed. Component: `web/src/app/resolver/ResolverClient.tsx`.

## Regime Change (RC) scoring

RC detects departures from historical base rates (distinct from triage_score which measures overall risk).

- `score = likelihood x magnitude`, clamped [0, 1]
- **Levels** (env-overridable, likelihood-only thresholds): L0 (likelihood < 0.15), L1 (likelihood >= 0.15), L2 (likelihood >= 0.35), L3 (likelihood >= 0.55). Env vars: `PYTHIA_HS_RC_LEVEL1_LIKELIHOOD`, `PYTHIA_HS_RC_LEVEL2_LIKELIHOOD`, `PYTHIA_HS_RC_LEVEL3_LIKELIHOOD`.
- **Track assignment**: RC level > 0 → Track 1 (full ensemble), RC level 0 + priority tier → Track 2 (single model, gemini-3.5-flash), otherwise no SPD.
- **Triage skip for RC L1+**: Hazards with RC level ≥ 1 are automatically promoted to Track 1, so triage LLM calls are skipped (saving 3 LLM calls per hazard: 1 grounding + 2 triage passes). These hazards receive synthetic `_RC_PROMOTED_DEFAULTS` (triage_score=0, tier="rc_promoted", status="rc_promoted" — the tier was "quiet" until 2026-09-03, which made every Track-1 question read as a quiet hazard that got a full ensemble; no consumer routes on this tier). Downstream `_write_hs_triage()` independently assigns track=1 from RC level, so triage output has no effect. Three triage skip patterns exist: ACE low-activity, seasonal skip, and RC-promoted.
- L1+ triggers adversarial evidence checks, which are BOTH generated AND injected into SPD prompts at L1+ (July 2026 alignment — previously injection required L2+, so L1 checks were paid for but never used). L2+ additionally forces `need_full_spd = TRUE`.
- **CRITICAL sync constraint**: The RC level threshold in `_select_rc_promoted_hazards` (horizon_scanner.py), the re-check inside `adversarial_check.py` (`level < 1` → skip), and the forecaster injection gates (`rc_level >= 1` in `forecaster/cli.py` `_load_structured_data` + `forecaster/prompts.py`) must all match. If they drift, checks are generated but silently never injected (or vice versa).
- **The tail-pack subsystem was removed July 2026** (vestigial once adversarial checks inject at L1+): no more `HS_TAIL_PACKS_ENABLED`/`PYTHIA_HS_HAZARD_TAIL_PACKS_ENABLED`, `_build_hazard_tail_query`, tail-pack generation block, or prompt "HAZARD TAIL PACK" guidance. `_select_rc_promoted_hazards` (renamed from `_select_tail_pack_hazards`) survives as the adversarial-check candidate selector, capped by `PYTHIA_HS_ADVERSARIAL_MAX_PER_COUNTRY` (default 2). The `hs_hazard_tail_packs` TABLE remains — it stores the RC/triage grounding packs the forecaster injects as "HS GROUNDING EVIDENCE".
- Distribution check warns when too many assessments exceed expected proportions
- RC prompt templates include a softened distribution anchor paragraph for small-country runs (where few comparative countries are included)
- **RC model**: Both passes default to `gemini-3.5-flash` (overridable via `PYTHIA_RC_MODEL_PASS1` / `PYTHIA_RC_MODEL_PASS2`)
- **RC data sources for ACE**: Conflict forecasts (VIEWS, conflictforecast.org, ACLED CAST) and ICG CrisisWatch "On the Horizon" flags are injected into ACE RC prompts. CrisisWatch is fetched once per run in `main()` and explicitly threaded through `_run_hs_for_country` → `run_rc_for_country` → `_run_rc_for_single_hazard` (falls back to in-memory cache if not passed).
- **RC grounding queries**: Use hazard-specific search labels (e.g. "armed conflict escalation signals" for ACE, "flood risk river levels" for FL) instead of generic terms, to improve Gemini Google Search result relevance
- See `docs/hs_regime_change.md` for full details

## Track 1 / Track 2 forecast system

The forecaster routes questions into two tracks based on RC level:

- **Track 1** (full ensemble): Multi-model ensemble producing both `ensemble_bayesmc_v2` and `ensemble_mean_v2` aggregation rows. Used for RC level > 0 (higher-RC or higher-complexity questions). Higher cost.
- **Track 2** (single model): Single `track2_flash` model. Used for RC level 0 questions with priority tier. No ensemble aggregation — produces a single forecast row per question. Lower cost. Reasoning trace is simplified (prior + rc_assessment only; updates array may be empty).

Track 1 questions always receive scenarios, whatever their triage tier (RC-promoted hazards carry `tier="rc_promoted"` and `triage_score=0` from `_RC_PROMOTED_DEFAULTS`; the scenario writer only tests `track == 1`).

Track 2 questions have no `ensemble_bayesmc_v2` or `ensemble_mean_v2` rows in `forecasts_ensemble`. The legacy CI step "Verify Forecaster wrote both aggregation methods" (`verify_forecaster_aggregations.py`) was removed because it hard-exited when all questions routed to Track 2. This is a resolved issue — the step should not be re-added.

## Sibyl parallel deep-research track

Sibyl (`sibyl/` package, July 2026) is a parallel forecasting harness that independently re-forecasts the **10 highest-volatility affected/fatalities questions** per run via agentic open-web research (Claude Opus + Brave Search), scored head-to-head against the standard track. Full integration map and design decisions: `sibyl/DISCOVERY.md`. Entry point: `python -m sibyl.run [--hs-run-id X] [--n N]`; workflow: `run_sibyl.yml` (chains off HS Triage, re-uploads `pythia-resolver-db`, dispatches publish). **The Sibyl-end publish dispatch is the forecast pipeline's SOLE release trigger** — HS Triage no longer triggers publish directly (removed 2026-07-16), so the release order is `pythia → Sibyl → interpreter → publish → inline DB inspect` (a single release per cycle; the interpreter combined report + PDF run inside the Sibyl leg after the current-run bundle, before its canonical upload — so the published DB already carries the interpretations row). The dispatch fires on `success()` even when Sibyl finds 0 eligible questions (the canonical DB is uploaded regardless), so a no-eligible cycle still produces exactly one release. Consequence: the forecast release now depends on Sibyl reaching its end — if a Sibyl run breaks on an infra error, recover by manually dispatching `publish_latest_data.yml`.

**Architecture** (per question, in DESCENDING volatility order so the budget cap sacrifices the least-volatile work first): load Resolver base rate (the ONLY shared input with the standard track, by design) → K=3 independent agentic trials (sequential belief-updating loop, ≤10 steps, tools = `brave_search` + `fetch_url` only — never the structured connectors) → linear-pool the trial CDFs (PCHIP in log1p space, numpy — scipy is not a dependency) → identity calibration hook (`CALIBRATION_ENABLED=False`; deferred PIT-based recalibration documented in `sibyl/calibration.py`) → discretize onto `BUCKET_SPECS` buckets → write.

**Key decisions (do not silently change):**
- **Volatility proxy** = `hs_triage.regime_change_score` (likelihood × magnitude), tiebreak `triage_score`. There is no first-class volatility score in Pythia, and **no minimum-volatility gate** — the workflow gate is purely `eligible count >= 1` (top-N ordering only prioritizes under the budget cap).
- **Test-mode derivation** (July 2026): Sibyl chains off HS via `workflow_run`, which cannot carry the upstream `test_mode` input — so `sibyl/run.py` reads `hs_runs.is_test` for the resolved run and sets `PYTHIA_TEST_MODE=1` itself (stamping all Sibyl outputs `is_test`); `select_top_questions` includes a test run's `is_test` questions via `hs_run_is_test()`. Before this, Sibyl silently found 0 eligible questions on every test-mode pipeline run.
- **Eligible pairs** = `{(ACE,FATALITIES),(FL,PA),(TC,PA),(DR,PHASE3PLUS_IN_NEED)}` (config `ELIGIBLE_HAZARD_METRICS`). EVENT_OCCURRENCE is never eligible and never used as padding — a shortfall below N is logged loudly and the run proceeds.
- **Native output**: same bucket-row format under `model_name='sibyl'` in `forecasts_raw` + `forecasts_ensemble` (`weights_profile='sibyl'`), written under the question's **standard forecaster run_id** so `compute_scores` (DISTINCT model_name) and the question-page SPD panel pick it up with zero changes. A trial emits ONE quantile set for the monthly value; the same bucket vector is written for all 6 `month_index` values. Full provenance (trial traces, quantiles, costs, divergences, leakage) goes to `sibyl_forecasts`/`sibyl_runs`.
- **`'sibyl'` is in `AGGREGATE_MODEL_NAMES`** (`compute_calibration_pythia.py`): scored, but excluded from the ensemble-member weight softmax.
- **Cost ledger**: every Opus call and Brave query is logged to `llm_calls` with `phase='sibyl'` (itemised on /v1/costs; `sibyl` is a first-class bucket in `phase_group`/`CANONICAL_PHASES` in `resolver/query/costs.py` — the Opus vs `brave-web-search` split shows in the by-model tables). NOTE: before the July 2026 costs-page fix, `phase_group` had no `sibyl` branch, so Sibyl cost fell into `other` and the `sibyl_ms` runtime column was always 0 — do not remove the `sibyl` branch. The authoritative budget total is the in-process `CostTracker`; the hard cap (`SIBYL_RUN_HARD_CAP_USD`, default $40) is checked at question AND trial boundaries — the in-flight unit finishes (overshoot ≈ one question), remaining questions are persisted as `skipped`/`run budget cap`, and `sibyl_runs.budget_capped` is set. Never abort mid-trial.
- **Leakage controls** (`sibyl/leakage.py`): every search passes a Brave `freshness` date-range ending at asOf (via the `freshness_override` kwarg added to `fetch_via_brave_search` — one search path, no second Brave client); resolution-source domains (ACLED, IFRC GO, IDMC, GDACS, FEWS NET, IPC) are blocked always; the post-asOf snippet classifier only activates in backtest (`SIBYL_BACKTEST_MODE=1`, asOf = question `window_start_date`); residual leak rate persisted per question.
- **`claude-opus-5` rejects sampling params** (HTTP 400): `providers.call_anthropic` drops `temperature` for `_ANTHROPIC_NO_TEMPERATURE_PREFIXES` (opus-4-7/4-8, **opus-5**, sonnet-5, fable) — these are LITERAL id prefixes, so every Anthropic bump needs its own entry. Trial diversity comes from per-trial perspective seeds in the prompt (`TRIAL_PERSPECTIVES`), not temperature. The model has a `pythia/model_costs.json` entry (`[5.00, 25.00]` per 1M tokens) — a missing entry means silent $0 cost logging AND a broken budget cap.
- **Dashboard**: `/v1/sibyl/{runs,summary,questions,question_detail}` (`pythia/api/routes/sibyl.py`, tolerant of pre-Sibyl DBs), `sibyl` block in `/v1/diagnostics/run_summary`, optional `model=sibyl` param on `/v1/risk_index`, `/sibyl` Next.js page (sortable JS-divergence table + trial traces; **Questions / About these metrics** page-level tabs, where the About tab `web/src/app/sibyl/SibylMetricsAbout.tsx` explains JSD-vs-standard, inter-trial JSD, and Volatility with technical + plain-language + interpretation copy — the three table headers also carry `InfoTooltip` summaries), RunSummaryView Sibyl section, PA-KPI Sibyl toggle. JS divergence reuses `_js_divergence` from `generate_calibration_advice.py`.

**Env vars**: `SIBYL_MODEL` (default `claude-opus-5`), `SIBYL_K` (3), `SIBYL_MAX_STEPS` (10), `SIBYL_AGGREGATION` (`linear_pool`|`vincent`), `SIBYL_RUN_HARD_CAP_USD` (40), `SIBYL_BUDGET_USD_PER_QUESTION` (unset), `SIBYL_N_QUESTIONS` (10), `SIBYL_BACKTEST_MODE` (0), `SIBYL_CALIBRATION_ENABLED` (0), `SIBYL_LIVE_LOOKUPS_ENABLED` (0), plus Brave/fetch tuning knobs in `sibyl/config.py`.

**Tests**: `tests/test_sibyl_*.py` + `tests/sibyl_test_utils.py`, run by `sibyl-ci.yml` (the only workflow that runs the Sibyl suites; other workflows do reach into the top-level `tests/` directory — `forecaster-ci.yml` runs `tests/test_poll_llm_batches_guard.py` + `tests/test_check_pipeline_active.py`, `crisiswatch-ci.yml` runs `tests/test_refresh_crisiswatch.py`). The smoke test is fully deterministic (model call + Brave mocked).

## Batch-API staged pipeline + prompt caching (July 2026)

Provider Batch APIs (OpenAI `/v1/batches`, Anthropic `/v1/messages/batches`, Gemini `batchGenerateContent`) process independent requests asynchronously at **50% off input AND output tokens** (≤24h completion). The pipeline's LLM families are mutually independent within a wave, so an alternative **staged** execution mode submits them as provider batches and resumes when they finish. **All flags default OFF — `--phase full` / `--stage full` / the legacy `run_horizon_scanner.yml` remain byte-for-byte today's synchronous behavior.**

**Batch layer** (`pythia/llm_batch.py`): provider adapters (submit/poll/fetch/cancel, raw `requests`), custom-id encoding (`^[A-Za-z0-9_-]{1,64}$`; forecaster families key by (question_id, model_key), HS families by (iso3, hazard_code, pass_idx), and **every id carries a `sha1(pipeline_id)[:8]` scope segment** — the DB travels between runs and custom_id is the sole replay key, so an unscoped id would let month 2 replay month 1's outputs; the 2026-07-28 audit's critical finding). `llm_batch_requests` carries a `pipeline_id` column; `submit_pending` only submits THIS pipeline's pending rows and sweeps stale foreign rows via `expire_and_purge_stale` (expire >max-wait in-flight, delete >45d). `get_result` accepts a legacy unscoped row ONLY via a JOIN proving its batch belongs to this pipeline (deploy-time continuity), returns errored items WITH usage (callers cost the burned tokens then take the sync fallback), and extracts the SUBMITTED prompt from the stored request body (`sent_prompt` — collect-stage rebuilds embed a different 'Today' date). `collect_batch` is idempotent (stamps `usage.service_tier="batch"` so the shared cost helper halves the price), **salvages completed items from canceled/failed/expired batches before expiring the remainder** (providers preserve pre-cancel results), flips result-missing items to `expired`, and never zeroes counters on a re-collect. State lives in two DuckDB tables (`llm_batches`, `llm_batch_requests`) — **the DB travels in the artifact, so the DB IS the cross-stage state**; both HS and forecaster mains close the batch connection at stage end (WAL checkpoint — the staged artifact excludes `.wal`). `clear_request_bodies` nulls stored bodies after collect (artifact size). Request bodies come from `forecaster/providers.py::build_body_for_spec` → `resolve_request_params` (the SAME param-resolution the sync `call_chat_ms` uses — never rebuild bodies elsewhere; batch payloads must stay byte-identical to sync payloads; Anthropic `cache_control` in batch bodies is behind `PYTHIA_BATCH_PROMPT_CACHE` (code default 0, **set to 1 in `pythia_pipeline_stage.yml` since 2026-08-03** — see the prompt-caching section below), and OpenAI `prompt_cache_key` is never emitted in batch bodies — no batch discount exists for it. **The one permitted sync/batch divergence is the `cache_control` TTL** (`_batch_cache_ttl()` in `forecaster/providers.py` — a function, not a module constant, deliberately so it reads the env per call and stays monkeypatchable; default `1h`): the prompt bytes stay identical, so a replayed batch result still answers the prompt a sync fallback would have sent).

**Forecaster phases** (`forecaster/cli.py --phase {full,submit,collect} --pipeline-id pl_X`): `submit` runs the normal per-question flow but `_try_batch_phase` intercepts each member call (spd_v2 / binary_v2 / track2_spd families), enqueues the body and returns a sentinel; then `submit_pending` creates provider batches and main() prints `BATCH_PIPELINE_ID=` / `FC_RUN_ID=` and exits. `collect` reuses the submit run_id (from `llm_batches.run_id`), ingests finished batches (canceling stragglers past `PYTHIA_BATCH_MAX_WAIT_H`), replays each member call from `llm_batch_requests`, and **falls back to the sync `call_chat_ms` path on any miss** (marking `fallback_sync`) — parsing, aggregation, calibration weights and DB writers are the unmodified downstream code. A provider excluded via `PYTHIA_BATCH_PROVIDERS` is deferred at submit (never called) and runs sync at collect.

**HS stages** (`horizon_scanner/horizon_scanner.py --stage {full,hs_submit,hs_rc_collect,hs_finalize} --pipeline-id pl_X`, seam module `horizon_scanner/hs_batch.py`): RC→triage dependency forces two waves. `hs_submit` mints the hs_run_id + hs_runs row, runs RC grounding (sync Brave, persisted to hs_hazard_tail_packs), builds RC prompts and enqueues/submits the `hs_rc` family (triage skipped). `hs_rc_collect` replays RC (sync fallback per miss), **reloads RC grounding packs from the DB** (never re-fetches/re-persists), saves each country's merged RC dict to the `hs_stage_state` table, then runs triage grounding + enqueues/submits `hs_triage`. `hs_finalize` loads RC from `hs_stage_state` (batch replay as fallback), replays triage, and runs the unmodified `_write_hs_triage` + adversarial checks + coverage/stdout contract. Invariants: replayed results rich-log to `llm_calls` exactly once (existence guard keyed on the UPPERCASED label `RC_{hz}_PASS_{n}`); the seasonal-filter `run_date` derives from the run id (`run_date_from_run_id`) so a month boundary between stages can't flip a hazard's active set; `_rc_model_spec`/`_triage_model_spec` are the single pass-model resolvers shared by sync + enqueue. The collect-stage run_id resolves from `llm_batches.hs_run_id` by pipeline_id (forecaster collect uses `run_id IS NOT NULL`, HS uses `hs_run_id IS NOT NULL` — no cross-talk; both families share one pipeline_id per cycle).

**Workflow orchestration**: `pythia_pipeline_stage.yml` ("Pythia Pipeline Stage", **owns the monthly production cron since 2026-07-27** — `0 0 1 * *` enters at hs_submit with empty inputs — plus workflow_dispatch with inputs stage/pipeline_id/db_run_id/test_mode and four **smoke-run inputs** (2026-07-28, all default to production behavior on cron/empty: `batch_providers` → `PYTHIA_BATCH_PROVIDERS`, `only_countries` → `PYTHIA_HS_ONLY_COUNTRIES`, `grounding_primary` → `PYTHIA_GROUNDING_PRIMARY_BACKEND`, and boolean `disable_brave` which withholds `BRAVE_SEARCH_API_KEY` so Brave falls through on the missing-key path WITHOUT breaker trips — the expression must keep its negated `!inputs.disable_brave && secrets.X || ''` form because `x && '' || y` always yields y in GitHub expressions), same `pythia-resolver-db` concurrency group, timeout 330, run-name embeds `{pipeline_id} — {stage}` which the poller's dispatch-once guard matches on; the hs-submit job condition includes `github.event_name == 'schedule'`). Stage chain: `hs_submit → hs_rc_collect → hs_finalize_fc_submit → fc_collect_finalize`; alternate entry `fc_submit` (sync HS in-job, forecaster batching only). Stages S1–S3 upload the DB as **`pythia-resolver-db-staged`** (deliberately NOT the canonical name — canonical discovery must never pick up a half-finished pipeline DB) + a tiny `pythia-batch-state` JSON (`scripts/ci/emit_batch_state.py`); **both uploads are signature-gated, DB before state** (a state artifact without its DB would green-light a successor whose forced download fatally fails), and **every stage fails RED on a signature regression** — including the final stage when the canonical upload + Sibyl dispatch were withheld (before 2026-07-28 these went green with withheld uploads and the pipeline stalled silently). The final stage uploads canonical `pythia-resolver-db`, runs the forecaster collect with `--hs-run-id` pinned to the pipeline's epoch (an interleaved HS run would otherwise flip the question set and every replay would miss), and dispatches Sibyl (`gh workflow run run_sibyl.yml -f db_run_id=<its own run>`), preserving the pythia → Sibyl → publish release chain. Collect stages download the staged DB via `force-run-id` (fatal if missing — a stage never guesses its DB). The workflow sets `PYTHIA_BATCH_API_ENABLED=1` in its env (the staged path is the opt-in). `poll_llm_batches.yml` (cron `*/15`, timeout 15, NOT in the DB concurrency group) runs `scripts/ci/poll_llm_batches.py`: reads the newest batch-state per pipeline from Actions artifacts, polls provider batches (needs the 3 provider secrets), and dispatches the next stage when all are terminal or older than `PYTHIA_BATCH_MAX_WAIT_H` — convergent by design (missed ticks delay, never lose, a pipeline; collects are idempotent so double-fires are harmless). **Failed/cancelled/timed-out stage runs count toward a dispatch-attempt cap** (default 3, `PYTHIA_STAGE_MAX_DISPATCH_ATTEMPTS`, cooldown `PYTHIA_STAGE_RETRY_COOLDOWN_MIN`=60): a stalled pipeline emits a `::error` with the manual re-dispatch command instead of re-firing every 15 minutes forever. A cheap **activity gate** exits idle ticks in seconds unless a `pythia-batch-state` artifact was created in the last 72h — otherwise the cron would burn far more Actions minutes than the pipeline itself; manual dispatch bypasses the gate (recovery valve if a pipeline stalls past the window).
