Since 2026-08-03 the gate is `python3 -m scripts.ci.check_pipeline_active --emit active`, with `actions/checkout@v4` running *before* it so the gate can be the shared script; it is stdlib + `gh` only, so idle ticks still skip setup-python and the pip install. **The weekly `ingest-structured-data.yml` is gated while a pipeline is in flight** (`scripts/ci/check_pipeline_active.py` gate job: newest batch-state artifact <`PYTHIA_PIPELINE_ACTIVE_WINDOW_H`=72h old with no newer successful fc_collect_finalize → skip with a `::notice`; `force_during_pipeline` dispatch input overrides; fails open) — the concurrency group only serializes single runs, and an ingest landing mid-pipeline would be silently discarded by the final canonical upload. **The monthly cron moved here from `run_horizon_scanner.yml` on 2026-07-27** (owner decision; the legacy workflow is manual-dispatch-only as the sync fallback — never re-add its cron while this one is live, or the pipeline double-runs); "Pythia Pipeline Stage" is in all three canonical-DB candidate lists.

**Prompt V3 order + caching** (Phase 1, flags `PYTHIA_PROMPT_V3_ORDER` / `PYTHIA_PROMPT_CACHE_ENABLED`, both default OFF): the SPD (`build_spd_prompt_v2`, `return_parts=True` → (prefix, suffix)), binary, RC and triage prompt builders have a static-first assembly (role/task/buckets/hazard guidance/method+schema first, per-question data last) that creates multi-KB shared prefixes for OpenAI automatic caching (+ `prompt_cache_key`), Gemini implicit caching, and Anthropic `cache_control` (via `call_chat_ms(cache_segments=...)`; Sibyl's step prompts use a 4-block template with breakpoints). Legacy assembly is byte-identical to the pre-refactor prompts (verified against HEAD; guarded by `forecaster/tests/test_prompt_v3_order.py`). **The forecaster call sites were only actually WIRED to the cache plumbing on 2026-07-28** (audit finding C1: before that, `cache_segments` had one caller — Sibyl — and `prompt_cache_key` none, so the flags were on in production while Opus 5 paid full input price on every SPD call): the Track-1/Track-2 SPD sites build via `return_parts=True` and thread `cache_prefix` + `prompt_cache_key` (`pythia:{family}:{hazard}:{metric}:t{track}`) down `_call_spd_members_v2(_compat)`/`_call_spd_bayesmc_v2` → `_call_spd_model_for_spec`, where segments are derived against the prompt ACTUALLY sent (tail evidence appends can't break the segments-join==prompt invariant); the binary path passes the OpenAI key only; HS RC/triage (Gemini) needs no body change. Verification: `scripts/post_run_diagnostics.py`'s "Prompt cache" section warns when the flag is on and cache reads stay zero. Guarded by `forecaster/tests/test_prompt_cache_wiring.py`. **Both flags are ON in the production workflows since 2026-07-27**: `pythia_pipeline_stage.yml`, `run_horizon_scanner.yml` (kept in lockstep so the sync fallback produces the same prompts), and `run_sibyl.yml` set `PYTHIA_PROMPT_V3_ORDER=1` + `PYTHIA_PROMPT_CACHE_ENABLED=1`; code defaults remain OFF for local/dev runs. `scripts/compare_prompt_order_runs.py` remains the regression harness — run it against the first production cycles (compare SPD JS-divergence, parse failures, RC/triage distributions vs the last pre-V3 run) and flip the env flags back to "0" in the three workflows to revert. Cost telemetry (Phase 0): `usage_to_dict` passes through `cache_read_input_tokens`/`cache_creation_input_tokens`/`cached_tokens`/`service_tier`/`batch_id`; `pythia/model_costs.json` supports object-form entries with cached rates; `compute_cost_split_usd` is the single cost formula (used by both `estimate_cost_usd` and `llm_logging`) applying cached rates and the 0.5× batch multiplier.

**The batched path had a 0% cache hit rate by construction until 2026-08-03.** Measured on the 2026-08-01 production DB: all 99 Anthropic, 198 OpenAI and 1,015 Gemini calls logged **zero** `cache_read_input_tokens` and zero `cache_creation_input_tokens` — because every one of them went through a provider Batch API, and `_batch_prompt_cache_enabled()` defaulted to off. Sibyl, the only synchronous Anthropic path, was the sole exception and works well (2026-07-31: 108,309 read tokens on 345,277 prompt tokens = 31%, against 5,519 write tokens — a 19.6:1 read:write ratio, ~11% off its Opus bill). Three changes: (1) `PYTHIA_BATCH_PROMPT_CACHE=1` in `pythia_pipeline_stage.yml` — the code default stays 0, since batch cache hits are best-effort (items fan out across nodes; an entry is only readable once the first response has begun) while the write premium is certain, but with the flag off the hypothesis could never be tested and the downside is ~$0.06/cycle; (2) batch bodies now request a **1h TTL** (`_batch_cache_ttl()`) — the 2026-08-01 SPD batch ran **41 minutes**, so a 5-minute entry written by the first item is long gone before the last item is scheduled; (3) the cacheable-minimum gate is now **per model** (`_anthropic_cache_min_chars`, keyed by literal id prefix like the sampling-param guards) instead of one 4096-char constant — the minimum is not monotonic across generations (512 tokens on claude-opus-5, still 4096 on Opus 4.6/4.5 and Haiku 4.5), and the old constant silently left every `binary_v2` prefix (~760 tokens) unmarked on the one model that would have cached it. **Expected ceiling is modest and should be checked against, not assumed:** the measured longest common prefix within each (model, phase, hazard, metric) group is 23% of Anthropic prompt tokens, and input is only 36% of Anthropic cost (output is 64%), so best case is ~$0.43/cycle of a $6.07 Anthropic bill. **Verify after the next cycle** via `batch_economics.py` / `post_run_diagnostics.py` (both already read `cache_read_input_tokens`); if reads stay at zero, set the workflow flag back to "0" — that is the whole revert. Guarded by `forecaster/tests/test_prompt_cache_wiring.py` (`test_batch_bodies_buy_an_hour_ttl_but_sync_bodies_do_not`, `test_cache_minimum_is_per_model_not_one_constant`). **Rule: a cache flag that is on in the workflow proves nothing — only a non-zero `cache_read_input_tokens` does.**

**Env flags**: `PYTHIA_BATCH_API_ENABLED` (0), `PYTHIA_BATCH_PROVIDERS` (openai,anthropic,google), `PYTHIA_BATCH_MAX_WAIT_H` (24), `PYTHIA_BATCH_MAX_REQUESTS`/`PYTHIA_BATCH_MAX_BYTES`/`PYTHIA_BATCH_HTTP_TIMEOUT_SEC` (chunking/HTTP), `PYTHIA_PROMPT_V3_ORDER` (0), `PYTHIA_PROMPT_CACHE_ENABLED` (0), `PYTHIA_BATCH_PROMPT_CACHE` (code default 0; **"1" in `pythia_pipeline_stage.yml` since 2026-08-03**), `PYTHIA_BATCH_CACHE_TTL` (`1h`; `none` reverts to Anthropic's 5-minute default), `PYTHIA_ANTHROPIC_CACHE_MIN_CHARS` (unset — overrides the per-model cacheable-minimum table), `PYTHIA_STAGE_MAX_DISPATCH_ATTEMPTS` (3) / `PYTHIA_STAGE_RETRY_COOLDOWN_MIN` (60) (poller stall cap), `PYTHIA_PIPELINE_ACTIVE_WINDOW_H` (72, ingest gate).

**Poller self-rescheduling (2026-07-29, IMPORTANT)**: `poll_llm_batches.yml`'s `*/15` cron **is not honoured by GitHub** — observed firing roughly hourly (07:21, 04:29, 01:08, 23:40), which parked staged pipelines between stages for hours and made the whole staged design look broken. Scheduled workflows are best-effort and frequent crons are throttled. The cron became **ignition only**: `poll_llm_batches.py` emits a `rearm` step output and the workflow, when it is true, sleeps ~5 min and `gh workflow run`s **itself** with an incremented `chain_depth`. **Since 2026-08-03 the cron is only the BACKSTOP** — each submit-side stage of `pythia_pipeline_stage.yml` dispatches the poller itself right after uploading its batch state (`pythia_pipeline_stage.yml:294, 502, 749, 1006`), because the cron proved unreliable at ignition (a 3h38m gap between scheduled ticks on 2026-08-01). Do not remove the cron: it is what recovers a pipeline whose ignition dispatch failed. Re-arm fires only for `REARM_ACTIONS = {waiting, dispatched, already running, cooling down}`; **`already completed` and `STALLED` are terminal and must stay that way** — the final stage (`fc_collect_finalize`) emits no batch state, so its predecessor's state lingers for the artifact's 14-day retention and would otherwise re-arm forever. That is why `_dispatch_decision` splits the old `already running/done` reason into `already running` vs `already completed` — **do not re-collapse them**. Three layered guards: live-pipeline check, `PYTHIA_BATCH_MAX_WAIT_H` age bound, and a `POLLER_MAX_CHAIN` cap that is **derived, never hardcoded** — `_max_chain()` returns `max(200, int(wait_h*60/POLLER_TICK_MINUTES * 1.5) + 1)` with `POLLER_TICK_MINUTES = 5.5`, i.e. **393 links (~36h) at the production 24h ceiling**. An explicit `POLLER_MAX_CHAIN` still wins as an operator hard-stop. (The cap was once hardcoded to 200 under a comment claiming "~7-minute cadence, a little over 24h"; both halves were wrong and it killed a live chain 6h early — see the 2026-07-31 entry in Known failure modes.) Self-dispatch is safe because the workflow already has `permissions: actions: write` and `concurrency: cancel-in-progress: false` (a self-dispatch queues rather than cancelling), and `workflow_dispatch` bypasses the activity gate so chained ticks always poll. Each tick also writes `diagnostics/poll_decision.json` (per-pipeline verdict, batch states, action) uploaded as **`pythia-poller-decisions`** — previously dispatch reasoning existed only in job logs, which expire, so a stall had no durable evidence trail. Env: `POLLER_CHAIN_DEPTH`, `POLLER_MAX_CHAIN`, `POLLER_DECISION_PATH`. Tests: `tests/test_poll_llm_batches_guard.py`. **The workflow's activity gate is `scripts/ci/check_pipeline_active.py --emit active` (2026-08-03)** — the SAME decision core the weekly-ingest gate uses, with the opposite sense (`active` = in flight; `proceed` = NOT in flight). It was previously an inline shell copy implementing only the artifact-age half of the test, so for ~72h after every pipeline finished each hourly cron tick paid a full checkout + pip install + provider poll only to print `already completed` for every pipeline (~70 wasted runs per cycle — this is what "the poller looks like it is still hunting for something" actually was). `actions/checkout@v4` now runs BEFORE the gate so the gate can be the shared script; the gate is stdlib + `gh` only, so idle ticks still skip setup-python and the pip install. **Both modes fail OPEN to `true`** — a broken gate must neither silence the weekly ingest nor stop the poller advancing a live pipeline — so the two senses cannot be collapsed into one negation. Note the cron itself still fires regardless: this shortens idle ticks, it does not remove them from the Actions run list.

**Tests**: `pythia/tests/test_llm_batch.py` (adapters mocked, state machine, custom-id charset + pipeline scoping, batch-tier pricing, body parity, salvage/counters), `forecaster/tests/test_batch_phase.py` (submit/collect/fallback via the `forecaster.cli.*` seams, cross-pipeline miss, sent-prompt fidelity), `horizon_scanner/tests/test_hs_stages.py` (mode matrix, enqueue body parity, replay + log-once, grounding/stage-state round-trips, hs_triage rerun idempotency, pack coexistence — this one runs in **horizon-scanner-ci**, not forecaster-ci), `forecaster/tests/test_prompt_v3_order.py`, `forecaster/tests/test_prompt_cache_wiring.py`, `tests/test_poll_llm_batches_guard.py`, `tests/test_check_pipeline_active.py` — **all wired into forecaster-ci since 2026-07-28** (they previously ran in no workflow).

## Testing

```bash
# Run all pythia tests (skip fastapi-dependent tests if not installed)
python3 -m pytest pythia/tests/ --ignore=pythia/tests/test_api_question_bundle.py -v

# Run cross-module tests (skip fastapi/openai-dependent tests)
python3 -m pytest tests/ --ignore-glob='tests/test_api_*' --ignore=tests/test_web_research.py -v

# Run RC calibration tests
python3 -m pytest resolver/tests/test_rc_calibration.py -v

# Run resolver tests
python3 -m pytest resolver/tests/ -v
```

**pytest config** (`pytest.ini`): 120s timeout, thread-based, log_cli enabled.
**Markers**: `@pytest.mark.db`, `@pytest.mark.allow_network`, `@pytest.mark.slow`, `@pytest.mark.nightly`, `@pytest.mark.legacy_freeze`.

Some test files require `fastapi` or `openai` which may not be installed locally — exclude them with `--ignore` as shown above.

## Common environment variables

| Variable | Purpose |
|----------|---------|
| `PYTHIA_DB_URL` | DuckDB connection URL |
| `PYTHIA_LLM_PROFILE` | LLM profile: `prod` |
| `PYTHIA_TEST_MODE` | Pipeline test mode flag (1/true/yes = stamp all output as test data) |
| `PYTHIA_LLM_CONCURRENCY` | Max concurrent LLM calls |
| `PYTHIA_API_TOKEN` | Admin API auth token |
| `PYTHIA_WEB_RESEARCH_ENABLED` | Enable shared retriever (0/1) |
| `HS_MAX_WORKERS` | HS concurrent country workers |
| `FORECASTER_RESEARCH_MAX_WORKERS` | Research phase concurrency |
| `FORECASTER_SPD_MAX_WORKERS` | SPD phase concurrency |
| `PYTHIA_HS_RC_LEVEL*_*` | RC threshold overrides |
| `PYTHIA_HS_RC_DIST_WARN_*` | RC distribution warning thresholds |
| `PYTHIA_HS_ADVERSARIAL_MAX_PER_COUNTRY` | Max RC-promoted hazards per country given adversarial checks (default 2) |
| `PYTHIA_HS_RC_PASSES` | RC LLM passes per hazard (default 1, max 2; 2 only adds signal with a distinct `PYTHIA_RC_MODEL_PASS2`) |
| `PYTHIA_HS_TRIAGE_PASSES` | Triage LLM passes per hazard (default 1, max 2; 2 only adds signal with a distinct `PYTHIA_TRIAGE_MODEL_PASS2`) |
| `PYTHIA_ADVERSARIAL_CHECK_ENABLED` | Enable adversarial checks for RC L1+ (0/1, default 1) |
| `PYTHIA_GROUNDING_PRIMARY_BACKEND` | Primary grounding backend: `brave` (default), `openai`, or `gemini` |
| `BRAVE_SEARCH_API_KEY` | Brave Search API subscription token (required for Brave grounding) |
| `PYTHIA_BRAVE_MAX_RPS` | Max Brave Search API requests per second (default: 15, paid limit is 20) |
| `PYTHIA_HS_RESEARCH_WEB_SEARCH_ENABLED` | Enable web search for the legacy country-level RC evidence packs (0/1, **default 0** at every read site). The HS caller (`_maybe_build_country_evidence_pack`) is marked DEPRECATED and no longer called; the live readers are `forecaster/cli.py` and the debug bundle |
| `PYTHIA_PREDICTION_MARKETS_ENABLED` | Enable the prediction market retriever (0/1). **Unset falls through to `pythia/config.yaml`'s `prediction_markets.enabled: true`, so the code default is ON** — it is off in production only because both workflows pin it to "0" (see known failure modes) |
| `PYTHIA_GOOGLE_SPD_TIMEOUT_FLASH_SEC` | Gemini Flash SPD timeout (default 300s) |
| `PYTHIA_GOOGLE_SPD_TIMEOUT_PRO_SEC` | Gemini Pro SPD timeout (default 300s) |
| `ANTHROPIC_MAX_OUTPUT_TOKENS` | Anthropic max output tokens for SPD calls (default 16384) |
| `PYTHIA_ANTHROPIC_SPD_MAX_TOKENS` | Anthropic SPD-specific max tokens override (default `max(ANTHROPIC_MAX_OUTPUT_TOKENS, 32768)` = **32768** — deliberately above the 16384 general ceiling because Opus 5 charges adaptive thinking against the same budget; there is no separate thinking budget in the API). It does NOT govern the PA machine's extraction calls, whose answer budget is the rulebook's `extraction.max_output_tokens` (8000) |
| `ACAPS_USERNAME` / `ACAPS_PASSWORD` | ACAPS API credentials (the username var is `ACAPS_USERNAME`, not an email-named one) |
| `GDACS_MONTHS` | Number of months of GDACS history to fetch (default 3; 135 for full backfill to 2015) |
| `GDACS_REQUEST_DELAY` | Seconds between GDACS API requests per worker (default 1.0) |
| `GDACS_ENRICH_WORKERS` | Thread-pool size for GDACS per-event RSS enrichment (default 6; 1 = sequential) |
| `GDACS_FORCE_RSS` | Force static RSS feed path ("1" to enable; overrides auto-detection) |
| `GDACS_FORCE_JSON` | Force JSON search API path ("1" to enable; overrides auto-detection) |
| `FEWSNET_MONTHS` | Number of months of FEWS NET IPC history to fetch (default 12; 120 for backfill to 2016) |
| `FEWSNET_REQUEST_DELAY` | Seconds between FEWS NET API retries (default 1.0) |
| `IPC_API_KEY` | API key for api.ipcinfo.org (required for the IPC connector; OPTIONAL for the PA machine's drought path, which falls back to the `phase3plus_in_need` rows already in `facts_resolved`) |
| `EMDAT_API_KEY` | EM-DAT GraphQL API bearer token (PA resolution machine, ladder rung 1). Unset → the rung falls back to the cached window where one exists (STALE, recorded as `served_from_cache`) and reports UNAVAILABLE only when the cache is empty too |
| `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` | Also backs the PA machine's `hazard_extraction` role (ladder rung 2, Haiku-class document transcription) |
| `IDMC_API_KEY` | IDMC IDU client id, sent as the `client_id` query param (PA resolution machine, lower-bound rung). **Falls back to `IDMC_HELIX_CLIENT_ID`** — the same IDMC credential, same `client_id` param, same `helix-tools-api.idmcdb.org/external-api/idus/` host (this rung uses `/all/`, the ingestion path `/last-180-days/`) — so one secret serves both. **Not** `IDMC_API_TOKEN`: that is a bearer token for `backend.idmcdb.org`, and its presence is a feature flag (`run_connectors.py` derives `RESOLVER_SKIP_IDMC` from it), so it must never be repurposed. Neither set → the rung reports UNAVAILABLE, recorded on the row |
| `RELIEFWEB_APPNAME` | ReliefWeb `appname` parameter for the silence sweep (falls back to `pythia-resolution-machine` with a warning) |
| `IPC_API_MONTHS` | Number of months of IPC API history to fetch (default 24; IPC analyses are infrequent) |
| `IPC_API_REQUEST_DELAY` | Seconds between IPC API retries (default 1.0) |
| `GDELT_EVENTS_DAYS` | Number of days of GDELT 1.0 daily exports to fetch (default 90) |
| `GDELT_EVENTS_REQUEST_DELAY` | Seconds between GDELT daily file downloads (default 1.0) |
| `ACLED_USER_AGENT` | User-Agent sent on the ACLED OAuth token requests (default a browser-shaped string). The grants also send `Accept: application/json`; a bare `python-requests` UA with no Accept header is the shape a WAF hands the website to |
| `ACLED_TOKEN_CACHE_PATH` | Where the cross-process ACLED token cache lives (default `<tempdir>/pythia_acled_token.json`, deliberately outside the checkout so no artifact upload sweeps it up; empty string disables it). One token per run: every ACLED process reads it before asking for a token and writes it after obtaining one |
| `PYTHIA_RUN_LOG_DIR` | Directory for the run-scoped evidence streams the resolver debug bundle reads (HTTP requests, response envelopes, the hazard cell + figure ledgers). **Unset = recording OFF**, which is the code default and what keeps local runs and tests unchanged; `resolver_update.yml` sets it to `diagnostics/run_log` |
| `PYTHIA_HTTP_RECORD` | Set to 0 to keep the HTTP recorder off while the other streams record (default 1 when `PYTHIA_RUN_LOG_DIR` is set) |
| `PYTHIA_RUN_STARTED_AT` | Run start stamp the resolver debug bundle reports (written by the workflow's diagnostics-prep step) |
| `PYTHIA_STEP_TIMINGS_JSON` | Path to a per-step timing JSON the resolver debug bundle renders as `run/step_timings.csv` (absent = the CSV is empty and says so) |
| `PYTHIA_BUNDLE_KEEP_STAGING` | Keep the resolver debug bundle's staging tree after the zip is written (0/1, default 0; for debugging the builder itself) |
| `PYTHIA_BUNDLE_WORKFLOW_LOGS` | Capture the cycle's Actions logs into the debug bundle (0/1, default 1; needs `GITHUB_TOKEN` + `GITHUB_REPOSITORY`, degrades to a recorded problem without them) |
| `PYTHIA_BUNDLE_FETCH_PROVIDER_OBJECTS` | Fetch the raw provider batch objects into the debug bundle (0/1, default 1; needs the provider keys, degrades to a recorded skip without them) |
| `PYTHIA_INTERPRETER_MAX_PACK_TOKENS` | Current-run bundle hard token cap (default 250000; low-ranked question records truncated, recorded in the manifest) |
| `PYTHIA_INTERPRETER_TOP_N` | Attention list length for the current-run bundle's deltas (default 8) |
| `PYTHIA_INTERPRETER_PER_CAPITA_FLOOR` | Absolute `eiv_nominal` floor for the per-capita attention ranking (default 10000) |
| `PYTHIA_INTERPRETER_ENABLED` | Interpreter kill switch (default 1) |
| `PYTHIA_INTERPRETER_MODEL_ID` | Purpose-specific override, wins over the config `interpreter` role |
| `PYTHIA_INTERPRETER_TEMPLATE_VERSION` | Interpreter template version (default v4) |
| `PYTHIA_INTERPRETER_MAX_ENTRIES` | How many entries the whole report carries (default 5; split 60/40 worsening/heavy-burden, either side handing unused slots to the other) |
| `PYTHIA_INTERPRETER_UNUSUAL_PERCENTILE` | The "unusual" key: a forecast's divergence percentile within its own run (default 0.80) |
| `PYTHIA_INTERPRETER_MINPROB` (and `_MINPROB_{METRIC}`) | The heavy-burden LEVEL key: probability above the threshold in ANY horizon (default 0.25). Also the binary movement threshold, in probability points |
| `PYTHIA_INTERPRETER_GATE_MODE` | `delta` (v4: the movement key) or `level` (the pre-v4 rollback, without a deploy) |
| `PYTHIA_INTERPRETER_MAX_WATCHLIST` | Watchlist lines before the rest are left to the appendix question table (default 10) |
| `PYTHIA_INTERPRETER_MAX_TENSIONS_PER_ENTRY` | Reconciled contradictions per entry (default 2) |
| `PYTHIA_INTERPRETER_GENERIC_PHRASE_MAX` | Sections a stock phrase may appear in before the lint flags it (default 3) |
| `PYTHIA_INTERPRETER_NOVEL_EVIDENCE_SCOPE` | `disagreement` (default) or `all` — novel evidence from a second reader that agreed is a research note, and covering all ten questions ran to two pages |
| `PYTHIA_INTERPRETER_THRESHOLD_{PA,FATALITIES,PHASE3}` + `_POP_SHARE` | What counts as an impact worth mobilising against; the share exists so a flat 50,000 floor does not exclude every small island state |
| `PYTHIA_INTERPRETER_BASERATE_MIN_OBS` | Below this many observations an anchor is thin: demoted, labelled, never dropped (default 12) |
| `PYTHIA_INTERPRETER_LEAD_TIME_{HAZARD}` | Months between a decision and the month it covers, for the decision calendar (DR 3, ACE 2, FL/TC 1) |
| `PYTHIA_INTERPRETER_SIBYL_{DISAGREEMENT_RATIO,UNSETTLED_SHARE,NOVEL_FACTS}` | The second reader's section (defaults 1.5, 0.25, 2) |
| `PYTHIA_INTERPRETER_SECTOR_{LIST_SIZE,MIN_RANK_GAP}` | The ACAPS rank-gap lists (defaults 5, 5) |
| `PYTHIA_INTERPRETER_MIN_RESOLVED_FOR_SKILL` / `_SKILL_BOOTSTRAP` | Part B's small-sample floor and bootstrap size (defaults 100, 2000) |
| `PYTHIA_PUBLIC_BASE_URL` | Base URL for absolute question links in the PDF (default https://fredforecaster.org) |
| `PYTHIA_INTERPRETER_THINKING` | Anthropic `output_config.effort` level for the interpreter call (default high) |
| `PYTHIA_INTERPRETER_MAX_OUTPUT_TOKENS` | Interpreter max_tokens — thinking shares this budget (default 32768) |
| `PYTHIA_INTERPRETER_STRICT_VALIDATION` | When 1, a validation failure suppresses publication (Phase 4 consumer; default 0) |
| `PYTHIA_INTERPRETER_VALIDATION_RETRIES` | Correction passes after a failed validation (default 1; 0 disables) |

| `PYTHIA_HS_ONLY_COUNTRIES` | Restrict HS to specific countries (comma-separated ISO3s) |
| `PYTHIA_HS_FALLBACK_MODEL_SPECS` | Fallback model for HS triage (default from profile `hs_fallback`) |
| `PYTHIA_HS_LLM_MAX_ATTEMPTS` | Max retry attempts for HS triage LLM calls (default 3) |
| `PYTHIA_HS_GEMINI_TIMEOUT_SEC` | Gemini timeout for HS triage (default 120s) |
| `PYTHIA_DUCKDB_MEMORY_LIMIT` | DuckDB memory limit for API (default "150MB"; applied to the read connection, the db_sync introspection connection, and ui_runs writes) |
| `PYTHIA_COMPUTE_DUCKDB_MEMORY_LIMIT` / `PYTHIA_COMPUTE_DUCKDB_THREADS` | DuckDB runaway guard on the COMPUTE path (defaults "4GB" / "4", applied by `_db_utils.apply_compute_memory_guard` in compute_resolutions/scores/calibration/advice, the AI bundles and the interpreter — deliberately generous; without any limit DuckDB takes ~80% of runner RAM and an OOM-killed step leaves a stale WAL) |
| `PYTHIA_DUCKDB_THREADS` | DuckDB thread count for API (default "2") |
| `PYTHIA_MAX_CONCURRENT_HEAVY` | Max concurrent heavy API queries (default "2"; covers export *build* time only since the temp-file download refactor — set to 1 on Render) |
| `PYTHIA_ALLOW_INPROCESS_RUN` | Allow `/v1/run` to launch the HS+forecaster pipeline inside the API process (0/1, default 0 — must stay off on Render; local dev sets 1) |
| `PYTHIA_EXPORT_TMP_DIR` | Directory for spilled/cached download exports (default: system tmp + `/pythia-exports`; must NOT point at the Render persistent disk `/data`) |
| `PYTHIA_CORS_ALLOW_ORIGINS` | CORS allowed origins for API (default "*") |
| `PYTHIA_SYNC_FROM_ARTIFACTS` | Enable DB sync from GitHub artifacts on API startup |
| `GPT5_CALL_TIMEOUT_SEC` | GPT-5.x SPD call timeout (default 300s) |
| `GEMINI_CALL_TIMEOUT_SEC` | Gemini SPD call timeout (default 300s) |
| `PYTHIA_SPD_ENSEMBLE_SPECS` | Override SPD ensemble at runtime (comma-separated `provider:model_id`) |
| `PYTHIA_USE_CALIBRATION_WEIGHTS` | Apply calibration weights to SPD ensemble aggregation (0/1, default 1) |
| `PYTHIA_BLOCK_PROVIDERS` | Comma-separated provider names to exclude from ensemble |
| `PYTHIA_RC_MODEL_PASS1` / `PYTHIA_RC_MODEL_PASS2` | Override RC LLM model per pass |
| `PYTHIA_CREDIT_RETRY_PAUSE_{PROVIDER}` | Credit-retry pause in seconds per provider (default: OpenAI=900, Anthropic=300, Google=600) |
| `PYTHIA_CREDIT_RETRY_MAX_{PROVIDER}` | Credit-retry max attempts per provider (default: 3 for all) |
| `PYTHIA_BATCH_API_ENABLED` | Master switch for provider Batch-API submission (0/1, default 0) |
| `PYTHIA_BATCH_PROVIDERS` | Providers allowed to batch (default "openai,anthropic,google"; others stay sync) |
| `PYTHIA_BATCH_MAX_WAIT_H` | Max hours to wait on a provider batch before cancel + sync fallback (default 24) |
| `PYTHIA_OPENAI_BATCH_FILE_WAIT_SEC` / `PYTHIA_OPENAI_BATCH_VALIDATE_WAIT_SEC` / `PYTHIA_OPENAI_BATCH_SUBMIT_ATTEMPTS` | OpenAI submit-time guards (defaults 60 / 120 / 3): wait for the uploaded JSONL to report `processed`, watch the new batch leave `validating`, and re-upload on a file-access rejection |
| `PYTHIA_PROMPT_V3_ORDER` | Static-first prompt assembly for cache-friendly prefixes (0/1, code default 0; set to 1 in the three production workflows since 2026-07-27) |
| `PYTHIA_PROMPT_CACHE_ENABLED` | Anthropic cache_control blocks + OpenAI prompt_cache_key (0/1, code default 0; set to 1 in the three production workflows since 2026-07-27) |
| `PYTHIA_SPD_V2_USE_BAYESMC` / `PYTHIA_SPD_V2_WRITE_BOTH` | Gate whether `ensemble_bayesmc_v2` is produced at all (both default "0" in `forecaster/cli.py`) — with both off, Track 1 writes only the mean aggregation |
| `PYTHIA_HS_PRIORITY_THRESHOLD` | Triage-score cutoff for the "priority" tier that routes RC-0 questions to Track 2 (default 0.50) |
| `PYTHIA_PROVIDER_FAILURE_THRESHOLD` / `PYTHIA_PROVIDER_COOLDOWN_SECONDS` | Provider-level circuit breaker in `forecaster/providers.py` (defaults 6 consecutive failures / 60s cooldown) — separate from, and in addition to, the Brave breaker |
| `PYTHIA_LLM_RETRIES` / `PYTHIA_GOOGLE_SPD_RETRIES` | Transient-retry depth for SPD calls (default 3) |
| `PYTHIA_TRIAGE_MODEL_PASS1` / `PYTHIA_TRIAGE_MODEL_PASS2` | Override the triage LLM model per pass |
| `PYTHIA_SCENARIO_MODEL_ID`, `PYTHIA_TRACK2_MODEL_ID`, `PYTHIA_GROUNDING_MODEL_ID`, `PYTHIA_WEB_RESEARCH_MODEL_ID`, `HS_MODEL_ID` | The purpose-specific overrides that win over the config `roles` block (see the model-registry section) |
| `PYTHIA_SPD_HARD_FAIL` | Make SPD failures fatal to the in-process pipeline runner |
| `CANONICAL_DB_ARTIFACT_NAME` / `CANONICAL_DB_RUN_ID` / `CANONICAL_DB_WORKFLOW` | Steer the API's canonical-DB artifact discovery (`pythia/api/db_sync.py`) |
| `PYTHIA_POPULATION_CSV_PATH` | Population table backing per-capita risk-index normalization |

Provider API keys: `OPENAI_API_KEY`, `GEMINI_API_KEY` (`GOOGLE_API_KEY` is accepted as a fallback), `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`. Connector auth: `ACLED_USERNAME`/`ACLED_PASSWORD` (or `ACLED_REFRESH_TOKEN`), `GO_API_TOKEN` (IFRC), `IDMC_API_TOKEN`, `ACAPS_USERNAME`/`ACAPS_PASSWORD`, `IPC_API_KEY`, `BRAVE_SEARCH_API_KEY`. API auth accepts `PYTHIA_API_TOKEN` or `PYTHIA_API_KEY`.

## Test mode

Test mode runs the identical pipeline as production. The only difference is that every row written during a test run carries `is_test = TRUE` in a dedicated boolean column. The website hides test data by default; users can opt in via the "Test ON/OFF" toggle in the navigation bar.

**Architecture:**
- `pythia/test_mode.py` — single source of truth; `is_test_mode()` reads `PYTHIA_TEST_MODE` env var
- `is_test BOOLEAN DEFAULT FALSE` column on all pipeline tables (hs_runs, hs_scenarios, hs_triage, hs_country_reports, hs_hazard_tail_packs, hs_adversarial_checks, questions, forecasts_ensemble, forecasts_raw, llm_calls, question_research, question_run_metrics, scenarios, question_context, run_provenance, resolutions, scores, eiv_scores)
- API endpoints accept `?include_test=true` query parameter (default: hidden)
- Calibration (`compute_calibration_pythia`, `generate_calibration_advice`) excludes test data automatically
- Downloads include an `is_test` column
- `scripts/mark_test_runs.py` retroactively marks existing runs as test data
- `.github/workflows/mark_test_runs.yml` workflow wraps the script for CI use
- `scripts/reconcile_llm_calls_is_test.py` self-heals `llm_calls.is_test` (flips FALSE→TRUE for calls whose run_id/hs_run_id belongs to a test run — repairs the historical forecaster stamping gap so test costs don't leak into non-test cost views); idempotent, wired into `compute_calibration_pythia.yml` every cycle. The forward fix is `log_forecaster_llm_call` inheriting `is_test_mode()` by default in `forecaster/llm_logging.py`

**Triggering test mode:**
- Set `PYTHIA_TEST_MODE=1` in environment (or `true`/`yes`)
- `run_horizon_scanner.yml` has a `test_mode` checkbox input
- `manual_test.yaml` always sets `PYTHIA_TEST_MODE=1`

## Build and run

```bash
# Install dependencies
pip install -r python_library_requirements.txt

# Initialize DB schema
python3 -c "from pythia.db.schema import ensure_schema; ensure_schema()"

# Run Resolver pipeline (fetch facts from ACLED + IDMC -> DuckDB)
python3 -m resolver.tools.run_pipeline
# Or specific connectors: python3 -m resolver.tools.run_pipeline --connectors acled idmc

# Ingest ALL structured data (conflict forecasts, ACAPS, IPC, ReliefWeb, NMME)
python3 -m pythia.tools.ingest_structured_data
# Filter by source: python3 -m pythia.tools.ingest_structured_data --sources conflict acaps ipc
# Valid sources: views, conflictforecast, acledcast, acaps_inform_severity,
#   acaps_risk_radar, acaps_daily_monitoring, acaps_humanitarian_access, ipc, reliefweb, acled_political, nmme, gdacs, fewsnet_ipc, ipc_api, gdelt
# Aliases: acaps (all 4 ACAPS sources), conflict (views+conflictforecast+acledcast)

# Standalone entry points (still work independently):
python3 -m resolver.tools.ingest_nmme                        # NMME only
python3 -m resolver.tools.fetch_conflict_forecasts            # conflict forecasts only
python3 -m resolver.tools.fetch_conflict_forecasts --sources views conflictforecast_org acled_cast

# Refresh CrisisWatch data — CI path (Wayback Machine, no browser needed)
python3 -m scripts.refresh_crisiswatch --source wayback --only-if-newer --max-age-days 60 --expect-edition-by-day 7
# Local live-site path (requires Playwright: pip install playwright && playwright install chromium)
python3 -m scripts.refresh_crisiswatch [--output PATH] [--verbose] [--channel chrome]

# Run Horizon Scanner
python3 -m horizon_scanner.horizon_scanner

# Run Forecaster
python3 -m forecaster.cli --mode pythia

# Post-forecast pipeline
python3 -m pythia.tools.compute_resolutions
python3 -m pythia.tools.compute_scores
python3 -m pythia.tools.compute_calibration_pythia
python3 -m pythia.tools.generate_calibration_advice

# API server
uvicorn pythia.api.app:app --reload --port 8000

# Dashboard
cd web && npm install && npm run dev
```

## Prompt editing

Before editing any prompt source files, always run `bash scripts/snapshot_prompts.sh` first. This archives the current prompts before changes so the About page can show historical versions. Commit the snapshot alongside the prompt edits.

Key prompt files:
- `forecaster/prompts.py` — Forecaster SPD prompt builder
- `horizon_scanner/rc_prompts.py` — Per-hazard RC prompt builders (ACE, DR, FL, TC; the HW builder is retained as dead code — HW is in `BLOCKED_HAZARDS`)
- `horizon_scanner/hs_triage_prompts.py` — Per-hazard triage prompt builders
- `horizon_scanner/rc_grounding_prompts.py` — RC-specific grounding queries (per-hazard)
- `horizon_scanner/hs_triage_grounding_prompts.py` — Triage-specific grounding queries (per-hazard)
- `pythia/web_research/backends/brave_search.py` — Brave Search API grounding backend (primary, deterministic, $5/1K queries). Wired to Brave circuit breaker: checks `is_tripped()` at entry, records success/failure per query.
- `pythia/web_research/brave_circuit_breaker.py` — Thread-safe circuit breaker for Brave Search API. Trips after 3 consecutive failures; short-circuits all subsequent Brave calls when tripped. Module-level singleton, reset per HS run.
- `pythia/web_research/backends/gemini_grounding.py` — Gemini grounding backend (second fallback)

## Overview editing

Before editing `docs/fred_overview.md`, always run `bash scripts/snapshot_overview.sh` first. This archives the current overview before changes so the About page can show historical versions. Commit the snapshot alongside the overview edits.

## Code conventions

- Copyright header on every file: `# Pythia / Copyright (c) 2025 Kevin Wyjad`
- License: Pythia Non-Commercial Public License v1.0
- DuckDB is the only database backend (no Postgres/SQLite)
- `pythia/db/schema.py` is authoritative for Pythia tables; `resolver/db/schema.sql` for Resolver tables
- Config loaded via `pythia.config.load()` which reads `pythia/config.yaml`
- LLM providers abstracted through `forecaster/providers.py` (OpenAI, Google, Anthropic — xAI/Kimi/DeepSeek support removed 2026-07-07)
- All LLM calls logged to `llm_calls` table with cost, tokens, latency, error tracking. This includes RC/triage LLM passes (logged by providers module), grounding calls (logged explicitly in `_run_grounding_for_hazard` via `log_hs_llm_call` with `hazard_code=grounding_{hazard}`), and adversarial checks (logged by `web_research` module for evidence fetches and by providers module for synthesis calls).
- Env vars override config defaults; threshold env vars use `_env_float()` pattern
- Structured data connectors follow a standard pattern: `fetch_*()` → `store_*()` → `load_*()` → `format_*_for_prompt()` / `format_*_for_spd()`
- **Reasoning traces** are required in SPD output JSON for Track 1 (full ensemble). The `reasoning_trace` object captures the prior SPD, sequential evidence updates with numeric deltas, point estimate, and RC assessment. Track 2 requires only `prior` and `rc_assessment`. Traces are stored in `reasoning_trace_json` columns on `forecasts_raw` and `forecasts_ensemble`, validated by `forecaster/trace_validation.py` (diagnostic only), and analyzed by `generate_calibration_advice.py` for prior anchoring diagnostics.
- **Question-level web research pipeline is deprecated**: The `fetch_evidence_pack` / `_build_question_evidence_queries` / `_merge_question_evidence_packs` flow is bypassed. SPD prompts now receive structured data directly via `_load_structured_data()`: conflict forecasts, ReliefWeb, HDX Signals, HS grounding evidence (RC/triage grounding packs from `hs_hazard_tail_packs`, rendered as "HS GROUNDING EVIDENCE"), ACAPS, IPC, ACLED political, NMME, ENSO, seasonal TC, GDACS event history (FL/DR/TC), GDELT (ACE), CrisisWatch (ACE), adversarial checks (RC L1+). The `question_research` table is no longer populated by the pipeline (only placeholder rows). Env vars `PYTHIA_RETRIEVER_ENABLED`, `PYTHIA_WEB_RESEARCH_ENABLED`, `PYTHIA_SPD_WEB_SEARCH_ENABLED`, `PYTHIA_FORECASTER_SELF_SEARCH` are set to "0" in the workflow.
- HS pipeline is per-hazard: each hazard gets its own RC grounding, RC call, triage grounding, and triage call (single pass each by default on gemini-3.5-flash; `PYTHIA_HS_RC_PASSES`/`PYTHIA_HS_TRIAGE_PASSES=2` restore the 2-pass merge — only useful with a distinct pass-2 model)
- **Grounding backend order**: Brave Search API is the primary grounding backend (direct, deterministic, $5/1K queries); OpenAI GPT-4.1-mini web search is the first fallback; Gemini Google Search grounding is the second fallback. Override with `PYTHIA_GROUNDING_PRIMARY_BACKEND=openai` or `=gemini` to change the primary. All three backends are attempted in order until one returns sources. RC, triage, and adversarial grounding all use this three-tier chain. Triage grounding calls are logged to `llm_calls` with `hazard_code=TRIAGE_GROUNDING_{hazard}` (RC grounding logged as `grounding_{hazard}`).
- RC and triage grounding use different signal categories and recency windows (RC: TRIGGER/DAMPENER/BASELINE; triage: SITUATION/RESPONSE/FORECAST/VULNERABILITY)
- **Grounding recency windows**: RC grounding uses moderate windows (ACE=30d, DR=60d, FL=30d, HW=30d, TC=60d) balancing change signal detection with sufficient search coverage for a monthly forecast cycle. Triage grounding uses wider windows (ACE=60d, DR=90d, FL=60d, HW=60d, TC=60d) for the operational picture. Both are defined in their respective `RECENCY_DAYS` dicts.
- **Grounding source steering**: All 10 grounding prompts (5 RC + 5 triage) include a `PRIORITIZE THESE SOURCES` section with hazard-specific source priority lists and a `RECENCY FILTER` instruction. RC prompts prioritize wire services and specialist sources for novelty; triage prompts elevate OCHA/humanitarian sources for the operational picture.

## LLM credit-retry safety net

When LLM providers return billing/quota-exhaustion errors (distinct from transient rate limits), a credit-retry wrapper pauses and retries, waiting for auto-recharge. Implemented in `forecaster/providers.py` inside `call_chat_ms`, wrapping the existing transient-retry inner loop.

**Provider-specific config** (`_CREDIT_RETRY_CONFIG`):
- OpenAI: 900s pause, 3 retries (worst-case 45min)
- Anthropic: 300s pause, 3 retries (worst-case 15min)
- Google: 600s pause, 3 retries (worst-case 30min)
- Providers outside the table (none currently) get no credit retry

**Detection** (`_is_billing_error`): Conservative per-provider rules that distinguish billing errors from rate limits. OpenAI: 429 + quota/billing keywords but NOT "rate limit". Anthropic: 400/403 + insufficient/billing/blocked. Google: 429 + RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED + quota/billing. Returns False when uncertain.

**Env-var overrides**: `PYTHIA_CREDIT_RETRY_PAUSE_{PROVIDER}` (seconds), `PYTHIA_CREDIT_RETRY_MAX_{PROVIDER}` (count). E.g. `PYTHIA_CREDIT_RETRY_PAUSE_OPENAI=600` reduces OpenAI pause to 10 minutes.

**Usage tracking**: `credit_retries_used`, `credit_retry_pauses_sec`, `billing_error_detected` fields added to the usage dict when billing errors are detected.

## Brave Search circuit breaker

Thread-safe circuit breaker (`pythia/web_research/brave_circuit_breaker.py`) that tracks consecutive Brave API failures across all call sites. When 3 consecutive raw API calls return errors, the breaker trips and all subsequent Brave calls are short-circuited (return immediately with `circuit_breaker_tripped` error). The fallback chain (OpenAI → Gemini) takes over grounding.

**Safety gate**: When the breaker is tripped, `_write_hs_triage` checks whether each hazard received grounding evidence (via `_hazard_has_grounding()` querying `llm_calls`). Hazards without grounding are blocked from forecasting (`need_full_spd = False`, `data_quality.brave_budget_gate = "blocked_no_grounding"`). Hazards that completed grounding before the breaker tripped proceed normally.

**Lifecycle**: Breaker resets at the start of each HS run (`main()` → `reset_brave_breaker()`). Stats logged at run end. `HS_BRAVE_BREAKER_TRIPPED` emitted as workflow output.

## Known failure modes

Older post-mortems — bugs fixed in code that has since been rewritten, or whose current
behaviour is described in the reference sections above — live in
[`docs/archive/claude_md_failure_modes.md`](docs/archive/claude_md_failure_modes.md). **Every rule
they taught is in [`## Invariants`](#invariants) at the top of this file**; check both before
re-reporting an issue, and check `docs/audit_2026-07.md` too.

- **The reconciliation called an idempotent upsert a failure and could not tell three sources on one table apart** (fixed, 2026-09-04, from run 33841370196 — Group G of the resolver-faults series). (G1) `connectors_claiming_rows_show_a_table_delta` compared a connector's claimed count against `rows_after - rows_before` of its target table and flagged NMME and ACAPS Risk Radar: both had upserted onto keys that already existed, so the count did not move, and a false alarm in a health check trains the reader to ignore it. The claim itself was also incomparable — `counts.written` was a COUNTRY count for per-country sources and a row count for self-storing ones, and no writer reported the rows it actually inserted or replaced. (G2) VIEWS, conflictforecast and ACLED CAST all write `conflict_forecasts`, and all three were marked "agrees" against a single delta of 546; had CAST written nothing, the check would still have passed. Fixed by attributing writes rather than counting rows: `facts_resolved` and `facts_deltas` gained `updated_at`, stamped by `write_facts_tables` on every MERGE (matched or new; `created_at` never moves for a matched row, and a pre-stamp table gains the column on `init_schema`); every per-country store (`acaps.store_*`, `store_reliefweb_reports`, `store_acled_political_events`) returns the rows it wrote and the orchestrator reports the sum as `counts.rows_written` beside the country count (self-storing sources carry theirs on a sentinel key — `__rows_written__` for NMME, whose `ingest_nmme.main` now returns its upsert result); and the check is `connectors_claiming_rows_touched_their_source_rows`, which counts rows of the target table WITHIN the connector's own source filter (`summarize_all_phases._CONNECTORS`'s `where`) whose write stamp (`updated_at`, else `fetched_at`, else `created_at`, else `fetch_date`) is at or after `PYTHIA_RUN_STARTED_AT`, and fails a connector that claimed rows and stamped none. `checks/reconciliation.md` gained `rows written (MERGE)`, `source filter` and `touched since run start` columns, with the table delta kept for context only; the run summary's "Δ this run" now prints the MERGE count. (G3) The six checks the review listed all exist by name and are pinned as a set by `test_the_six_requested_contradiction_checks_are_all_registered`. **Rule: see the new "Verification and telemetry" line in `## Invariants`.** Acceptance after the next Resolver Update: `connectors_claiming_rows_touched_their_source_rows` PASS with NMME and Risk Radar no longer named; `SELECT COUNT(*) FROM facts_resolved WHERE updated_at >= <run start>` greater than 0; and the reconciliation table shows a non-blank `touched since run start` for each of the three conflict-forecast sources separately. Tests: `resolver/tests/test_group_g_reconciliation.py`, `pythia/tests/test_ingest_rows_written.py` (named in forecaster-ci), one case in `resolver/tests/test_resolver_debug_bundle.py`.
- **56 outlook rows for 8 outlooks, an August update dated May, a category that changed between runs, "October 2025" in a date column, and no NOAA release reachable** (fixed, 2026-09-04, from run 33841370196 — Group F of the resolver-faults series). (F1) `seasonal_tc_outlooks` was keyed on `(basin, source, fetched_at)`, so every Phase 4 run appended a complete copy of everything it found; the duplicates produced most of the bundle's collision warnings and hid the one real misread. The table is rebuilt on `(basin, source, forecast_season, issue_date_key, category)` with an ON CONFLICT upsert that keeps the first `fetched_at`; the migration collapses the legacy rows to the earliest fetch per key, parses `issue_date` out of `raw_json`, and deletes the two BoM rows whose "season" was the climatology baseline `1980-81`. (F2) The ATL `august_update` carried `issue_date 2026-05-28` beside August figures. The 3 September fix read the header's own `Issued:` line first, but on the real PDF the quote of the May forecast sits INSIDE the 1,500-character header, so header-first still picked May. `resolve_issue_date` now takes the `Issued:` line whose month matches the filename's month, falls back to the PDF's CreationDate when the only line contradicts the filename, and otherwise keeps the contradiction WITH a flag. A re-parse with the corrected date supersedes the misread row in the store, so the next Phase 4 run leaves one August row dated August. (F3) The NWP outlook of 11 May was `extended_range` in July's runs and `pre_season` in September's: the URL-month rule from the 3 September fix says May means pre-season, which is true of the Atlantic and false of the NW Pacific, where May is the extended-range product. The document's own title decides now, and the URL month is the fallback for a title naming nothing. (F4) BoM's `issue_date` was the free text "October 2025" (and "October {season_year}" when the page had no date line at all); SP, SWI and NIO carried nothing. Every scraper's date now goes through `seasonal_tc.dates.parse_issue_date`: a real date, or the first of a month with `month_precision_only`, or NULL with `no_issue_date_in_source` / `climatology_context_has_no_issue_date`; BoM and SWI read their pages' own dating lines; and `named_storms_reason` says why a basin carries no count (BoM publishes probabilities, IMD publishes no count outlook). `every_tc_outlook_has_a_parseable_issue_date_or_a_reason` fails on a row with neither, and on a table still in the legacy shape. (F5) NOAA: the generated candidate `noaa-predicts-below-normal-2026-atlantic-hurricane-season` was the right URL all along, and "0 reachable" was noaa.gov refusing the `PythiaBot/1.0` User-Agent (bom.gov.au did the same in July). The fetch sends a browser UA and logs the HTTP status per URL, and the 2026 initial, update and Central Pacific releases are curated in `KNOWN_URLS[2026]` — found by search from the sandbox, where noaa.gov is denied, so the first live Phase 4 run is the verification. **Rules: see the four new "Connectors and upstream data" lines in `## Invariants`.** Acceptance after the next Resolver Update: `SELECT COUNT(*), COUNT(DISTINCT (basin, source, forecast_season, category)) FROM seasonal_tc_outlooks` returns two equal numbers; `SELECT issue_date, named_storms_forecast FROM seasonal_tc_outlooks WHERE basin='ATL' AND category='august_update'` returns an August date beside 10; `SELECT COUNT(*) FROM seasonal_tc_outlooks WHERE issue_date IS NULL AND issue_date_reason IS NULL` is 0; `SELECT category FROM seasonal_tc_outlooks WHERE basin='NWP' AND issue_date='2026-05-11'` is `extended_range`; and a NOAA row appears for basin ATL. Tests: `horizon_scanner/seasonal_tc/tests/test_group_f_outlooks.py`, three cases in `resolver/tests/test_resolver_debug_bundle.py`.
- **EM-DAT locked out and named nowhere, a scorer that matched nothing for a year, five tables written by nobody, and facts published in 2027** (fixed, 2026-09-04, from run 33841370196 — Group E of the resolver-faults series). (E1) EM-DAT answered every call with "Invalid key passed or insufficient user access", `haz_raw_emdat` held 0 rows, and the cyclone pass exited non-zero with a message that said only that a source could not be read. The connector now classifies every failure (`classify_failure`: `auth_rejected`, `server_error`, `network`, `no_key`, `other`), prints a `::error::` naming `EMDAT_API_KEY` on a rejection, records every fetch on the `source_fetches` run-log stream (the bundle renders it as `hazard/source_fetches.csv`), and the machine's unreadable-source reason says "renew EMDAT_API_KEY or the account tier — a re-run will not fix this". `emdat_is_read_when_a_key_is_configured` FAILS when a key is set, a ladder hazard ran, and either the cache is empty or a fetch was rejected. **The key itself is the owner's to renew; the check stays red until it is.** (E2) Of the twelve empty tables the run's bundle listed, four were the forecast chain's (`scores`, `resolutions`, `eiv_scores`, `baseline_scored_forecasts` — all populated by the 4 September chain; a Resolver Update bundle cannot see them filled because the chain runs after it), `calibration_weights` is empty because no (hazard, metric) has reached the writer's twenty resolved questions (ACE/FATALITIES stands at 16), `views_scored_forecasts` was a real writer bug — `score_views` filtered `source = 'views'` against a column holding `VIEWS` and logged "nothing to score" on every round — `manifests`/`snapshots` belong to the snapshot-export CLI and are empty in the pipeline's DB by design, and `ipc_phases`, `pm_checks`, `meta_runs` (plus `gtmc1_runs`/`gtmc1_actors`, found the same way) had no writer anywhere. The scorer compares case-insensitively with the connector's literal pinned in its test; the dead tables are dropped when empty on schema init and no longer declared; `pythia/ipc_phases.py`, its tests, its `ipc` ingest group and its three loader call sites are deleted (the prompt builders' optional `ipc_phases` kwarg remains, never populated). `no_declared_active_table_is_empty_after_the_run` keeps a registry of what the Resolver Update writes (FAIL when empty) and what other workflows carry (reported with the writer named, and `calibration_weights` with the writer's own threshold re-derived). (E3) `facts_resolved` and `facts_deltas` carried `publication_date = 2027-03-01` and the freshness report called both fresh. Three writers: `enrich._fix_pub` raised every publication date to `as_of_date` — the END of the period, which for an IPC projection is next year — and then clamped it to today, so the FEWS NET reporting date the connector supplied became the run date on every run; `make_deltas.process_group` dropped the column so the storage fallback copied the period end into the delta uncapped; and `duckdb_io._ensure_frame_has_columns` did the same for any writer with no publication date at all (IDMC rows for the current month read as published on the 30th). All three fixed, `repair_publication_dates.py` rewrites the rows already written (Phase 5, every update), the freshness report measures from the newest value that has happened and counts the rest under `n_future`, and `no_fact_carries_a_publication_date_after_the_run_date` fails on any row dated after the bundle's date. **Rules: see the three new "Verification and telemetry" lines and the three new "Connectors and upstream data" lines in `## Invariants`.** Acceptance after the next Resolver Update: `SELECT COUNT(*) FROM facts_resolved WHERE TRY_CAST(publication_date AS DATE) > CURRENT_DATE` and the same over `facts_deltas` are both 0; `SELECT COUNT(*) FROM views_scored_forecasts` is greater than 0 after the next scoring round; `SELECT table_name FROM information_schema.tables WHERE table_name IN ('ipc_phases','pm_checks','meta_runs','gtmc1_runs','gtmc1_actors')` returns nothing; and `emdat_is_read_when_a_key_is_configured` turns from FAIL to PASS once the key is renewed. Tests: `resolver/tests/test_group_e_repairs.py` (named in forecaster-ci) and six cases in `resolver/tests/test_resolver_debug_bundle.py`.
- **The drought path wrote nothing for nine years, ignored a table it had just loaded, lost its main feed, and zeroed a month that had not happened** (fixed, 2026-09-04, from run 33841370196 — Group D of the resolver-faults series). (D1) `hazard/cell_ledger.csv` held 29,136 `unexplained_no_row` cells, 28,728 of them drought at 3,024 a year from 2017 to 2025, and `haz_base_rates_severity` had no DR row. Two causes. The backcast's drought months all came back INCONCLUSIVE (no indicator could speak for a past month) and their reason went only to the run-log ledger, which `haz_backcast.yml` never enables — so a bundle built from the database alone had nothing to say. Worse, those trigger rows (`triggered=false`) were counted by `compute_occurrence` as assessed quiet years, manufacturing a ~0% drought occurrence rate. Every no-row verdict now stamps `no_row_reason` into `trigger_detail_json` at decision time (drought in `decide_drought`; the sweep and ladder via `detect.record_no_row_reason`), an undecided cell stamps `assessed: false` and is left out of the occurrence denominator, the bundle reads the DB-side reason, and `no_assessed_cell_lacks_a_reason_for_having_no_row` + `drought_severity_base_rates_exist` fail when either fault returns. **DR severity rows still need frozen resolved drought values, which need indicator coverage of past months — that is what D3's time series supplies; the check will FAIL until the first backcast with the new feed lands, which is the honest state.** (D2) NMME merged 2,408 rows and the drought pass logged "seasonal_forecasts holds no usable rows" for every month: the entry's `where` pinned `lead_months = 1`, so a month whose lead-1 vintage was not in the table read as empty while other vintages about it sat there. `_snapshot_from_pythia_table` now admits every vintage that speaks for the month and prefers the shortest lead, the `where` is `variable = 'prate'` only, a miss prints `describe_pythia_table` (row count, matching rows, date span, distinct leads), and `nmme_indicator_read_for_every_month_seasonal_forecasts_covers` checks the cache against the table. (D3) All three ASAP warnings routes 404 on every month. JRC still publishes the monthly hotspot ASSESSMENT as one zipped CSV since October 2016 (`files/hotspots_ts.zip`, the route HDX Signals reads; HDX mirrors it as `asap-hotspots-monthly`, reached here through the CKAN API via the new `hdx-ckan://<dataset>` candidate form) with columns `asap0_id, date, hs_code (0 none / 1 hotspot / 2 major / 3 not assessed), hs_name, comment`. The new `asap_hotspots` entry (`time_series: true`) is parsed into ONE SNAPSHOT PER MONTH (`_parse_feed_series` — the old parser collapsed a dated series onto its newest month), cached once per process, treats `hs_code 3` as no reading and absence as unknown (ASAP assesses ~80 countries), and refuses a file that resolves 0 of N records naming the columns it saw. **Unverified from the sandbox** (ec.europa.eu and data.humdata.org are denied): the exact archive URL and whether the CSV carries a country name or ISO code beside `asap0_id` — if it carries only the id, the 0-of-N refusal fires loud on the first run and the repair is a vendored id-to-ISO3 map. The legacy `asap` entry stays, dormant (no url), so its cached snapshots still evaluate. Also: the drought pass had resolved 159 provisional zeros for 2026-08 and 2026-09 on the word of the one feed of three that answered. `drought.indicators.min_answered_for_zero: 2` makes such a cell INCONCLUSIVE (`indicator_too_few_feeds_for_zero`), and `no_drought_zero_rests_on_fewer_feeds_than_the_rulebook_demands` re-derives the count from every row's provenance. (D4) The 2026-09 pass wrote those 159 zeros on 4 September: the workflow's trailing window includes the CURRENT month, cyclone and flood suppress zeros through their detector coverage gate, and drought's `coverage_ok` is hardcoded true. `decide_drought` now returns PENDING (`month_in_progress`) for any month that has not ended. **The repair reaches the rows**: PENDING and INCONCLUSIVE write nothing, so `retract_unsupported_row` deletes an unfrozen row the verdict contradicts (any provisional row for an in-progress month; a provisional absence zero below the feed floor or without coverage), leaves a value row, a measured zero and a frozen row alone, and the run logs written / retracted / retained counts; `no_drought_resolution_for_a_month_still_in_progress` fails on any DR row at or after the bundle's month. The existing tests kept their meaning through `legacy_feed_rulebook()` in `test_hazard_resolution_drought.py` (the legacy warnings feed re-enabled at a test URL, floor 1), and the shipped values are pinned in `resolver/tests/test_hazard_resolution_drought_group_d.py`. **Rules: see the eight new "PA resolution machine" lines in `## Invariants`.** Acceptance after the next Resolver Update and the first nightly backcast: `no_assessed_cell_lacks_a_reason_for_having_no_row`, `nmme_indicator_read_for_every_month_seasonal_forecasts_covers`, `no_drought_zero_rests_on_fewer_feeds_than_the_rulebook_demands` and `no_drought_resolution_for_a_month_still_in_progress` PASS in `checks/contradictions.md`; `SELECT COUNT(*) FROM haz_resolutions WHERE hazard='DR' AND year*100+month >= <current month>` is 0; and `drought_severity_base_rates_exist` turns from FAIL to PASS once the backcast has frozen drought values on the hotspot series.
- **Seven small faults from run 33841370196, each of which put a wrong or missing row behind a green step** (fixed, 2026-09-05 — Group H of the resolver-faults series). (H1) The bundle's ACLED coverage query ran `SELECT MIN(ym)` against `acled_monthly_fatalities`, whose column is `month` (a DATE): the failure was one line in problems.md and the coverage CSV was silently absent. The query uses `strftime(MIN(month), '%Y-%m')`, and a new check runs every `DB_QUERIES` entry whose tables exist so a binder error is a FAIL naming the query. (H2) `run/step_timings.csv` was empty on every run because nothing ever wrote `PYTHIA_STEP_TIMINGS_JSON`; the bundle now reads this run's job from the GitHub jobs API (the bundle step holds `GITHUB_TOKEN`; `_pick_this_job` matches the job by its in-progress step, then runner name), writes `step_timings.json` beside the CSV, and says "no GITHUB_TOKEN" rather than shipping empty. (H3) `git.json` reported `dirty: true` for a CI run because `git status --porcelain` was taken whole and a run leaves hundreds of untracked outputs, and its `commit` was not the head of `recent_commits` because the log was path-filtered to `resolver/` and `horizon_scanner/`. `_git_state` counts tracked changes apart from untracked files (`dirty` means tracked only), logs from HEAD unfiltered, records `GITHUB_SHA` and whether HEAD matches it and whether the clone is shallow; the check `bundle_records_the_commit_the_runner_checked_out` fails on a mismatch or a tracked change. (H4) 16 GDACS TC events (ids 1001273..1001315) resolved to no country in the Phase 2 CONNECTOR and were dropped, while the PA machine had placed the same events from their position since Sept 2026 — two readers of one feed, one rule. `GdacsConnector._expand_to_country_months` now calls the machine's `_iso3s_near_event` through a lazy import (the machine borrows the connector's helpers, so a module-level import would be circular), stamps `iso3_from_geometry`, and logs the placed and the still-unplaceable events by id. (H5) Two Chile flood extractions failed on `max_tokens`: the extraction model is Haiku-class with no thinking, the answer budget was the rulebook's `extraction.max_output_tokens: 2000`, and `PYTHIA_ANTHROPIC_SPD_MAX_TOKENS` (which the task named) governs the Opus SPD path and not these calls — adaptive thinking has no separate budget in the API; on Opus 5 it shares `max_tokens`, which is why the SPD default is 32,768. The rulebook budget is 8,000, a truncated answer is re-read once at twice the budget (both calls billed and counted), and a document too long even then is recorded `truncated_after_retry_at_<n>_output_tokens` rather than as a failure; `no_extraction_this_run_was_lost_to_output_truncation` fails on an error row naming max_tokens with no retry marker. (H6) The nightly backcast spent exactly its 2,000-call share on 2026-09-01 and every later cell that month was written NO_DATA with `budget_capped` in its provenance — a frozen row, never rewritten, saying "ReliefWeb was silent" about documents fetched and never read. The decision is CHECKPOINT AND RESUME, not a higher cap: in a backcast a budget-capped cell with no existing row gets no row, its trigger row says `extraction_budget_deferred` with the binding limit, the month is recorded `deferred` in `haz_backcast_progress` with `deferred_cells` (the CHECK is widened by a one-time table rebuild in `_widen_backcast_status_check`), `month_is_complete` treats deferred cells as owed rather than lost, and the next run walks exactly the owed cells (`countries_filter` = the list) once the calendar month's allowance resets. The run summary carries `extraction_budget_bound`, `extraction_binding_limit`, `months_budget_deferred`, `cells_deferred_for_budget` and `budget_policy: checkpoint_and_resume`; `haz_backcast.yml` prints the same line as a `::notice` and in the step summary. A live run never defers. `budget_deferred_backcast_cells_are_owed_by_the_resume_ledger` fails when a deferred cell carries a row or its month is not `deferred`. (H7) CrisisWatch: the refresh logged "not newer than existing — skipping write" and 78 rows landed anyway, because the JSON file honoured the guard and the DB writer (`INSERT OR REPLACE` for every entry on every run, called from three workflows) did not; and 85 parsed against 78 stored had no line for the seven. `store_crisiswatch_entries` is now the one guard: `content_hash` on the row, insert / update / unchanged / skipped counts, `fetched_at` moving only with content; `bulk_store_crisiswatch` returns the rows held for the edition and records the accounting to the `crisiswatch_store` run-log stream; `_load_from_json` stamps every parsed entry `stored`, `merged_into_<ISO3>` or `unresolved_iso3` (the scraper stamps `iso3_reason` per entry and `parse_accounting` in the JSON); and the refresh script writes a same-month republication when its content hash differs (ICG revises entries) and skips an identical one. `crisiswatch_entries_parsed_are_all_accounted_for` fails when the reasons do not sum to parsed or the table does not hold what the store claimed. **Rules: see the four new "PA resolution machine" lines, the two "Verification and telemetry" lines and the "Connectors and upstream data" line in `## Invariants`.** Tests: `resolver/tests/test_group_h_small_items.py`, `horizon_scanner/tests/test_crisiswatch_store.py`, the same-edition cases in `tests/test_refresh_crisiswatch.py`, and `test_the_crisiswatch_accounting_check_reads_the_store_stream` in `resolver/tests/test_resolver_debug_bundle.py`.

- **Three distinct ceilings across 310 ledger rows, two wrong-country documents, and a unit column that lied** (fixed, 2026-09-04, from run 33841370196 — Group C of the resolver-faults series). (C1) The figures ledger carried exactly three ceiling values — 5, 20 and 955 — which at the 3x multiplier implies GDACS exposures of 1.67, 6.67 and 318 people for national monthly flood totals; 72 well-sourced extracted figures were rejected against them. The hypothesis that `gdacs:population` carries a unit the parser ignored (`value="1.67" unit="Million"`) could not be verified: gdacs.org is denied from the sandbox, and the public GDACS RSS fixtures show `unit` naming the MEASURE (`Pop74`, `Population in 100km`, `""`) beside a value in people. So the connector now parses the unit (people-units pass, `Million`/`Thousand` scale, anything else is UNKNOWN with a warning) and keeps unit, raw value and element text verbatim on every event and in `haz_raw_gdacs`; `sanity.min_plausible_exposure` (1000) makes any smaller exposure UNKNOWN exactly as a zero already was (`rules.usable_exposure`, applied by the ceiling rule, `reconcile._ceiling` and `candidates.exposure_ceiling_basis`); and `resolve-hazards --reconsider-rejected` replays the ladder for every resolved cell whose provenance carries an `exceeds_gdacs_exposure_ceiling` rejection, from the caches, on every Resolver Update (Phase 2.5) — unfrozen cells rewritten, frozen cells logged to `haz_revisions`. (C2) Documents 4222929 and 4222839, Spanish and English accounts of March-to-May floods affecting 82,000 people, were filed under AFG / FL / 2026-07 because ReliefWeb's `country.iso3` filter matches any country a regional round-up lists; `figures.apply_attribution` now requires the document's `primary_country` to be the cell's and the date the figure is about to fall in the cell's reporting window, the cached document carries the evidence, and the bundle check `no_accepted_figure_lies_outside_its_cell_reporting_window` re-runs both tests over the ledger. (C3) "187 families affected" was written as value 935 under unit `households`, and 15,600 households became 63,959.99999999 people: the ledger now carries `stated_value`/`stated_unit`, `value_persons` (whole persons) and `conversion_factor` as separate columns, and household conversions round. **Rules: see the four new lines under "PA resolution machine" and "Verification and telemetry" in `## Invariants`.** Acceptance after the next Resolver Update: `no_resolution_exceeds_a_ceiling_that_rejected_a_figure_in_the_same_cell` PASSES, `SELECT COUNT(DISTINCT ceiling) FROM figures_ledger` is no longer three single- and triple-digit values, and `diagnostics/haz_reconsider_{flood,cyclone}.json` names the cells rewritten. Tests: `resolver/tests/test_hazard_resolution_ceiling_repair.py`.

- **Four `enso_state` rows still said Neutral with nothing behind them, 919 history rows called themselves fresh, and one index source was alive** (fixed, 2026-09-04, from run 33841370196 — Group A of the resolver-faults series). Four findings. (1) The pre-fix rows at 2026-07-09/12/15 and 2026-08-28 stated `Neutral` beside null `oni`, `nino34_anomaly`, `status`, `observation_date` — they postdated the newest backfilled observation, so any "state as of" query for mid-July or late August returned a phase with no measurement, the exact fault the computed-phase work was meant to end. The `--recompute` CLI could fix one row when an operator remembered; now `repair_unbacked_rows` runs at the end of every `fetch_and_store_enso`, after the workflow's ONI backfill, rebuilds each such row from the newest `oni_table` observation dated at or before it (the 2026-08-28 row reads El Niño / strong / ONI +1.80 / observed 2026-07-01 / 58 days), deletes a row nothing predates, and logs repaired / deleted / untouched. (2) The backfill keyed 919 ONI seasons on `fetch_date = observation_date` and stamped every one `status='fresh'`, `age_days=0`, `raw_context` "Current state: La Niña, weak" for January 1950 — one table carrying two meanings with nothing to tell them apart. `row_kind` (`live` | `historical` | `repaired`) now says which; `classify_row_kinds` migrates pre-column rows by their signature; historical rows carry `status='historical'` and "ENSO state for YYYY-MM"; `load_enso_state_from_db`, `load_last_good_record`, `consumers_of_record` and the bundle's narrative check read live/repaired rows only; and the backfill never overwrites a live row on a shared date. (3) Rank 1 (ERDDAP) answered HTTP 400 and rank 2 (`wksst8110.for`) served observations from 2021-01-27, so only the ONI table worked, `nino34_anomaly` was a verbatim copy of `oni` on every row, and `ONI_JUMP_LIMIT`/`ONI_AGREEMENT_TOLERANCE` had nothing to compare. ERDDAP is now asked for every column with the anomaly found by name pattern (a renamed column is a parse failure naming the columns seen, never a 400 naming nothing); rank 2 moved to `wksst9120.for` (CPC changed base period in early 2021 and froze the old file); every rank is read on every run and recorded in `index_evidence_json`; the weekly reading is stored as absent (`nino34_weekly` NULL) when only the seasonal table answered; and the bundle check `enso_ladder_has_two_live_ranks_in_the_last_30_days` fails when fewer than two ranks answered. (4) `load_enso_state_from_db` refused observations over 30 days old, so the seasonal TC module built its context with no ENSO signal ("65 days old (max 30)") on the day the ENSO module correctly detected a strong El Niño — the ONI is structurally 30-70 days behind. `DB_MAX_OBSERVATION_AGE_DAYS` is 100. **Rules: see the four new lines under "Connectors and upstream data" in `## Invariants`.** Acceptance after the next Resolver Update: `SELECT COUNT(*) FROM enso_state WHERE enso_phase IS NOT NULL AND (oni IS NULL OR nino34_anomaly IS NULL)` = 0. Tests: `horizon_scanner/tests/test_enso_repair.py` (run by horizon-scanner-ci) and `test_the_enso_ladder_must_have_two_live_ranks` in `resolver/tests/test_resolver_debug_bundle.py`.
- **ACLED: seven refresh failures a run, three token requests, web pages read as empty results, and a CAST vintage nine months frozen served as "leads 1 to 3"** (fixed, 2026-09-04, from run 33841370196 — Group B of the resolver-faults series; verified against the live API with a valid token the same day). Three things about the code were CORRECT and are unchanged: the password grant sends `scope=authenticated` (`scope=acled` is rejected with `invalid_scope`), the grant bodies are form-encoded properly, and pagination loses nothing (year=2026: 5,000 + 4,879 + 0 rows, 9,879 distinct `(country, admin1, month, year)` keys — the API orders by month and splits a month across the page boundary, which is not a duplicate). What changed: (B1.1) the stored refresh token returned `invalid_grant` on every job, so every caller logged an ERROR and fell back to the password grant; the password grant now comes FIRST when credentials exist and the refresh grant runs only without them. (B1.2) tokens live 86,400 s and the run requested one per process plus retries; `acled_auth` now keeps a file cache (`ACLED_TOKEN_CACHE_PATH`, fingerprinted to the account, tmp-dir by default) that every process consults. (B1.3) ACLED's gateway answers an unauthenticated API call with a Drupal page titled "Unauthorized" — HTTP 200, even with `Accept: application/json` — so `describe_response` (the one thing that made the 2026-09-03 incident diagnosable) is now behind `parse_json_response`, the single JSON path for `acled_client.fetch_events`, `ACLEDClient._fetch_page`, `acled_cast._fetch_all_records` and `acled_political._fetch_with_retry`; an HTML body raises `AcledHtmlResponse` (status, URL, first 200 characters), `acled_political` counts them and the ingest reports the connector as failed, the HTTP recorder now records `content_type` per call, and the bundle check `acled_html_responses_are_recorded_as_connector_failures` fails a run that reports ok beside a web page. (B2.1) CAST has served the 2025-12-10 vintage since January (year=2027 returns nothing; five target months, January to May 2026, all past by September), and `_transform` computed leads from that issue date — so a reader asking for leads 1 to 3 in September received January to March. `conflict_forecasts.is_stale_vintage`/`vintage_age_days` are stamped on every row at every write, every prompt reader filters `target_month >= this month` (`SERVED_TARGET_FILTER_SQL`; VIEWS at 65 days gets the same treatment), and the bundle check `no_conflict_forecast_served_has_a_target_month_in_the_past` runs the readers' own predicate. Escalation to ACLED (account kwyjad@unicef.org, `/api/cast/read`, newest timestamp 2025-12-10, no 2027 targets, five months not six) is drafted in the PR; the Gmail connector was not authorised from the session. (B2.2) the unfiltered fallback pulled the archive OLDEST first (page 1: 2023-24 rows, newest timestamp 2024-09-12) and would have reported success — deleted; an empty result stays empty. (B2.3) the vintage's target months are logged and reported as found, never inferred from `_MAX_LEAD_MONTHS`. (B2.4) records are deduplicated on the admin1 key before the country SUM, with the drop count and distinct-key count logged beside the raw count. (B2.5) 88 countries × 5 months = 440 and the run wrote 438 country-months: every country-month dropped by the ISO3 map or the lead filter is now named, and every country carrying fewer months than the vintage is listed in `connector.summary['countries_with_month_gaps']`, so the next run's log names the two. (B2.6) `_derive_issue_date` reads epoch timestamps in UTC. **Rules: see the five new "Connectors and upstream data" lines in `## Invariants`.** Tests: `resolver/tests/test_acled_html_response.py`, `test_acled_token_cache.py`, `test_acled_cast_accounting.py`, `test_conflict_forecasts_served.py` (the cross-package files are named in forecaster-ci / horizon-scanner-ci), plus two cases in `test_resolver_debug_bundle.py`.

- **The September Resolver Update died in Phase 1: an HTTP 200 that was not JSON, and a tier-0 connector that went green with nothing** (fixed, 2026-09-04, from run 33796381874). The 3 Sept dispatch failed after three and a half minutes at "Phase 1: ACLED monthly fatalities", so Phases 1 (rest) through 5 never ran and no canonical DB was uploaded. Three separate defects, in the order they bit.
  1. **Both ACLED OAuth grants were answered HTTP 200 with a body `json.loads` refused.** The `status_code != 200` guard passed, `resp.json()` raised, and the run died on `Expecting value: line 1 column 1 (char 0)`. That message names neither the status, the content type, the URL reached after redirects, nor one character of what was served, and FOUR causes produce it: a Cloudflare challenge (ACLED sits behind one and runner addresses are routinely challenged), a moved route serving the marketing site with a cheerful 200, a gateway content-negotiating HTML to a request that never asked for JSON, and a dead credential. Each wants a different repair and the run recorded nothing that could tell them apart. `_log_token_http` would have held the body, but it logs at DEBUG (the job runs at INFO) and passes its fields as `extra=`, which renders in no default formatter, so the body was recorded nowhere at all. Both grants now go through one `_token_request`, and a 200 whose body will not parse raises the SAME described error a non-200 does, via `describe_response`: status, content type, `final_url` when redirects moved it, the redirect chain, a `body_shape=html` marker, and a body snippet redacted against the environment's own secrets (an OAuth refusal quotes the username back, and this string goes to a public log).
  2. **The requests never asked for JSON.** They sent `Content-Type` and nothing else, so a bare `python-requests` User-Agent with no `Accept` header, which is the request shape a WAF or gateway is most free to answer with a web page. This repo has hit the same wall twice already: BoM 403s a generic UA, and crisisgroup.org's Cloudflare blocks CI Playwright. Both grants now send `Accept: application/json` and a real User-Agent (`ACLED_USER_AGENT` overrides it). **This is a candidate fix, not a proven one**: the sandbox proxy denies `acleddata.com`, so it could not be tested against the live endpoint, and only CI holds the credentials.
  3. **The ACLED connector failed to authenticate, wrote zero rows and exited 0.** In the step ABOVE the crash, `acled_client` logged `wrote resolver/staging/acled.csv rows=0 (error: Expecting value...)` and `=== DONE acled_client (rc=0) ===`, so the FATAL Phase 1 connectors step went GREEN having written no tier-0 conflict rows at all. `main()` returns a bool and the `__main__` shim discarded it. This is the worse half of the incident: had the fatalities step found the staged events file, or been non-fatal, the entire monthly ingest would have completed green with no ACLED data in `facts_resolved`. It is also the repo's own invariant, in a connector that predates it: "the source said nothing happened" and "we could not read the source" are different facts. `cli_main()` now exits 1 only when the source could not be READ, with a `::error` annotation naming the reason; a deliberate `RESOLVER_SKIP_ACLED` and a genuinely empty window still exit 0, and `main()` keeps the bool contract `test_connectors_headers.py` pins. **`ifrc_go_client` had the identical hole** (an exception swallowed into an empty CSV and a `return`), one connector along in the same fatal step, and got the same treatment.
  **The cause is still unproven and is settled by `scripts/ci/diagnose_acled_auth.py`**, run via the `diagnostics` dispatch input on `resolver_update.yml`. It probes the route with a GET, runs both grants, repeats the password grant in the pre-fix header shape so content negotiation can be isolated, and searches the candidate alternate routes only after everything else has failed. **Next step: dispatch the workflow with `diagnostics: acled_auth`, read the verdict, and act on it**. Rotate the secret for `credentials_rejected`, change `OAUTH_TOKEN_URL` for `endpoint_moved`, take it to ACLED for `waf_challenge`. Note the refresh token was already expected to be stale (CLAUDE.md has carried "rotate the expired `ACLED_REFRESH_TOKEN`" since 3 Sept), so every ACLED process falls through to the password grant, which is why one endpoint fault took out both paths at once. **Rules: see the three new "Connectors and upstream data" lines in `## Invariants`.** Tests: `resolver/tests/test_acled_auth_non_json_body.py`, `resolver/tests/test_connector_unread_exit_code.py` (14 of their 17 cases fail against the pre-fix code; the 3 that pass pin behaviour that was already correct) and `scripts/ci/tests/test_diagnose_acled_auth.py`.
- **Pre-flight audit of the Resolver Update workflow before the September 2026 dispatch** (fixed, 2026-09-03). The workflow at HEAD had never run: the 28 Aug run executed #872, and everything from #873 to #882 was untested on this path. Read against the 28 Aug `backfill-diagnostics` artifact and the nightly backcast's, the code had these defects, each fixed in the same PR. (1) **`haz_raw_population` was created by every entry point and loaded by none** — no workflow ran `population.py` — so `impact.national_population` returned None everywhere, the population cap never fired, and #879's `population_fallback_share` could never run: with the ceiling multiplier at 3.0 a cell with no positive GDACS exposure had no upper bound at all. Phase 2.4 and the nightly backcast now load it; `LadderRun.no_ceiling` and the acceptance report's "what bounded each resolved value" table make the count visible. (2) **The drought gate read last cycle's rows**: Phase 2.5 drought ran before the HDX Signals store and the NMME ingest, both now moved above it as Phase 2.4. Its HDX entry also admitted `ipc_food_insecurity` and `wfp_market_monitor` as drought evidence — the circularity the rulebook's own comment forbids — and the pick between several rows for one country-month was left to the table's physical order. Restricted to `jrc_agricultural_hotspots`, the pick made deterministic (the warning wins), and the validator now refuses a circular `where`. (3) **One alerting feed could zero every country it never monitored**: an absent country counts as "no warning", `min_available: 1` was satisfied by that, and `RESOLVED_ZERO` followed. `drought.indicators.min_present_readings` (1) requires a reading that NAMED the country before a zero rests on absence; otherwise the cell is INCONCLUSIVE with reason `indicator_no_coverage`, and a month that zeroes over 90% of its cells on absence logs an error. NMME's `lead_months` is now an offset on the issue date (`date_offset_column`), so the gate for month T reads the forecast ABOUT T, not the one issued in T. (4) **Extraction could outrun its step**: rung 2 reads one document at a time (~2.5 s), the 28th has 2,000 calls to spend, and a step-level timeout SIGKILLs the shell — no `::warning`, no summary JSON, remaining months skipped. Steps are 90/90/30 minutes under coreutils `timeout`, the summary is written after every month, `ExtractionBudget.binding_limit` names the limit that bound (last night's log said "cap of 3000" when the 2,000 backcast share was it), and an optional `extraction.max_calls_per_run` caps a live pass.
